# Exawatt Roadmap

This is the canonical roadmap. Keep it current and cohesive. Do not create competing plans.

This file follows the published [Exawatt roadmap convention](../product/reference/roadmap-convention.md) (`exawatt-roadmap: v2`). Any repo can adopt that convention to get the same Exawatt roadmap lens; its migration section is written for the agent doing the adaptation.

Roadmap rule: architect 10 miles ahead, build one mile at a time.

## Execution front (2026-08-03)

The 2026-08-02 demo arc **landed in one day**: ENG-036 G0/G1, ENG-026 N0–N5, ENG-027 W1–W4, ENG-004 V3.0/V3.1, ENG-008 E4→E8, ENG-032 T0, the `0023` rename with the legacy trio retired, and ENG-023 D3a/D3b/D4. The explanatory picture is now on screen. The 2026-08-02 front below is kept for its reasoning; the ordered work is now this:

1. **Codex turn truth** (ENG-016, triaged `f8fade12`) — a Codex tab showed the finished glyph while its agent was still working. This is a regression of D38's latch and D40's protocol, and status truth is the product's core claim, so it outranks every feature below. A green check that lies is worse in a demo than a surface that is missing.
2. **ENG-008 E5** flip Consumption from `preview` to `live` — the local data already exists (E0), and this converts the product's most-asked question ("how are you tracking cost per agent?") from a demo answer into a true one. **LANDED 2026-08-10** contract-first in two parallel halves (typed IPC contract + renderer swap with the manifest flip; the incremental scanner behind the contract in flight) — see the E5 milestone line.
3. **ENG-030 open-source readiness** — gate-driven, not calendar-bound. **Amended 2026-08-11:** the "public this quarter" calendar commitment is dropped; each publication deliverable ships when its own gates pass, with no single launch event (operator review with verified comps). **Shaped 2026-08-10:** the operator decisions are closed by decision `0036`: fresh public `Full-Vibe/exawatt` as the daily product repo; current history retained in private `exawatt-company`; AGPL application, Apache compatibility spec, CLA, private cloud/official distribution, and source builds with no Exawatt service configuration. OS2–OS6 are the publication path. **Amended 2026-08-06:** OS0 and OS1.1–OS1.4 were pulled forward because invited users already ran released builds without accounts.
3a. **BUG-091 then BUG-090, ahead of the rest of ENG-030's publication path**
   (operator, 2026-08-19). Both were found while executing OS2.2 and both are
   enqueued rather than left in the backlog, because each gets worse the later
   it is taken.

   **BUG-091 is done (2026-08-19).** It was a live product defect, not a doc
   defect: the goal-visual request carried the Session label, which the
   operator may have typed, to Exawatt, and nothing downstream needed it. The
   client now sends the opaque `identityKey` the published Apache contract
   always specified, no cached image was invalidated, and the Settings
   disclosure is back to "No text you wrote".

   **BUG-090** taxes every agent, every day: a correct change to a widely
   imported module cannot land while a sibling session is busy, and the
   pre-landing checks report the timeouts as named test failures, which reads
   as "your change broke these". Six attempts on a verified-sound change on
   2026-08-18 is the measured cost. It compounds with fleet size, so it is
   cheapest to fix before the contribution loops of OS6.2 add outside PRs to
   the same queue. **LANDED 2026-08-18:** the floor re-runs the files it names,
   once, alone, and reports a pass-on-retry as a recorded suspected flake
   rather than as your break.

4. **ENG-031 website** — if demoing is a standing posture, the site is the always-on demo, and it is where users and contributors land. Still unshaped; a design pass precedes any building.

**2026-08-03 sequencing amendment:** ENG-003 S2 was pulled forward and landed; ENG-003 S3 then supplied the scalable model-picker foundation and ENG-016 D46's lightweight Launch Configuration runtime landed on top of it. Neither item remains in the unsequenced set.

**2026-08-06 external-user amendment.** A new front opens above the demo arc, because it is time-sensitive in a way the arc is not: **invited external users exist and cannot use the product's hosted half.** An invited user has been running `0.1.8` for two days with no account, which silently disables context labels, conversation summaries, goal visuals, and the ⌘⇧F feedback box — and no instrumentation exists that would ever have surfaced that. Ordered work: (1) ENG-030 OS0.1–OS0.3, so an invited user can find and complete sign-in and recover from a failed one; (2) ENG-030 OS1.1–OS1.4, so the next such gap is visible within a day instead of discovered by audit; (3) ENG-035 A2's GitHub identity, which currently leaves a public profile that cannot be refreshed; (4) ENG-025 F3's lane split, so non-operator feedback lands somewhere that is neither ignored nor auto-promoted to canon. This front does not stop the daily-driver arc; where they contend for the same surface, correctness of the daily driver still wins.

**Front status, 2026-08-07 (CLOSED):** every ordered item landed and is live. (1), (2), (4) shipped to production; (3) landed as incident `0007` — desktop identity linking had never worked in any build — with only the operator's real-build verification (link, then publish) outstanding; OS1.5 completed the arc with the unified Privacy surface, the context-label and recap switches, and main-process analytics coverage. **Shaped 2026-08-10:** ENG-030's publication decisions are no longer blocked on the operator; decision `0036` and `projects/open-source-readiness.md` define OS2–OS6.

**Preview debt is now a clock.** Five `preview` surfaces shipped in one day. Each is a promise, and previews that never flip to `live` decay into vaporware. Every surface swap that lands (E5 first) should reduce the count; if the count is not falling a month from now, that is a signal to stop adding surfaces.

**2026-08-16 connected-Agent pull.** The operator's two existing OpenClaw VPS
installations make ENG-010 the next new capability slice. The product does not
begin with provisioning or “push to cloud”: C0–C3 preserve source topology,
connect and observe existing configured Agents read-only, project one coworker
per configured Agent, and prove quit/relaunch reattachment before write
authority. ENG-033 is now the shaped umbrella over that progression, not a
parallel feature surface. Daily-driver correctness and the active publication
front still win any direct surface or delivery conflict; `next` means the first
unclaimed feature packet, not permission to interrupt a red correctness gate.

Ordered front from 2026-08-02, retained for its reasoning (all but V3.2/V3.3 complete):

1. **ENG-036 G0** the design-system kernel — every surface below cites it, and it is the operator's largest stated unsolved problem. G0 only; the rest of the system continues in parallel
2. **ENG-026 N0/N1** readiness grammar and the surface map
3. **ENG-027 W2** the Demo Workspace live — LANDED 2026-08-02: the Voltaic fixtures run behind the fleet transport boundary, demo panes render the content source, Demo is available in the switcher. The altitude handoff and preview surfaces it gated are unblocked
4. **ENG-008 E4** the expository Consumption surface
5. **ENG-004 V3.3 → V3.0** the spatial stream, feel first (operator, 2026-08-02): V3.3's slices land in order S1 input → S2 continuity → S3 tiled board → S4 chrome, V3.0's cards→board entry pose LANDED first (2026-08-03 reconciliation: it was Voltaic-tuned and integrated the same evening this ordering was written, extending the one D11 transition owner), so S2 absorbs its rig-side execution into the continuous-transition machinery — one integrated arc, never two competing spatial plans. Execution contract: the project doc's "V3.3 execution contract" section; packets in `projects/demo-arc-execution.md`
6. **ENG-026 N3–N5** with **ENG-028 T1** and **ENG-029 C1** supplying their content

The active daily-driver arc (ENG-015, ENG-016, ENG-021, ENG-023) continues in parallel and does not stop; where the two conflict for the same surface, the demo arc yields to correctness of the daily driver, never the reverse. Long-arc items (ENG-005 through ENG-014, ENG-030 through ENG-034, ENG-037) stay planned; ENG-035 is now active-build, and ENG-038 went active-build 2026-08-11 when the operator pulled its first slice forward (Claude plan-window visibility).

The demo the front is aimed at carries three operator-named risks — it looks unpolished up close, the story does not land, and there is nothing to show at scale. Reliability is explicitly NOT among them, so this arc does not spend slack on hardening.

Execution packets for 4–6 parallel agents, with the file-ownership collision map, are in **`projects/demo-arc-execution.md`**. That document holds no scope of its own; every packet points back at the item that owns it.

## Operating Model

`roadmap.md` is the singular engineering roadmap. It should stay readable as the fleet grows by acting as the top-level sequence, status index, and conflict-resolution surface.

Use this file for:

- ordered roadmap items
- current status
- concise scope and exit criteria
- a compact milestone list (id, title, status, date landed, one line)
- links to deeper engineering project docs, architecture docs, and decision records
- explicit notes when priorities, sequencing, or ownership boundaries change

Use linked project docs when a roadmap item needs durable detail beyond this file, such as milestone narratives, acceptance tests, design constraints, implementation notes, or future agent work packets. Linked project docs must not become independent roadmaps; they are execution detail owned by a roadmap item.

Milestone narratives belong in the project doc, not here. Each project doc carries a dated **Roadmap milestone log** section holding the full historical narrative that this file used to inline; the roadmap keeps only the one-line milestone contract and the link. When a milestone lands, write the narrative in the project doc and add or update one line here.

When updating linked project docs, also update the corresponding roadmap item status, milestone pointer, or exit criteria if the canonical plan changed. If a linked project doc stops matching this roadmap, reconcile it immediately by updating, archiving, or marking the stale material.

Roadmap item IDs are stable handles. Preserve IDs when reordering items. Add a new ID for genuinely new roadmap items; do not recycle IDs from retired or archived work.

Later milestones frequently amend or supersede earlier ones. Before acting on any milestone decision, check the [Amendment chain](#amendment-chain) at the end of this file — it lists every supersession that is still load-bearing.

Allowed statuses:

- `planned`: accepted direction, not next for implementation
- `next`: ready to start when active work frees up
- `active-build`: currently being designed or implemented
- `blocked`: accepted direction, but progress needs a named decision, dependency, or external condition
- `done`: shipped or otherwise complete for the current roadmap resolution
- `stale`: superseded or no longer trusted; update, archive, or replace promptly

## Now

### ENG-001 Repo and docs consolidation

Status: done

Scope:

- retire active Godot/Helios direction
- preserve useful retired thinking in `docs/archive/`
- establish canonical product and engineering docs
- define the documentation contract in `AGENTS.md`
- align `/architecture` with current architecture vocabulary

Exit criteria:

- no active docs point to Godot as current architecture
- README links only to canonical docs
- `/architecture` uses canonical Exawatt concepts

### ENG-002 Agent Terminal Workspace (dogfood parity)

Status: active-build — RESHAPED 2026-07-02 from "Electron local OpenClaw pseudo-parity" to the canonical operator workflow (`docs/product/operator-workflow.md`). W0.1–W0.4 landed and parity is real; excellence moved to ENG-015 and adoption blockers to ENG-016. Closes on accumulated use, not a fixed dogfood week.

Scope:

- agent-first terminal hosting in Electron (node-pty in main, xterm.js in the renderer): the product gesture is "launch an agent" — pick a harness, pick or create a git worktree — not generic terminal multiplexing (decision `0005`)
- the terminal regime is a first-class, long-lived UI regime in its own right (operator, 2026-07-03: an AI-native tmux++), developed in parallel with the spatial regime as independent skins over one session/fleet system
- launch always requires a project directory (no silent home-dir default); the last-used directory is remembered and prefilled
- the working directory is the grouping key mapping Sessions to a Project / Context Group: same-project tabs share a color and cluster adjacently
- Initiative-labeled windows containing session tabs — a thin slice of ENG-005 (labels and grouping only, not the full Initiative primitive)
- restart persistence of layout, names, worktrees, and working dirs; running processes do not survive restarts and ENG-018 owns deterministic rehydration, not a detached backend (decision `0012`)
- per-session status normalized into the same FleetState the DOM board and Spatial consume, so real sessions are the live data source
- fast keyboard driving end to end, plus an incremental context layer: naming → auto-summarized micro-context subtitles → context augmentation
- hybrid rendering rule stands: terminal text and controls remain DOM (decision `0003`). AMENDED 2026-07-06 (operator): the spatial surface is no longer this workspace's overview/switcher — the regimes deliberately diverge (ENG-015, ENG-004), and terminal game-feel investment happens under ENG-015
- Live Mode and Demo Mode using the same UI architecture

Exit criteria:

- accumulated operator use establishes Exawatt as the normal surface for real daily coding-agent work (1–6 agents across 2+ Projects); ENG-016 records fallbacks while implementation continues
- launching a new Claude Code or Codex agent in a fresh worktree is one gesture
- live sessions appear in `/fleet` and `/fleet/spatial` with truthful status
- Demo Mode demonstrates the same flows without live agents

Milestones:

- W0.1 PTY foundation (landed): node-pty + xterm.js in the Electron shell; real shell and interactive Claude Code sessions in a chosen cwd with full TUI fidelity.
- W0.2 Workspace parity (landed): directory-required launch, directory-keyed project clustering with per-project colors, restart persistence with auto-revive, fast keyboard switching.
- W0.3 Fleet truth (landed): live PTY sessions normalize into FleetState via LocalSessionsTransport; board and map show real sessions with truthful status; desktop = local truth, web keeps Demo/OC.
- W0.4 Context layer (landed): persisted double-click rename propagated to the PTY, plus main-process auto-summarized micro-context subtitles; the summarizer module is the context-augmentation seam.
- W0.5 Spatial cockpit (rescoped 2026-07-06, operator): retired as an ENG-002 milestone — the regimes diverge instead of converging; driving Sessions from the map returns as ENG-004 work.

Project doc:

- `docs/engineering/projects/agent-terminal-workspace.md`

### ENG-015 Stellar small-fleet command (1–10 agents)

Status: active-build — created 2026-07-06 (operator): before any long-arc work, the UI for operating one to ten agents must be stellar. The investment target is the TERMINAL regime as the approachable daily driver.

Divergence direction (operator, 2026-07-06; navigation amended 2026-07-10): the two UI regimes keep distinct jobs and visual identities — the terminal workspace stays an AI-native tmux++ sized for 1–10 agents while AgentField evolves toward an RTS-style command map at fleet scale. They form one command-altitude continuum for NAVIGATION only (Agent near, Team middle, Fleet far — names per decision `0023`); the terminal remains DOM/xterm and the field remains R3F.

Scope:

- attention: harness-aware "this agent needs you" detection (bell/notify sequences + turn-boundary quiescence), badges, an attention queue with a jump key, macOS dock signals — mirrored into FleetState so every surface agrees
- command-altitude navigation: a persistent Agent → Team → Fleet control in the Electron shell, each altitude one click or one absolute shortcut away (`⌃⌘1`/`⌃⌘2`/`⌃⌘3` since ENG-016 D19)
- command velocity: keyboard-first everything — global fuzzy session switcher, split panes, complete no-mouse coverage of daily actions, shortcut discoverability
- exposé & motion: a zoomable live-tile overview of all sessions, animated switching, depth/motion in the chrome (reduced-motion respected)
- context paging: research-backed support for swapping human working memory between dramatically different contexts — visual identity anchors, re-entry recaps, "while you were away" digests, stable spatial addresses, boundary-batched interruptions
- generic for any user (no operator-bespoke tuning); personal taste routes to future settings

Exit criteria:

- running 5–10 parallel agents daily, no stalled or asking agent goes unnoticed for more than a few seconds
- every daily action is keyboard-reachable; reaching any session takes ≲2 keystrokes via the switcher
- the overview → focus → overview loop feels game-quality (motion, depth, zero jank)
- after switching initiatives, "where was I here?" answers itself in ~2 seconds without reading scrollback
- the operator judges the daily-driver experience stellar (subjective bar is the real bar)

Milestones:

- S1 Attention system (landed 2026-07-06): needs-you detection in the PTY layer, pulsing tab badges, oldest-first ⌘J queue, dock badge/bounce, attention mapped into FleetState as `blocked` + blockerInfo.
- S1.1 Reported turn truth (landed 2026-07-27): a source that declares its own turn boundary outranks byte quiescence in both directions, so the strip stops trailing the Agent. Measured against a real Claude Session, inference lagged the reported `Stop` by 6-7s on every turn — long enough to read "working" for an Agent that had provably finished. Inference stays as a backstop for sources that report nothing and for turns that end without a report. Rides ENG-023's harness event channel; adds no new light and no new IPC.
- S2 Command velocity (landed 2026-07-09): ⌘K fuzzy session switcher, palette launch commands, ⌘D split panes, ⌘E inline rename, ⌘/ cheat sheet, no-mouse coverage of every daily verb.
- S3 Exposé, motion & discoverability (landed 2026-07-10): ⌘O exposé overview with rich live tiles, persistent key-hint bar, workspace verbs in ⌘K, forced dark theme, terminal typography settings. Pane-content switch animation deliberately skipped — motion lives in the chrome. Its raw-scrollback card body is superseded by ENG-021 E1.1.
- S3.1 Command-altitude continuum (landed 2026-07-10; absolute-key refinement 2026-07-12): the title bar persistently exposes Terminal → Sessions → Spatial; `/workspace?view=sessions` deep-links the middle altitude without restarting PTYs.
- S4 Context paging (active-build): recap delivery is ambient under ENG-016 D18; S4.1 goal visual anchors landed 2026-08-03 for direct live dogfood — deterministic natural-environment backdrops in Team tiles, cached by accepted goal and regenerated only for an ENG-021 semantic pivot or operator correction, with deterministic fallback and a server-only `fal-ai/fast-sdxl` prototype boundary. Live rejection of v1's washed-out pseudo-interface imagery promoted v2: cache invalidation, private goal-to-scene derivation (FAL never receives goal text), and theme-aware localized scrims. One app-global, device-local **Agent tile backgrounds** control defaults on across Electron and hosted web; off suppresses rendering everywhere and future Electron requests. The operator selected full-card geometry, rejected scenes as a category, and chose Graphic Form as the leading language on 2026-08-04; `/hud-gallery/goal-visuals` now holds Graphic Form against six metaphor-led alternatives across three fixed goals. The same global preference is exposed as **Backgrounds** in Team's top chrome for rapid comparison; the live Team prompt remains unchanged pending a final pick. Ship the chosen language, then retire the study. Independent demand evidence 2026-08-13 (a partner conversation): a designer running Claude Code, Codex, and Cursor side by side described this exact failure unprompted — "there's a lot going on just within Cloud, and then across Codex or Cursor, and I forget about what I was working on easily" — and the operator graded the shipped ambient recap himself against the bar in the same demo: the "Since you left…" line "is okay, but I'm trying to build a little bit more, like, kind of, hey, remember what we were working on, and here's how you can jump back in." The job is framed as lowering a hurdle, so re-entry owes the operator a way back IN, not only a report of what changed. No scope shaped from this; it sharpens the existing exit criterion that "where was I here?" answers itself in ~2 seconds.
- S5 Durable Projects (landed 2026-07-10; interaction model amended 2026-07-12 by ENG-016 D14 and decision `0013`): rename, Supabase registry + reclaim migration, resolution bridge, missing-dir locate, synced identity/color; the registry is shared truth and local workspace state is the offline fallback.

- S6.1 Team grid keyboard ownership (landed 2026-08-07; FIX-002 + FIX-006, the two halves that needed no design pass): arrows move by measured tile geometry instead of ±1 through a flat list, and the grid yields every key to a focused control that owns text or its own activation. Rows come from vertical overlap rather than a column count, so ragged rows, full-width empty-Project rows, taller cards, and Project boundaries need no special case; a row edge falls through to reading order so nothing is a dead end. Both halves are pinned by tests that fail without them, and the jsdom/no-layout path keeps reading order so the behaviour stays defined where there is nothing to measure. FIX-006's original field no longer exists (D49 deleted it), so its row is recorded as PREVENTED, not fixed — the rule is what shipped.
- S6.1.1 Team grid selection ownership (landed 2026-08-16; FIX-002 closed a second time): the reopened report blamed the row-edge fallback and the geometry was right — 160,000 randomised layouts through the pure model and a seven-viewport sweep of the live rig both came back clean. Every arrow key ends in `scrollIntoView`; crossing a Project boundary scrolls because the next Project's row is below the fold; Chromium re-dispatches a mouse event at the cursor's last known position whenever content moves under a stationary pointer — so the tile that slid under the resting cursor took back the selection the key had just set, one column to the left. S6.1's 400ms arm window guarded the mount only, and this fires on every scroll. The pointer now claims the selection only when it has MOVED (`teamPointerMoved`, exact rather than timed, subsuming the mount case it replaces). The structural half: the movement policy that was wrong lived in the overlay, where only a live grid can reach it, so `teamGridNeighbor` took over the whole contract — measures are `TeamGridRect | null`, an unmeasured tile is SKIPPED rather than becoming a phantom rect at the viewport origin competing for "nearest row", an unmeasured origin falls back to reading order instead of inventing a position, and the no-layout reading-order path moved out of the component. `nextSelection` measures and delegates. 18 hand-written-rect cases pin the model; `eval:workspace:team` gained a fifth gate that rests the pointer on a tile and drives ArrowDown across the boundary, reading the expected target off the rendered rects — red on the pre-fix tree, green after. Boyscout, found because the glide gate would not stay green while landing: S6.3's FLIP ref-callback cache EVICTED ITSELF on detach, so one detach (Strict Mode double-invokes refs on every dev mount) started an endless new-closure/detach/evict cycle, `rects` was empty at every layout effect, and the re-sort snapped in silence. The cache now survives detach (rect and timer cleanup stay), pinned by `use-flip-tiles.test.tsx`. The glide gate had a blind spot wide enough for that to pass through — one computed-style sample 80ms after the click — and now records the slot's own `transform` transition EVENTS armed before the click, which survive a starved renderer where per-frame sampling cannot.
- S6.1.2 Team ring and gate truth (landed 2026-08-16): `eval:workspace:team` is GREEN on `origin/master` and needs no quarantine — seven consecutive runs at load 14 through 200 (the last three under deliberate synthetic load), 10 tiles gliding every time. BUG-026 waived it reporting the glide gate red "on unmodified origin/master"; its attempt ref's parent is `9506c65` at 17:17, and S6.1.1 landed at 17:35, so that tree still had the self-evicting FLIP ref-cache and the old 80ms sampling gate. The observation and the diagnosis were both right; only the baseline was. `agent:land` rebases at the END, so a long session tests a checkout that ages the whole time it runs — **"red on master" is a claim about a TREE, and belongs in a worktree cut from `origin/master` when the claim is made.** The same aging-baseline trap then bit this very change (a stale `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` from its own base), and its third and most dangerous member is `git stash`: the stash is ONE stack shared by every worktree, so `pop` takes whoever pushed last and can land another session's work in your tree, while `drop`/`clear` destroy it — an agent did pop `agent/oss-wp5a-ci`'s stash into its own tree the same day. `AGENTS.md` now forbids stash beside `git add -A`, both being commands whose target is "whatever happens to be there" rather than a name you chose; the substitutes are a throwaway `--detach origin/master` worktree and a commit on your own branch. Also closes BUG-021: `cycleTab` fed the ring the durable manual arrangement the STRIP displays while Team painted S6.3's sort, so one press moved one place in the strip and landed wherever that Session sat in the grid. Display order is now an explicit parameter of the ring, `teamViewProjects` is the one producer both the grid and the ring read, and the trace the row asked for is `tab-ring-altitudes.test.ts`. The roadmap's "one deterministic result across altitudes" is half falsified: the strip and the grid legitimately display different orders (S6.3 shipped that deliberately), so what is shared is the OWNER, not the result. Boyscout: BUG-013's status corrected to `done` against the code — `PausedAgentRecord` renders in production, `RetainedTerminalPane` is gone, `eval:workspace:paused` is registered.
- S6.3 Team ordering shipped (landed 2026-08-07; FIX-008 closed same-day from the S6.2 bench review): the operator rejected needs-you-first outright ("active first without needs you first shouldn't exist") and named the two sorts himself mid-build ("Started vs. Activity"): a compact Started · Activity control in the Team chrome. **Started** is the stored default, at his word ("keep it like Google Chrome": start time, oldest first, a new Agent appends, nothing on screen changes address). **Activity** leads each Project with its most recent activity at the fidelity the surface has — working now first, then Agents by newest attention signal (a bell IS activity aimed at the operator), the rest in Started order; per-tab activity timestamps would refine it without renaming. Re-sorting is FULLY LIVE by his choice: a tile glides to its new slot the moment its band changes, via a FLIP wrapper that owns position only (D45's position-tween vocabulary, `cubic-bezier(0.25,1,0.5,1)`), so it can never fight the tile's own entrance/selection transforms; reduced motion snaps. The preference is device-local web storage, deliberately not the Electron settings channel (one surface's view order, promoted only if a second surface needs it). Ordering is a view — the ribbon's durable D20/D45 manual arrangement is never written. The Team surface finally owns a browser gate: `eval:workspace:team` runs the rig at `/hud-gallery/team-order` through the PRODUCTION toggle and asserts created default, active-first bands, a mid-flight FLIP transform (a silent snap fails), and reload persistence; `expose-overlay`/`team-order*`/`use-flip-tiles` join `SURFACE_GATES`. Boyscout: the overlay's optional props defaulted to fresh `{}` per render, and anything memoized on them re-derived every render — surfaced as a focus boomerang the moment the view order joined the chain; stable frozen empties now.
- S6.3.1 Team ordering review pass (landed 2026-08-07): three defects found by reading the shipped change, all introduced by it. The live sort made `items` rebuild whenever activity identity changed — which `setActivity` does exactly when an Agent starts or stops working — and the follow-active-tab effect keyed on `items`, so a busy fleet dragged the keyboard back to the active tile while the operator was scanning; the effect now mirrors a change of ACTIVE TAB rather than any new array, reproduced first as a failing test. A second toggle inside the 320ms glide inherited the first one's cleanup and snapped it; glide timers are now per tile, cancelled by the next glide and on unmount. The stored sort was read in an effect, so every open painted Started first and animated into place; it is now an external store read through `useSyncExternalStore`, so the first client render already carries it, gated by a new settle check (stored sort applied on first paint, zero tiles transformed). A `settling` guard added for that last case was DELETED once sabotage showed it never fired. Swept alongside: cached per-tile ref callbacks, and no measurement pass under reduced motion.
- S6.2 Team ordering bench (landed 2026-08-07; superseded same-day by the S6.3 pick — the page survives as S6.3's eval rig): `/hud-gallery/team-order` renders the REAL Team altitude over a ten-agent fixture fleet with a candidate order strip — As arranged / Active first / Needs you first — re-feeding the same Projects in each mode's view order. The engine (`team-order.ts`) is pure, tested, and stable: ordering happens within each Project only, manual order survives inside every band, and nothing is ever written back to the durable arrangement D20/D45 own. The mode vocabulary, whether it ships as a view mode or a transient filter, and where the strip lives in production chrome remain the operator's pick, made from this bench. Alongside it, the roadmap-lens projection both altitudes feed `useProjectRoadmap` is extracted to one owner (`roadmap-lens-input.ts`, riding D51's `paintsAttention`) — the two inline copies had already drifted syntactically, the same two-consumers-one-unwritten-contract shape as BUG-009.
- S6 Team altitude pass (planned, deliberately unshaped pending a design pass; operator, 2026-08-07: "we should pair all the Team-view UI work / bugs / enhancements together"): every open Team-surface row travels as ONE change instead of four scattered small-fixes — FIX-002 (arrows move through a flat list while the layout promises a grid), FIX-006 (the tile's agent-title field takes focus but not typing), FIX-008 (no way to float the Agents you were working on to the front), and S4.1's unfinished goal-visual language (ship the operator's Graphic Form pick, evaluate FIX-005's Nano Banana, then retire the study). Two of those are already known to be ONE defect: `expose-overlay.tsx`'s `onKeyDown` owns both, moving selection ±1 through a flat `items` array and guarding only `[data-roadmap-rail]`, so inside a text field plain `j`/`k` are preventDefaulted into tile movement, `Enter` navigates away instead of committing, and `Escape` closes the overlay instead of cancelling the edit. The pass therefore has a real architectural subject rather than a batch: the Team grid needs one keyboard owner that knows its geometry and yields to text entry, and FIX-008 asks that same grid to reorder — which is the question of whether Team ordering is a view mode, a transient filter, or a sort, and whether it can move Agents at all without contradicting the durable manual arrangement D20/D45 established one altitude down. Do not shape that here; the design pass decides it with the operator, on the bench, from screenshots. RE-HOMES FIX-006 from ENG-016: it is a Team surface sharing an owner with FIX-002, not a daily-driver chrome defect.

Sequencing: precedes ENG-003 and all long-arc items (operator, 2026-07-06: excellence at 1–10 before the long arc).

Project doc:

- `docs/engineering/projects/stellar-small-fleet.md`

### ENG-016 Daily-driver adoption

Status: active-build — D0–D16, D18–D38, D40, and D41 landed through 2026-07-27; signed `v0.1.7` (2026-07-22) put every 2026-07-21 dogfood round into real use. D17 awaits one literal Little Snitch allow/deny observation on an identity-stable build. D39's fifth review-hardening slice landed 2026-07-24; pane ownership and recovery clarity/identity remain. D42 (2026-08-02 ribbon dogfood round: information loss + motion) landed the same day with its reproduction bench and eval gates; operator confirmation of the condensed-chip feel remains dogfood evidence. D43 surfaced the move-tab chords 2026-08-02; D44 landed and was review-hardened through Demo parity, directional availability, and cross-process contract joins on 2026-08-03; D47 restored Project-scoped relaunch through one split recovery control on 2026-08-03; D48 fixed ⌘K nav-row ranking on 2026-08-04; D49 landed the New Agent launcher on 2026-08-04; D50 closed the navigation-spine pass (pinned Project header, back-stack correctness, cross-group ⌘K ranking, chord-searchable ⌘/) on 2026-08-07; D53 gave relaunch recovery its chords and ⌘K verbs on 2026-08-13; D57 closed the discoverability contract over every command verb and landed FIX-012/FIX-014 on 2026-08-16, and D59 finished it the same day by carrying the last two pointer-only verbs onto real command surfaces; D54 opened 2026-08-13 as a deliberately unshaped design pass over the New Agent FLOW, which the operator named as the first target for external design help. D55 was planned 2026-08-16 as an evidence-led interaction-performance architecture: keep Electron and the DOM/xterm/R3F split, build an optional Electron gesture/trace rig, and optimize only the largest proven owner with parity reviews at every stage. Signed `v0.1.8` shipped 2026-08-05 (dispatched run 30963054166, 6.1 min, feed advanced to 0.1.8), batching D29–D49 plus ENG-032 appearance, ENG-035 Leaderboard, ENG-004 Fleet board, and ENG-022 queued delivery into the daily driver — everything landed since 2026-07-22 is now in real use. Signed `v0.1.9` shipped 2026-08-07 (dispatched run 31165144383, feed advanced to 0.1.9), batching D49, the ENG-004/ENG-023 Fleet board arc, and D50. Interaction policies here are explicit hypotheses, not fixed doctrine.

Scope:

- self-contained installed app: `/Applications/Exawatt.app` packages its own renderer and opens `/workspace`; it never gives the hosted website the privileged local PTY bridge (decision `0008`)
- delivery: signed/notarized CI builds publish to the public Supabase update channel and `electron-updater` applies them after an explicit restart that reports live-session impact; the clean-`master` local command is a development escape hatch with a locked, recoverable atomic transaction and no LaunchAgent
- stable dogfood identity: the clean-`master` installer produces an app whose verifiable macOS code identity is stable across source refreshes, so identity-based policy tools keep their Exawatt rules; Exawatt never imports, mutates, or broadens a user's firewall rules
- exact relaunch: every tab persists one exact Claude/Codex session ID and resumes only that ID; `--continue`, `--last`, cwd/recency guesses, and silent auto-revive are forbidden, and restored tabs explain their lifecycle state
- terminal fundamentals: ⌘F scrollback search, reliable selection/copy, ⌘-click on paths and URLs, image paste into harness sessions, deep scrollback with solid rendering performance
- chrome and navigation polish to a Slack / Superhuman / Linear bar: untruncated cwd, legible context summaries, legacy demo surfaces out of primary navigation, an obvious altitude rail, and an Escape-backs-out model with an explicit terminal-focus vs chrome-focus boundary
- navigation spine and project-first switching (IA/navigation audit, operator-accepted 2026-07-11): one typed navigation manifest drives palette, go-chords, header links, and footer suppression; ⌘K always shows Projects; chrome-scoped ⌘[ / ⌘] history; macOS Go/Session menus
- configurable native macOS notifications, default off, after the adoption blockers unless user evidence pulls them forward
- continuous dogfood evidence while agents keep implementing; Terminal.app fallbacks create work, not a calendar reset
- Project-first startup: a curated chooser opens Projects without spawning; a compact optional-task composer carries a visible Agent Source, a per-Project/harness Ask first / Auto-review / YOLO policy, and visible model + reasoning effort, without mutating harness config

Exit criteria:

- Exawatt launches from Spotlight and is bindable in the app switcher; agent closeout keeps the installed build at clean `master` without an operator pnpm/copy step
- two distinct clean-`master` dogfood builds retain the same signed Exawatt identity and keep working under existing Little Snitch allow/deny rules with no re-approval cycle
- the app works offline for local workspace/PTY use and reports its build SHA
- relaunching four same-Project tabs resumes the exact four saved harness IDs and cannot attach an unrelated newer conversation
- scrollback search, copy/⌘-click, and image paste work in daily use
- legacy demo surfaces are out of primary navigation; cwd and context summaries are fully readable
- accumulated operator use establishes Exawatt as the normal daily coding-agent surface, without a fixed consecutive-week gate (closes ENG-002)

Milestones:

- D0 Baseline gates (landed 2026-07-10): lint scope fixed; packaged-app PTY smoke added.
- D1 Self-contained Mac app (landed 2026-07-10): packaged loopback renderer, hardened privilege boundary, current-architecture native deps, offline workspace launch.
- D2 Agent-closeout install (landed 2026-07-10): locked/staged/rollback-safe `/Applications` replacement, build SHA, quiet restart notice; no LaunchAgent, no forced restart.
- D3 Exact session identity (landed 2026-07-10): Claude assigned UUIDs, explicit Codex picker fallback, persistence v3, restored-ended UI, exact resume without guessing.
- D4 Terminal fundamentals (landed 2026-07-10): deep-history search, deterministic copy/paste/select-all, validated URL/file opening, private temporary image paste, 50k-line history with WebGL fallback.
- D5 Chrome and keyboard polish (landed 2026-07-10): disclosed middle-truncated cwd, readable two-line summaries, legacy views out of primary navigation, packaged-tested F6/Escape focus boundary.
- D6 Notifications (landed 2026-07-10): persisted default-off native transport reusing attention truth, quiet while focused, click-through to the exact session.
- D7 Product-grade updates (landed 2026-07-11): packaged `electron-updater`, staged rollout metadata, fail-closed signed/notarized CI, private GitHub Releases plus anonymous Supabase Storage delivery (decision `0009`).
- D8 Navigation spine and project-first switching (landed 2026-07-11): navigation manifest as the single surface source, rail on all Electron surfaces, project-first ⌘K, chrome-scoped ⌘[/⌘], macOS Go/Session menus.
- D9 Keyboard authority and palette recents (landed 2026-07-11): workspace verbs join the rebindable shortcut registry, ⌘K frecency recents and contextual verbs, searchable ⌘/ overlay, per-surface window titles.
- D10 Menu truthfulness and interaction-race fixes (landed 2026-07-11): accelerators follow effective bindings after rebind, valid rename-editor HTML, URL-derived exposé toggle state. Operator-rejected and recorded: peek-before-navigate.
- D11 Command-continuum hardening (landed 2026-07-11): no-drag title-bar click islands, nonmodal Mission Control-style Sessions, manifest/registry-driven labels and ARIA, one reduced-motion-safe transition owner for Terminal ↔ Spatial.
- D12 Absolute command-altitude shortcuts (landed 2026-07-12): rebindable idempotent `⌘1`/`⌘2`/`⌘3` altitude destinations; Project ordinals moved to `⌘⌥1`–`⌘⌥9`. AMENDED by D18, then by D19 — see the Amendment chain.
- D13 Shortcut binding policy (landed 2026-07-12): altitude destinations classified as universal commands with reserved fixed Project/tab families and explained invalid bindings. AMENDED by D18 and D19 — see the Amendment chain.
- D14 Project opener and Agent-first start (landed 2026-07-12; permission UX hardening 2026-07-19): searchable Project chooser, zero-Session Projects that persist, compact composer with visible source and per-pair Ask first / Auto-review / YOLO policy (decisions `0013`, `0015`). AMENDED by D37.
- D15 Startup acceleration (landed 2026-07-12): self-contained launch frame before renderer extraction; warm blank wait fell 3.07s → 0.39s (87%) with cold usable time at parity.
- D16 Session/Spatial parity (landed 2026-07-12): fixed tab-ring shortcuts keep Sessions selection synchronized; stopped Sessions stay Session-backed Agents in Spatial with honest lifecycle state.
- D17 Stable dogfood signing identity (active-build; implementation and the automated two-SHA identity gate landed 2026-07-19, immutable/serialized/atomic delivery hardening 2026-07-20): stable verifiable Exawatt code identity across source refreshes. Literal Little Snitch allow/deny observation is the one open acceptance item.
- D18 Dogfood feedback round 2026-07-20 (landed 2026-07-20): eleven findings owned as one pass — offline authority, the ⌘1–9 tab / ⌘⇧1–3 altitude remap (amends D12/D13), activity-truth status glyphs, default-off dock badge, goal-oriented subtitles, ambient inline recap (retires the S4 popup), summonable composer, rehydration idempotency eval, TCC usage descriptions.
- D19 Shortcut doctrine follow-ups (landed 2026-07-20): `⌘⇧3`–`⌘⇧6` are macOS screenshot hot keys the system consumes before apps, so altitude destinations move to `⌃⌘1`/`⌃⌘2`/`⌃⌘3` (amends D18); open zero-Session Projects become real tab-ring stops. AMENDED 2026-07-21 (operator): Settings consults the machine's effective macOS hotkey table instead of a hardcoded list.
- D20 Keyboard doctrine + arrangeable strip (landed 2026-07-20): ⌘T summons the Agent composer and shell moves to ⌘⇧T; tabs and Projects become reorderable by drag and by fixed keyboard family. AMENDED 2026-07-23 by D39 — see the Amendment chain.
- D21 Dogfood feedback round 2026-07-21 (landed 2026-07-21): ordinal keycaps reveal only while their modifiers are held, a complete ⌘T keyboard path, and durable per-Session goals that survive relaunch. Its amber attention-count pill is later deleted by D27.
- D22 Turn-state legibility (landed 2026-07-21; amends D18's attention-legibility slice): working / turn-finished / never-started read distinctly, with startedness as main truth on the attention monitor. Its glyph-only fresh-tab treatment is superseded by ENG-021 E1.1.
- D23 Close as a lifecycle, not a destructor (landed 2026-07-21): ⌘W parks a live tab and a second ⌘W archives it to a 14-day reopenable Recently-closed ledger. AMENDED same day by D24 — see the Amendment chain; the ledger and reopen verb survive, the two-step grammar does not.
- D24 Chrome-model close + instant new tab (landed 2026-07-21): ⌘W closes exactly like Chrome with a native confirm for started live agents; ⌘T pops a real draft tab instantly; keycap hints overlay without layout shift; task-box image paste; resize-redraw spinner fix. Amends D23; later amended by D27 (in-app confirm) and D28 (draft durability).
- D25 Non-interrupting development and evaluation launches (landed 2026-07-21): `EXAWATT_TEST=1` uses a hidden accessory-activation window so automated Electron runs never steal the operator's keyboard, Dock, or foreground.
- D26 Split-pane ownership (landed 2026-07-21; operator dogfood on the S2 split): the ⌘D pin follows the tab rather than the PTY, empty Projects and draft tabs keep the split up, and split layout math is extracted pure and unit-tested.
- D27 Dogfood round 2026-07-21 evening (landed 2026-07-21): ten findings — app-location back stack, in-app close confirm with macOS button semantics (amends D24), right-click menus, ⌘9 last tab, optimistic close, rail focus feedback, Settings esc, branded source dropdown, attention-count pill removed (amends D21).
- D27.1 Visible app-history controls (landed 2026-08-03): subtle Back and Forward buttons in the Electron title bar expose the same observable app-location stack as ⌘[/⌘], including truthful disabled state; no browser-history fork or second navigation owner.
- D28 Paste, draft durability, summarizer resilience (landed 2026-07-21): terminal ⌘V double-paste killed at the root (handled shortcuts consume their key event; every DOM paste funnels through one image-aware write), typed drafts become durable tab state (amends D24), and the summarizer trades permanent self-disable for exponential backoff with JSONL diagnostics.
- D29 ⌘K switcher turn-state parity (landed 2026-07-22): `pty:list` carries resize-aware `working` truth and renderer adoption stops racing newer quiet events; the old 15-second output heuristic is gone.
- D30 Status iconography and identity de-lamping (landed 2026-07-22): three dot iterations failed Carbon's status-indicator rule, so a fixed-slot learnable icon set replaces hue-only signalling and the Project diamond becomes an identity bar. AMENDED by D33.
- D31 Recent-conversation migration (landed 2026-07-22; hardening same day): the ⌘T page adds a Project-scoped browser over a source-neutral main-process catalog reconciling Exawatt, Claude Code, and Codex history through replaceable adapters (decision `0017`).
- D32 One button system, system-accent default (landed 2026-07-22): the app-wide default action color becomes the operator's macOS highlight color; Project color stays identity-only.
- D33 Calm attention and turn-state/subtitle truth (landed 2026-07-22): amends D30's pulsing bell to a static amber dot-in-circle, fixes acknowledge/resize/BEL ordering so stale working state cannot re-raise, and enforces the six-word subtitle contract.
- D34 Conversation-label/tab-title ownership (landed 2026-07-22): browser labels and tab names become separate concepts — only an explicit operator rename earns a visible primary title; persistence v6 records title ownership and repairs the bounded D31 leak.
- D35 Model/effort-visible Agent launch (landed 2026-07-22): compact Model and Effort selectors resolve each harness's catalog and defaults and cross the typed launch boundary without rewriting harness configuration.
- D36 Deterministic relaunch ownership and recovery hierarchy (landed 2026-07-22): provider identity belongs durably to Electron main and is indexed atomically by durable Session ID; recovery collapses to Resume N Agents plus Resume This Agent. AMENDED by D47 — see the Amendment chain.
- D37 Graceful empty-Project close (landed 2026-07-22; amends D14): closing the last Agent lingers three seconds, retracts, and closes without losing durable registry identity; the Project menu gains Close project.
- D38 Stable Agent turn boundaries (landed 2026-07-22): main latches a finished turn at the quiet or BEL boundary so later provider repaints cannot flip finished back to working (decision `0018`); only the guaranteed-human channel opens the next turn.
- D39 Interaction-contract rough-edge closure (active-build 2026-07-22; fifth review-hardening slice landed 2026-07-24): ⌘W closes an active empty Project; `⌘⇧T` becomes browser-style Session recovery and shell launch moves to `⌘⌥T` (amends D20); one semantic attention/availability/recovery contract; intent-safe complete drafts; keyboard-complete target-aware menus; continuous Project exit motion (decisions `0013`, `0020`). Remaining: pane ownership, honest recovery copy, recovery identity.
- D40 Status-light protocol (landed 2026-07-23): the Codex Micro five-signal model (off, active, result, needs-you, fault) becomes one source-agnostic projection across tabs, Sessions, switcher, and Spatial; only Active moves, and D30/D33 redundancy rules hold.
- D41 Elastic Project / Initiative ribbon (landed 2026-07-27): replace indivisible wrapping groups with a two-row target-bounds layout, selected/manual disclosure, overview overflow, parent/delegated Project signals, pointer-stable close motion, and a dormant empty-Project tail (decision `0022`). AMENDED by D42 where its disclosure/motion contract conflicts.
- D42 Ribbon truth density and constant-height chrome (landed 2026-08-02; operator dogfood round, seven findings): condensed always-rendered inactive-Project tabs (per-Agent state, count, and ordinal-hint anchors survive collapse), selection-invariant strip height with snap-only data changes (zero terminal resizes per switch, was 18), active-Project overflow precedence with parent-dependent admission, pointer-based reorder replacing HTML5 drag, motion diet on selection. Instrumented bench at `/hud-gallery/project-ribbon/bench` and the five-gate `eval:workspace:ribbon:bench`; full contract and landed log in the project doc. Amends decision `0022` and narrows ENG-021 E1.1 for inactive tabs.
- D43 Move-tab verbs surfaced (landed 2026-08-02; operator request): the D20 `⌘⌥[`/`⌘⌥]` arrangement chords gain their discoverable faces — **Move tab left / right** ⌘K rows, `⌘/` fixed-family entries (with the Project variant), and native Session-menu items with static display accelerators — all through one `move-tab` availability (decision `0020` parity). The chrome eval gains a palette-and-chord reorder gate and a deterministic Settings-escape wait (the prior press could race the esc listener's mount).
- D44 Command discoverability contract (landed + review-hardened 2026-08-03; operator, 2026-08-02 — "this should never happen"): one typed trigger manifest derives key dispatch and `⌘/`, requires non-empty or written palette/menu decisions, joins the real native menu, and runs through the same fixed-command layer in Demo and Live; directional reorder availability and status announcements close silent edge no-ops. Full evidence and retained execution spec in the project doc.
- D45 Single-row ribbon: fold, then scroll (landed 2026-08-03; operator dogfood on the installed build): retire the two-row budget, the wrap packer, and `+N` — one row whose height is constant by construction. Nothing is evicted: a Project folds into a counted container before anything disappears, the active Project's tabs shrink Chrome-style before anything scrolls, and horizontal scroll with edge fades is the last resort. Three presentations only (open / mini / folded); `ribbonExpanded` and its `◇` are deleted. Width snaps and position tweens. Supersedes decision `0022`'s two-row budget.

- D46 The Launch Configuration ribbon (landed 2026-08-03; amended decision `0028`): a lightweight task + ribbon + Start surface replaces the five-selector form while preserving `⌘T` → type → Enter, whole-configuration `⌥↑/↓` cycling, and direct `⌘⌥T` Shell. Successful launches alone train per-Project frecency; Project pins, quiet Customize/All, visible unavailable states, and the shared ribbon/`⌘K` catalog carry exact configured source/model/effort choices without a reusable configuration Draft. **Clone to…** starts a distinct Agent Session with bounded handoff text, preserves the original, and passes no resume identity. Agent Types remain coming soon. Supersedes D35's selector-row presentation; full landed log and retained contract in the project doc
- D47 Project-scoped relaunch (landed 2026-08-03; decision `0032`): the recovery bar's one-click default resumes eligible Agents only in the selected Project; a single attached scope menu contains the distinct selected-Agent and all-Projects alternatives, with counts, while shells and identity-missing Sessions remain excluded. Supersedes D36's two-scope presentation without weakening its exact-identity or no-silent-revival contracts.
- D48 ⌘K nav-row ranking (landed 2026-08-04; operator hit it live — “How come I can’t open Usage from cmd+k?”): a custom cmdk filter with structural score bands (exact-name > name-prefix > word-in-name > keyword > fuzzy fallback) replaces default command-score ranking; row values become primary name + unique suffix with auxiliary terms as cmdk keywords, “go (to) X” phrasings resolve, session-name prefixes share the nav band so session switching keeps winning its own queries, and a minimal cmdk patch restores the library’s broken-upstream group re-sort. Full evidence in the project doc.
- D49 New Agent launcher redraw (landed 2026-08-06 after five operator review rounds): the production Cmd+T composer now renders two-to-four whole setup cards, one attached Engine/Model/Thinking/Permission drawer, More, and Start through the same canonical component as `/hud-gallery/agent-launcher`. Cards reserve stable skeleton geometry, keep the model name whole, move capability/vendor to the quiet secondary line, and replace visible Suggested/Pinned/Used prose with subtle accessible corner marks. ArrowDown enters the selected card's drawer; Left/Right and Home/End move across cards. Frecency and Project pins rank exact configurations without reordering the active row; unavailable setups remain visible; a launchable OpenCode with no default renders `Choose a model` and blocks Start rather than disappearing or receiving an invented default. More retains the complete configuration/Shell catalog plus worktree, roadmap, and quick naming. Full findings, adapter contract, and visual/keyboard gates are in the project doc.

- D50 Orientation and the navigation spine (landed 2026-08-07; one operator row plus three triaged backlog items, all of them the same question — where am I, and how do I get back): the Project you are inside PINS at the ribbon's left edge while its own tabs scroll underneath and the next Project's header pushes it out (operator, 2026-08-06: "I find myself forgetting what project I'm in"), implemented as one extra term in the ribbon's existing transform rather than a second floating copy; BUG-006's two halves are fixed in the history module that owns them — a ⌘W-destroyed tab is no longer a stop, and applying a location no longer records the hybrid state observed mid-apply, which is what made Back a two-element oscillator; FIX-007's cross-group ⌘K ranking is root-caused to cmdk never dropping unmounted items from its group sets (one stale id makes a group's score NaN and the sort arbitrary), fixed in `patches/cmdk.patch` and pinned by a failing-first test, which also retires D48's `navigationFirst` exemption; and ⌘/ answers the question it exists for by matching a typed key combination against the BINDING (`cmd shift t` and `⌘⇧T` find the same row) instead of the rendered string (FIX-001).

- D51 The marker is the contract, and gates that are owed get declared (landed 2026-08-07): BUG-008 — an amber needs-you tab ⌘J refused to visit — came from two predicates over two different fields, every painting surface reading `tabIsLive(tab)` while the jump queue read `tab.exitCode === null`, which one reconciliation branch sets together for a Session that exited while adopted. Eligibility becomes one shared rule in `session-status.ts` (`paintsAttention` / `attentionJumpQueue`) that both the strip and the queue call, closing the mirror defect at the same time (⌘J could otherwise navigate to a Session wearing no marker); the remaining legitimate no-op announces on the workspace's live region instead of leaving a pressed key indistinguishable from a broken one (D44). Alongside it, the delivery floor stops silently skipping the evidence a change owes: the repository owns 31 eval gates and the changed-path floor routed to ONE, because every browser/Electron eval needs a dev server the floor does not own — so the floor now REQUIRES a gated surface's gate to be declared through `--verify` or waived on purpose with `--waive-gate`, refusing before any expensive work and naming the exact command. Routing them proved the point immediately: the contract refused this very change, and two of the three gates it named turned out to be RED on their own — `eval:workspace:chrome` still asserts D46's configuration ribbon (BUG-009) and `eval:navigation` waits for a retired status label (BUG-010), both broken since D49 landed with nobody the wiser, and both additionally blocked by the first-run overlay that `primeEvalBrowserPage` was written to dismiss and which had zero callers repo-wide. Those two are `quarantined` in the map — announced on every landing that touches their surface, never enforced, carrying the backlog id that repairs them — because deleting the entry would throw away the fact that the surface owes evidence at all. D50's own review items land here too: the pinned header reads its neighbour from the laid-out row rather than the caller's Project list (a Project mid-close is in one and not the other), the ⌘J-style apply epoch now swallows only genuine stages of the applied location, the nav resolver registers once instead of on every tab-activity tick, and the pinned header gains the browser gate it should have shipped with.

- D52 The gates start paying (landed 2026-08-07): the first solo pass under D51's surface-gate contract, and it found work by being run rather than by being reported. BUG-003 was not an overflow at all — the option menu capped its list at a CONSTANT 18rem, so opened low in a short window Radix flipped it upward until its first options sat at -16px, above the top edge where nothing can scroll them back; opencode's catalog is simply the largest and hit it first. The cap is now the room Radix already measures, the list flexes inside it, and a 560px-tall window is a launcher-bench gate that fails with the old constant. BUG-010 and BUG-011, the two gates D51 had to quarantine, are repaired and enforced again: the chrome fixture names D49's setup row, cards, drawer handle and "All engines and models" instead of D46's ribbon and Customize, matches the drawer's permission control by the OptionMenu's own accessible name rather than the bare label that resolved to a legacy hidden Select, and D49's Start regained the hook the gate needs; the altitude gate anchors on the protocol's real `Working:` chip and asserts membership rather than equality, because the `active` signal selects working AND reviewing. The shared menu primitive joins the gate map — it is only ever seen through the launcher, and changing it ungated is exactly how a menu that opened off-screen shipped.

- D53 Resume gets a keyboard (landed 2026-08-13; feedback row `f05da191`, operator on dogfood 0.1.9 — "there's no cmd+k or discoverable keyboard shortcut for resume this agent"): the verb that brings a parked fleet back after a relaunch was the one daily verb that required the mouse, against both D9 keyboard authority and the ⌘K-is-a-backstop rule. D36/D47's two scopes gain rebindable registry chords — `⌘⌥R` this Agent, `⌘⌥⇧R` the recovery bar's own default scope (this Project, else every Project) — plus contextual ⌘K rows that are ABSENT rather than greyed when nothing is parked, and keycap hints on the bar's existing controls. `R` rides `⌥` because `⌘R`/`⌘⇧R` belong to this app's own View menu (Electron's `reload`/`forceReload` roles register those accelerators natively, so the renderer never sees the keydown); `⇧` escalates scope exactly as `⌘⌥[` (tab) escalates to `⌘⌥⇧[` (Project). Deliberately NOT an extension of D39's `⌘⇧T`, which restores a CLOSED Session as a stopped tab and spawns nothing — one chord whose consequence (including whether a provider starts billing) depended on invisible state is the ambiguity D39 removed. Recovery semantics are untouched: exact provider identity only (ENG-018), no auto-resume, no new confirmation, one shared derivation behind bar, chord, and row. Row names lead with "Resume" so D48's name-prefix band ranks the verb over a fuzzy Session hit.

- D54 New Agent FLOW design pass (planned, deliberately unshaped pending a design pass; evidence: a partner conversation): the operator named `⌘T` new-agent creation himself as the first place external design help should land — "kind of the easiest to chew on first thing would be, like, Command-T, I just did, to open a new tab, like, starting a new agent. This is, from a product development perspective, probably the highest, easiest thing to start with, I would say, like, how to make this really excellent and usable." Boundary that makes this a distinct milestone rather than a D49 repeat: D49 redrew the composer's SURFACE through five operator review rounds and its setup-card contract, frecency/pin ranking, drawer, and keyboard path are landed and gated — what is named here is the FLOW, from the intent to start work through to an Agent that is running the right thing. Do not shape it here; the design pass decides scope with the operator, from options rather than one comp (working style recorded in the partner conversation). The pass must read `docs/engineering/design-system.md` and inherits D49's gates. SECOND INDEPENDENT REPORT (a partner conversation): a different partner, a non-engineer, hit the composer cold eight days later and named the same problem without prompting — "What should this agent do — bigger and more centered. And prioritizing this piece here at the top is the most important thing by just giving a little more air to breathe" — with ChatGPT's new-chat state as his reference, plus "you don't need the 11th thing on here… maybe it's like a fade thing, like the top three are there and then they fade out." The operator's reframe in the same exchange is the input worth carrying into the pass, because it ties the flow to the product's own metaphor rather than to a form: this is "putting a guy on the map… adding a new unit to your fleet," and the failure is that "it feels like just typing a bunch of [text]." Two independent reports raise confidence that D54 is real; they do not shape it. THIRD INPUT, and the first from the operator as a USER STORY (a partner conversation, 2026-08-17): walking the design partner through the surface, he told the flow as her story rather than as a screen — "I want to explore a few different design directions for a project, and therefore, I want to start two different dudes running in two different directions at once, so I hit command T to open one new tab, one new agent… and then I tell him to explore direction A, please, and then maybe paste in some images or whatever, and then click start." Two requirements ride it, both his words: switching harness "should be, like, super easy, quick and simple to, like, almost, like, one click or, like, certainly with a keyboard", against a source list he expects to reach "like a dozen different harnesses at some point" spanning local and cloud; and the whole thing "should be, like, super quick and snappy and delightful" while "there's, like, a lot of different details that matter." The demo also produced the flow's first measured failure, live and in front of a partner: ⌘T did not produce a composer in time to be shown ("it actually took a while to load. So that's a UX problem"), and he fell back to the gallery's launcher study — `BUG-062`. Speed is now part of what this pass has to answer for, not only layout. Still deliberately unshaped.

- D55 Interaction performance architecture (landed 2026-08-16; decision `0038`): retain Electron and the existing DOM/xterm/R3F ownership split. One opt-in Electron evaluator now measures four warm gestures with first acknowledgment separated from intentional settle time. The five-run staged-standalone control at 120Hz cleared the provisional 80ms acknowledgment budget everywhere — tab 9.6ms p95, Agent → Team 22.2ms, Team → Agent 9.1ms, Team → Fleet 6.8ms — so the planned stop condition fired and NO speculative production refactor survived. A Turbopack-only Agent → Team miss was traced to development route work and falsified by the production renderer. The evaluator remains outside gates/CI/telemetry; broader cold/live/camera work is reference scope, not committed sequence, and no marketing claim was promoted because this mile measured existing behavior rather than shipping a gain. Full evidence and review contract: `docs/engineering/projects/interaction-performance-architecture.md`.

- D57 A verb cannot be born undiscoverable (landed 2026-08-16; FIX-012 + FIX-014): D44 made FIXED key families undrift-able and deliberately left the REBINDABLE class alone, so a registry verb still lived in four hand-maintained lists — `defaults.ts`, the palette's row ids, the renderer's menu-command map, and the menu template buried in `main.ts`, which the renderer cannot import and no test ever built. That is why Resume reached three reports: D53 gave it chords and ⌘K rows and the native menu item was still missing, with nothing able to notice. One command-verb manifest now lives in `@exawatt/core`, reachable from BOTH processes, and declares every verb's keyboard binding, palette row, and native menu item — each leg either real or `null` with a written reason, exactly D44's union. `defaults.ts` becomes a projection of it, the palette reads its row ids from it, the Electron template is extracted to `application-menu.ts` and derives labels, accelerators, enablement and tenant gating from it, and `command-verbs.contract.test.ts` joins every declared id to the surface that publishes it (removing one menu row fails two assertions). Auditing all 27 verbs against the contract found six more missing legs, all fixed here: ⌘B Roadmap had no menu item, ⌘⇧F never showed its combo because the Help row was hand-built outside the accelerator join, ⌘/ had no menu home at all, ⌘, was never listed in the cheat sheet or rebindable, the four Start-Agent rows were a hardcoded list that a fifth launchable Agent Source would not have joined (now derived from `contracts/agent-sources.json`), and the Usage label's documented cross-process mirror is gone now that both processes share a package. FIX-014 rides the same module: the application menu leads with `Version <app version>` and keeps the build sha behind it. `eval:navigation:spine` becomes the declared gate for the menu surface, since the old template had no gate because it had no file. Declaring gates caught a second defect on the way in: `eval:navigation` passed and failed at random on one tree because it treated Chrome's own `/favicon.ico` probe as a product error and reported it without a URL (Chrome puts a failed subresource's URL in the console message's LOCATION, not its text) — both halves fixed.
- D58 One owner for the Session surface (landed 2026-08-16; BUG-004, BUG-019): both defects were the same disease — the terminal pane's responsibilities were spread across places that did not know about each other. Link behaviour had FOUR owners: xterm core's built-in OSC 8 provider (whose unclaimed default is the “Do you want to navigate…” dialog plus a `window.open()` Electron denies, and whose http-only filter silently dropped every `file://` citation Agents emit), `WebLinksAddon`, an ad-hoc path regex blind to wrapped lines and bare repo-relative paths, and a context menu that knew about none of them. Geometry had none: three call sites resized the PTY and nothing related reported columns to painted pixels. Now `terminal-targets.ts` is the single link vocabulary, `terminal-link-provider.ts` the single buffer-aware provider (WebLinksAddon deleted), and `terminal-geometry.ts` owns the 12/8px inset and the one fit → publish → PTY-resize step. `eval:electron:terminal` moves onto the worktree dev server through `withElectronApp` and gains both contracts: painted-vs-reported width against the shell's own `stty size`, and hover/click/context-copy for plain paths, URLs, and OSC 8 hyperlinks. Incident `0013` records why the earlier pass could not find the dialog it was looking for.

- D59 The last two pointer-only verbs (landed 2026-08-16; finishes D57): D57 audited all 27 command verbs and named the two it did not carry rather than leaving them silently absent, because both needed a workspace event whose gate `eval:workspace:chrome` was quarantined at the time. `Reveal in Finder` and `Close project` reached NO command surface: no palette row, no rebindable binding, no native menu item, and one strip context menu as their only home, which is the exact defect the manifest exists to forbid. Both are now declared verbs in the shared manifest and every surface derives: a File-menu item beside `Open Project…`, a ⌘K row, and an availability key the native enablement sync reads. Neither takes a chord, and the type made that a written answer rather than a silence: R is spent four times over (Electron's own reload roles plus the ⌥R recovery family), and binding the Project close on ⌘⇧W would put an irreversible multi-Agent close one shift from the single-tab close that combo already aliases. `Close project` routes through the SAME `requestProjectClose` the strip uses, so FIX-011's confirmation is a property of the act and not of the entry point, and no Agent can lose a turn silently from a menu. `tenantScope` widens from "verbs that launch" to "verbs that reach personal-workspace truth", because a verb no other shell implements cannot use the availability snapshot as its tenant gate. The join that was missing: `menu.section` was declared by every verb and read by NOTHING, so a verb could claim the File menu and be rendered into Session and pass — the contract test now tags each row with the menu it was actually built into and holds the template to the declaration. Mutation-proved: deleting the verb fails the suite at import, deleting only its menu placement fails three assertions, and mis-placing it fails the new section join.
- D60 The evals drive the product through one contract (repaired 2026-08-16; BUG-014): D49 replaced the composer's second control row and left it in the DOM behind `hidden={!customizeOpen}`, with `customizeOpen` initialised false and only ever set false again — a complete pre-D49 control surface no production interaction could reach and only automation could still touch, which is exactly why the contract drifted silently in nine eval scripts and four unit-test suites at once. 607 lines leave `launch-controls.tsx` and `ModelPicker` goes with them (decision `0033` already made `OptionMenu` the one menu primitive). The repair is both halves the 2026-08-13 narrowing predicted: `scripts/lib/electron-eval.mjs` states the launcher's driving contract ONCE — engine choice through D49's setup drawer, which lists every launchable source whether or not it publishes a model and so can select Codex where the catalog provably cannot — and `scripts/lib/harness-probe-fixture.mjs` states what every fixture harness owes the product ONCE, so a fixture Codex publishes a model catalog like the real CLI and the product stops correctly refusing to launch an engine with no model. Routing is the half that prevents recurrence: `roadmap-rail-eval.mjs` had no package command at all, so nothing could declare it and nothing ever ran it; it is `eval:roadmap:rail` now and gated, and `eval:electron:project-agent` gates `launch-controls.tsx` and the shared eval driver itself. Routing paid for itself immediately: the first run of an eval nothing had ever run found a live regression in the roadmap rail's designed empty state (BUG-040), reproduced against an unmodified master tree. Full narrative in the project doc's D60 entry.
- D61 Three quarantined gates come back, and two of the three defects were the eval reading COPY where it meant STATE (2026-08-16; BUG-038, BUG-039, BUG-040): D60 routed three evals nobody had run and they immediately reported three product regressions. Two were not regressions. The roadmap rail's designed empty state was healthy; the eval had walked into another Project because ⌘J is a fleet-wide queue and the rail follows the selected tile, and nothing could tell because the rail published only the Project NAME. No draft survived an exact resume; `New agent` is the designed fallback identity for ANY untitled Session, so a live one was counted as a draft. Both surfaces now STATE themselves — `data-roadmap-project`, `data-tab-lifecycle` — and every assertion reads the state instead of the copy. The third id was real twice over: a launch moved the operator to main's realpath of the working directory instead of to the Project group holding the tab, so after an exact provider resume `activeProject` was null and every launch verb opened the Project opener instead of a composer; and Grok called a concrete model id an `account-default`, which is the launcher's signal to omit the model flag, so the composer showed a model it did not pin. All three gates leave quarantine and are enforced (42/42, 17/17, 40/40). A fourth defect falls out of the same routing and is recorded rather than repaired: BUG-041, the setup drawer closing on its own first axis. Full narrative in the project doc's D61 entry.
- D62 One composer, one identity: the surface stops remounting under the operator's hands (2026-08-16; BUG-041): the New Agent composer was two render sites in `workspace-client` — the empty-Project stage and the draft tab's pane — and the operator's first draft intent is what moves between them. Materialising the tab was therefore a REMOUNT mid-gesture, so adjusting the first axis of a setup closed the drawer holding the other three, every time, in a fresh Project. Neither candidate repair was taken: carrying the drawer's open intent across the hand-off and creating the tab before the first axis both accept the remount and manage its damage. `resolveComposerSlot` resolves both moments to ONE slot with one identity, so React reconciles in place and nothing needs carrying — drawer, settled catalogs, caret, focus and scroll all stay put. The identity ends only where the draft does, so a discarded draft cannot leave its task in the composer that replaces it. Every sibling path through the same hand-off was driven and fixed with it. `eval:electron:lifecycle` and `eval:electron:idempotency` leave quarantine and are enforced, which took two eval repairs a step further out than the product defect: a fixture contract that answered neither `claude --safe-mode` nor `codex app-server`, so every probe leaked a process a CANCELLED quit was then blamed for, and two assertions still written against copy and an IPC method the product retired. Full narrative in the project doc's D62 entry.
- D63 A dialog's primary action owes a chord and a hint (2026-08-17; BUG-049, and D62's residual): the operator asked how to send the Submit feedback form from the keyboard, and the honest answer was that no dialog in the product could be. D44/D57 gave every verb the product offers a chord, a palette row and a menu item from one manifest; a dialog's primary action is such a verb and was the class the manifest never reached. `dialog-primary-action` is now a manifest verb — ⌘⏎, rebindable, scoped to `modal-open`, and the first verb ever to declare that context — and `DialogContent` REQUIRES a primary action or a written reason it has none, so a dialog cannot be born with an unreachable Send button. `DialogFooter` renders the declared action and prints the chord on its face, the register D27's `Close ⏎` established. Five dialogs declared; the ⌘⇧F capture bar was already keyboard-first and was not the surface reported. D62's residual is taken with it: the composer's drop was re-measured at 41px and took the pane's height with it, and is now 0px, by RESERVING the chrome row from the empty-Project stage rather than removing it from the new-tab page — so no design call was needed and `eval:workspace:chrome` passes unchanged. Full narrative in the project doc's D63 entry.
- D65 A gate must name every file its script can be broken by (2026-08-17; BUG-058): `eval:navigation:spine` was reported red and is not — three consecutive clean runs on an unmodified `origin/master` prove it, and the red the report cites was BUG-045, closed hours earlier by ENG-030 WP2b-5A. What was real is that the gate has asserted the ⌘⇧F Quick feedback step since F1 while its match set named neither file that owns it, so BUG-049 could change `product-feedback-provider.tsx` and `ui/dialog.tsx` without being asked to declare it. Both are in `SURFACE_GATES` now, and the pattern is named there rather than patched a third time: `nav-history.ts` (BUG-035), `workspace-client.tsx` (BUG-041) and these two all failed the same way, and the gate did not even route its own script, because the map is written from the surface a gate is nominally about while its script asserts more than that surface. Three steps that used fixed sleeps and went red only under concurrent-worktree load now use bounded waits on the identical assertions. Full narrative in the project doc's D65 entry.

Sequencing: D0 → D1 → D2, then dogfood begins while D3–D7 continue at full speed. D17 precedes additional adoption polish without interrupting active ENG-015 or ENG-017 work. ENG-017 follows the adoption blockers; ENG-004 V2.1+ stays parked until dogfood asks for the map. D46's source and model-picker dependencies landed in ENG-003 S2/S3 before the completed ribbon runtime; no D46 dependency remains open.

Project doc:

- `docs/engineering/projects/daily-driver-adoption.md`
- D55 execution brief: `docs/engineering/projects/interaction-performance-architecture.md`

### ENG-017 Project roadmap lens

Status: active-build — S0–S13 landed through 2026-08-03; S13 turned the Team-altitude lens into a human-operable roadmap with convention-v2 Backlog, launch/attach, liveness, declared-only sequence/state manipulation, and repo remediation. RE-HOMED 2026-07-20 (operator): the lens moved from the Terminal view to the Sessions altitude (S12) because Sessions is the zoomed-out view and the roadmap is zoomed-out context.

The operator's stated goal for parallel agents is that each one "has enough food to eat" — a well-defined roadmap it can execute autonomously for hours. That state stays canonical, durable, and malleable **in each project's repo**; Exawatt does not own it. What Exawatt adds is a really good visualization: the roadmap sequence within each Project and what each agent is currently chewing through.

Delivery requirement on the convention (a partner conversation):
publishing the convention as a repo file is not
enough, because the agent that has to conform a repo is running inside someone
else's checkout with no access to Exawatt source. The operator described his own
workflow and then named exactly why it does not transfer: "every time I bring a
project into Exawatt for me, I tell it to go look in the Exawatt repo and figure
out the shape of the roadmap that you need, and then do it. But I have the code
access and you don't… So it has to be public, like on some website, like
xwatt.com/spec or something." The convention text already exists at
`docs/product/reference/roadmap-convention.md`; what is missing is a FETCHABLE
public URL, which makes this a website surface (ENG-031) fed by this item's
canon, and a natural companion to ENG-030 OS4's public compatibility contracts.
The failure it fixes was observed live: the partner's Project showed no roadmap,
he could not make it conformant, and his own agent's attempt at "clearly and
