simply visualize that roadmap" returned a reformatted markdown file he judged
"doesn't make it any easier to understand" — which is the demand S13 already
serves, from a user who cannot reach S13.

Scope:

- publish the Exawatt roadmap convention (`docs/product/reference/roadmap-convention.md`) and read it with a tolerant, diagnostic-honest parser; repos adapt to the published convention and the parser reports unrecognized structure instead of guessing
- linear single queue per Project in v2, with Backlog as an explicit unsequenced state; parallel/multi-track roadmaps recorded as future
- visualize sequence, status, and what each live session is executing; link sessions to items by inference (branch, worktree, title, context summary, commit subjects, with visible confidence and evidence) plus optional declare-at-launch; unmapped sessions stay visibly unmapped
- launch/attach links are machine-local view annotations; declared-conformant roadmaps expose sequence/state-only manipulation under decision `0035`, with no git operation
- resting posture stays COLLAPSED but the strip carries real signal; the lens is WORK-FIRST (sequence, not dates); Team and Fleet are the AGENT-FIRST mirrors; blocked items and starving queues join the existing needs-you attention pipeline
- "current" is whatever has a live session ATTACHED and "next" is queue position; progress renders as honest n/m milestone fractions, never blended percentages; color is reserved for blocked/starving/parse trouble
- the manipulable lens assigns an agent by construction and may reorder, change status, or tick milestones in a declared-conformant repo; the repo file stays the only project-state truth
- future arc, recorded and explicitly not built now: Exawatt manages the queue and assigns agents to it (bridges toward ENG-013), and non-code work classes flow through the same queue

Exit criteria:

- from the workspace, "what is this agent working on, and what's next in this Project" answers itself without opening the repo's docs
- the visualization reads from repo state; deleting Exawatt loses no project state
- the collapsed strip alone answers "roughly where are we, and is anything blocked or starving" from peripheral vision
- a blocked item or an empty queue reaches the operator through the same attention system as terminal bells
- linked-session context (item id + milestone fraction) is visible on the agent-first surfaces

Milestones:

- S0 Canon (landed 2026-07-11): roadmap entry updated from the shaped design pass; project doc created.
- S1 Convention + parser (landed 2026-07-11): published convention spec, decision `0011`, and the `@exawatt/core` roadmap parser with fixture tests; Exawatt's own roadmap parses declared-conformant with zero warnings.
- S2 Read-only rail (landed 2026-07-11): validated main-process read IPC, ui-model lens, workspace rail with ⌘B summon cycle and keyboard queue walk, verified by `scripts/roadmap-rail-eval.mjs`.
- S3 Inference linking (landed 2026-07-11): closed-vocabulary matcher with ambiguity-means-unmapped, per-cwd git evidence IPC, confidence-encoded session chips, unmapped shelf, reciprocal context-bar chip.
- S4 Declare-at-launch (landed 2026-07-11): optional "working on" picker in launch controls, `workspace.json` v3→v4 tab annotation with chained migration tests, declared-overrides-inferred.
- S5 Live watch + polish (landed 2026-07-11): debounced dir-level file watch surviving atomic saves and git rewrites, header sweep + FLIP row motion on reparse, two external repos adapted and parsing with zero warnings, honest degradation on an unadapted repo.
- S6 Signal-rich strip (landed 2026-07-11): the 36px collapsed strip becomes a readable spine — one node per queue item, attachment-marked current node, blocked/starving as the loudest pixel, hover/keyboard peek.
- S7 Sequence-first rail (landed 2026-07-11): agent chips with elapsed time on every attached row, n/m fraction pills, a compact sequence bar in the header, suppressed nominal color, drill-in milestone expansion.
- S8 Attention integration (landed 2026-07-11): blocked items and queue-empty starvation become first-class needs-you events through the existing attention pipeline — no second notification machine.
- S9 Agent-first mirrors (landed 2026-07-11): Sessions tiles and ⌘K rows carry the linked item id + milestone fraction; Spatial adopts the same chip when ENG-004 unparks.
- S10 Manipulable-lens design play (UNGATED 2026-08-02 for the ASSIGN direction only): the visual-language question resolved 2026-07-12 — the operator kept the shipped presentation and retired the replacement directions module. The gate on manipulable GESTURES now has an operator answer: **the operator assigns, agents execute.** Self-claiming by agents ("sounds awesome but needs some designing") is the destination, not the start, and would need an agent marked or blessed as automatic and/or an item marked ready for whoever wants it. Repo-file truth stays canonical either way; ENG-029 C2's assignment records are how a declared assignment becomes readable by other agents.
- S11 Legibility refinement of the shipped rail (landed 2026-07-12): plain-language group headings, hero progress bar and blocked line, checkmark milestone checklist, honest read-only footer, focus-gated scrollIntoView; the manipulable verb is ASSIGN. Multi-project roadmap truth explicitly deferred by the operator.
- S12 Sessions-altitude home (landed 2026-07-20; operator): the rail docks as a permanent panel in the Sessions overlay scoped to the selected Project and leaves the Terminal view entirely; `⌘B` becomes "open Sessions with the roadmap focused". Read-only posture and repo-file truth unchanged.
- S13 Human-consumable roadmap (INFLATED 2026-08-02; SHAPED 2026-08-03 in an operator design pass — full brief in the project doc's "Third arc" section): "Right now the roadmap is too dense, way too much copy, hard to work with as a human… I want to be able to navigate and manipulate and check out the roadmap very smoothly, very easily, like a beautiful Linear app." The division of labor stands: `roadmap.md` is AGENT-first and stays dense because agents need contracts; the lens is the HUMAN view. Decided: a **sequenced list with time as an honest lens** (no invented dates — dates are irrelevant in an agentic coding world, but the model must not foreclose them for future customers); **progressive prose** opening with what and why at a five-second grok, state before contract; **one queue with `backlog` as a STATE**, researched not invented (the 2026 consensus is one tracker, and Linear makes Backlog a status, not a view) so a defect hangs off its owning item AND appears in the backlog lane as one record with visible provenance — quick-capture feedback, operator triage, or incident; the **Team-altitude panel stays home** (no new tab, no new destination — a real constraint on the design); and **launch-from-item** becomes S10's long-gated ASSIGN verb. Sub-milestones below.
- S13.1 Legibility (landed 2026-08-03): state-first progressive detail, focused-width Team panel, and contract prose behind disclosure replace the wall of text.
- S13.2 One queue, backlog as a state (landed 2026-08-03): convention v2, parser, triage protocol, canonical backlog records, and provenance-bearing rows landed together; v1 remains compatible.
- S13.3 Launch from the item (landed 2026-08-03): primary start gesture declares the item at launch; attaching a running Session persists the same local annotation.
- S13.4 Liveness (landed 2026-08-03): attached agents expose elapsed/turn state and item detail shows recent item-referencing commits.
- S13.5 Queue manipulation (landed 2026-08-03): declared-only sequence/state writes follow decision `0035` with a named permission seam, compare/refuse, no git, inline lifecycle, and guarded undo.
- S13.6 Repo readiness (landed 2026-08-03): v1/v2 conformance is visible with parser diagnostics and nonconformant repos can launch a prefilled remediation agent.
- S13 defect (fixed 2026-08-03): reproduced against the live ENG-032 theming Session; inference now uses stable roadmap-title terms from the persisted initial task/context when the Session cwd remains the parent checkout.
- S14 Project state beyond the code roadmap (INFLATED 2026-08-02 from the operator brief): "Company / project state. Not just the code / product / feature roadmap, but: Vision, goals, etc." The lens already reads repo-owned state; this extends WHAT it reads. Relates to ENG-005 (Initiative), ENG-013 (intent custody reads the same state), and ENG-029 (the blackboard is where non-roadmap project state accumulates). Deliberately unshaped.
- S15 Batch execution from a prepared queue (EVIDENCE 2026-08-03, Ben Kim;
  DESIGN PASS REQUIRED): one operator wants to define roughly 20 onboarding
  experiments as tickets and have Exawatt build all 20 without repeating the
  one-ticket/one-Agent launch ritual. The design must keep planning and
  execution separable: review one coherent batch, preflight source readiness,
  unique assignment, isolation, policy, and capacity, then dispatch one
  attributable/cancellable Session per ready item under an explicit concurrency
  ceiling. The batch declares whether items are independent alternatives,
  cumulative work, or dependency-ordered; experiments never imply that every
  result should merge. “20 tickets” also does not imply 20 simultaneous
  launches. Partial failure must not poison the batch, every item remains
  visible through the ordinary roadmap and attention paths, and the repo
  roadmap remains the only work-state truth. Reuse S13.3's launch-from-item command and D46 Launch
  Configurations rather than create a second launcher. ENG-029 C2 supplies
  durable assignment visibility; result integration/landing remains an open
  design boundary rather than an implied git automation feature. SECOND
  INDEPENDENT USER (a partner conversation): a
  non-engineer partner on a toy game project asked for the same missing verb
  from the opposite end of the scale — "one command says figure out a roadmap,
  check that roadmap, spin up the agents you need to do those roadmaps and
  automatically command them each with their tasks that they need. Just get
  moving." He had already hand-rolled the worse version, having his agent
  generate six role prompts that it then handed him to copy and paste one at a
  time: "is there a way to have it automatically then say, cool, there are six
  agents. Do you want me to go ahead and spin up those six agents for you now?"
  Two users with nothing in common — 20 onboarding experiments and a pixel
  dungeon — describing plan-then-dispatch. Corroboration for the milestone's
  existence, not new scope; the design pass this milestone already requires
  still owes the answer.
- S13.7 Findable progress cockpit (OPERATOR DOGFOOD 2026-08-16; DESIGN PASS REQUIRED): S13's human view remains a dense pile the operator does not use daily. Make canonical milestones visibly tick down as done/active/next, increase working-size legibility, and add a slash-first find gesture. Deterministic id/title/text retrieval is the baseline; semantic retrieval remains a candidate, not a preselected architecture. The repo roadmap remains the only state truth.

### ENG-018 Deterministic session rehydration

Status: done — shipped and accepted 2026-07-11 in signed `v0.1.4` → `v0.1.5`. Exawatt keeps no local agent process alive after explicit quit and installs no daemon, tmux backend, or LaunchAgent; the logical Session survives instead. Uninterrupted background execution remains a future, separately justified capability.

Scope:

- durable Session identity distinct from ephemeral PTY process identity and from exact provider conversation identity; workspace persistence v4→v5
- crash-safe, machine-local, bounded terminal history: atomic writes, restrictive permissions, serialized append journals with bounded compaction, periodic checkpoints, corruption isolation
- one shutdown coordinator for explicit quit and update restart: native warning when processes are live, pre-stop checkpoint, termination, verified-stopped commit, no orphan runtime
- quiet rehydration: restore workspace, history, and honest lifecycle status without auto-starting; exact per-agent resume and count-named workspace recovery; shells remain stopped
- bounded, churn-tolerant Codex discovery with pre-spawn identity association and an atomic main-owned durable identity index; parallel agents never use latest/cwd guessing

Exit criteria:

- confirmed quit leaves no agent, shell, daemon, or launchd process behind
- relaunch reconstructs each logical Session and its retained history without starting work; exact resume never falls back to latest/cwd guessing
- quit and restored states are obvious from compact status and contextual actions, without persistent explanatory copy or a startup modal
- automated coverage and the literal multi-agent/update exercise both pass

### ENG-004 Modular UI regimes and Spatial Operations Board

Status: active-build — V3.7 one world, camera altitude LANDED 2026-08-17 (board geometry no longer depends on altitude/focus; hotkeys move the camera on the keystroke's frame; `eval:spatial:motion` gates cuts, latency, hitches, rewinds). V3.5 one motion owner and V3.6 contents-agree-with-circle LANDED 2026-08-16 (board transitions run off one shared clock; gesture state survives live data ticks; population reads as a centred mass sized against its Project). V3.2 fleet-altitude command previews LANDED 2026-08-03 (real multi-select + scope readout, announced Direct verb); V3.0 altitude handoff and V3.1 demo-scale rendering LANDED 2026-08-02 (V3.0 tuned against the populated Voltaic Demo Workspace board per the wave-2 amendment). UX PASS 2026-08-02 (operator + hands-on drive): the board as shipped reads chunky — V3.3 Feel & Fidelity is shaped below and decision `0024` adopts the RTS unit-control model, superseding V2.4's drag-pan pointer grammar on the board. DESIGN PASS HELD 2026-08-02 (operator), resolving the "deliberately unshaped" state the 2026-07-24 demo-asset reframing left. The board's purpose is now stated: an INSTRUMENT for situational awareness and a STAGE for scale and command — not a map of org structure. Altitude names and the transition model are decided in `0023`. V2.1's truth half and V2.2 stay parked; V2.3's consolidation question stays open and gated on dogfood evidence. History: V2.4 experience pass landed 2026-07-20; V2.0 landed 2026-07-10; PARKED 2026-07-10 during the daily-driver arc.

Thousands-scale note (operator, 2026-08-13; a partner conversation; NO scope
shaped here). Walking the
altitudes for an outside designer, the operator said the top tier's
visualization paradigm is still unsolved in his own eyes: "I'm not in love with
this UI paradigm, there needs to be some way to visualize thousands." Three
things follow, none of which change any landed decision. (1) The **argument for
why thousands is a real number** is enterprise ROI scaling, not ambition: an
enterprise proves one demonstrably ROI-positive agent application against
measured token spend, and then "why not do two agents? Why not do 200? Why not
do 2,000?… they'll need UIs to do that, so I do think that's why this whole
third tab exists." That is the demand argument behind V2.1's parked truth half.
(2) The **RTS reference and its caveat were restated unprompted** — the
operator reached for StarCraft-style unit selection again ("where else in user
interfaces, can you like click on and manipulate and talk to like hundreds of
people at a time, and it's in these kind of like strategy games") and hedged in
the same breath: "not that I want it to be, like, too militaristic, but the UI,
there are some UI overlaps, some UI analogies." This corroborates decision
`0024` and V3.3 F7's existing caveat that the reference governs the CONTROL and
LEGIBILITY model, not the aesthetic; it is evidence for that boundary, not a
new one. (3) The **R3F/GPU choice is deliberately under-exploited today** —
"right now, it obviously doesn't take advantage of a lot of this stuff, but it
can" — which is headroom, not a defect, and decision `0007`'s restraint still
binds every use of it. Open design directions floated in that call and
recorded WITHOUT scope: game-UI aesthetics and isometric treatments. Do not
shape either here; they are input to a design pass, not accepted direction.
The Escher exchange in that call was a joke (operator, 2026-08-13) and is not a
design direction — it is preserved in the research record only so the transcript
reads correctly.

Aesthetic direction moved on 2026-08-17, and the move is the operator's own
reasoning rather than an agreement (a partner conversation; NO scope shaped
here). Asked directly
by the design partner why he had moved away from the neon game-UI look, he gave
four answers: he is not the user — "I really like it, but I fear that because I
like it so much, might be a little alienating, like I'm not that representative
of all users"; it made the agents worse — "it seemed like the visual direction
was kind of confusing my agents a little bit and getting them stuck in like
directions that only looked cool and weren't necessarily useful" (ENG-036
carries that half); the priority is ordered explicitly — "I don't want coolness
to get in the way of usefulness… clean and functional, I think is a higher
priority than like sci-fi"; and it is **not** a retirement — "I'm, you know, not
opposed to it. Like, if we can make this work and look clean and functional."
So the game-UI direction recorded above stays open and gains a test it did not
have: a proposal in that register is judged on whether it is clean and
functional first. Two neighbours moved with it. **Hex tiling gains an argument
against it from the person who wanted it** — "hex is really good as just, like,
kind of tiling system. But it kind of locks us into 2D." And **isometric is
named for the second time in four days as the camera-viewpoint answer** — "I
like isometric. And that's what I was trying to go for with the angle versus
top. So you could just like choose your angle" — matching his 2026-08-14 answer
on the Top/Angle control ("I don't want to retire it. I want it improved. Maybe
isometric versus other modes"). Two independent restatements raise confidence
that viewpoint choice is the real requirement; they still shape nothing, and
decision `0007`'s restraint binds whatever a design pass proposes.

The altitudes as one continuous zoom, restated against a decision that already
rejected it (same conversation; the boundary STANDS). Explaining the ladder to
the design partner, the operator went past the recorded model: "in theory, they
could and should, like, live in the same space, like, why can't I just, like,
fully zoom in, and keep zooming in, and keep zooming in, and there it is, the
whole app is just one continuous flow, but right now, they're built such that
they're, like, a little bit discontinuous." Decision `0023` considered exactly
this and rejected the literal version — "rendering xterm into WebGL so terminals
are literally zoomable objects on the board. Rejected: expensive,
fidelity-losing, and contrary to the zoom-changes-resolution rule" — and its
transition model already says Agent ↔ Team IS continuous while Team ↔ Fleet is a
DOM-to-WebGL boundary by construction. This is recorded as pressure on that
decision, not as a change to it: the aspiration is real and durable enough to
have surfaced twice, but nothing here re-prices what `0023` priced. Do not
implement toward it, and do not soften the exit criterion that zoom changes
information resolution rather than glyph scale.

Camera and layout evidence from an external user (a partner conversation; NO
scope shaped here). A non-engineer design
partner drove the board on his own `0.1.8` install and produced three
complaints in ninety seconds, which is the first unprompted outside read of the
shipped camera. (1) **Zoom-out hits an arbitrary ceiling** — "it seems like it
has a limit to how wide it'll go" — and the motion is "a little slightly
laggy"; close-in zoom was fine. (2) **The Top/Angle toggle is too thin to be
worth the control.** The operator agreed against his own shipped control:
"angle mode is like kind of silly." OPERATOR ANSWER 2026-08-14, and it is not
a retirement: "I don't want to retire it. I want it improved. Maybe isometric
versus other modes or something like that." Camera viewpoint stays a real
control; a two-option Top/Angle pair is not enough of one. Isometric is named
as a candidate, which meets the isometric direction ENG-036's design engagement
surfaced the day before — both remain open design directions, not accepted
scope, and decision `0007`'s restraint binds whatever a design pass proposes. (3) **Layout does not respond to activity.** With four
Projects the board spread them sideways; the partner wanted packing and
activity-weighted sizing — "if one's got a hundred things, make that one
bigger… they should rearrange" — and asked the framing question "why would I
want it so I can't see two of my projects, or more than one at a time?" V3.1's
density-sized zones size a zone by population; what is described here is
PACKING and rest-position, which do not exist. The operator's own instruction
is the actionable half and applies to F3's camera work: "I've been trying to
reinvent the wheel too much here. I think I just need to copy the zoom dynamics
of Figma, or the scaling dynamics of Civ, or just find some example where
they've actually put some design science into it." Recorded as an OPEN IA
question, not a decision: the same partner proposed Fleet as the entry altitude
("should fleet be view 1? And start wide dial down instead of start close") and
the operator agreed in the moment and stated the success criterion — "I would
view this product as successful if people are spending most of their time in
fleet." OPERATOR ANSWER 2026-08-14, which splits the proposal in half: "maybe it
could be the default view to start with, but I still like the ordering we have."
The STARTING view is open and belongs to a design pass; the altitude ORDERING is
not open. ENG-016's Project-first startup and ENG-026's IA still deliberately
open close, so nothing moves here without that pass.

Read every in-call agreement in that record against the operator's own warning
about how he behaves on these calls (2026-08-14): "in general on these calls I'm
pretty agreeable. Yes you should look for canon to promote, but also sometimes I
just say yes to keep the conversation flowing. And it's a jam session, no idea
is a bad idea." Agreement in the moment is not a decision. Promote it only when
he returns to it, reasons from it himself, or confirms it afterwards — which is
exactly what the two OPERATOR ANSWER stamps above are.

Identity note (operator, 2026-07-06; clarified 2026-07-10): this regime stays distinct from the terminal workspace, but its character is a **solid operations map**, not an immersive world. The operator rejected free-camera 3D, floating islands, oversized world objects, and decorative depth; decision `0007` records the reversal while preserving decision `0003`'s R3F and DOM/WebGL boundaries. V0.1–V1.3.1 remain implementation history and evidence, but their immersive 3D composition is superseded.

Scope:

- keep `/fleet` as the DOM operations UI for dense text, forms, chat, and accessible controls, and `/fleet/spatial` as the R3F Spatial Operations Board over the same fleet state
- introduce a pure `@exawatt/ui-model` boundary for shared selectors, view models, spatial layout data, and typed command contracts; the spatial surface never imports provider-specific protocol types
- prepare the spatial surface for later extraction into an independently packageable surface or Electron entrypoint
- render a stable tactical substrate grouped by Project / Context Group; one consolidated route with altitude-specific render regimes (Fleet aggregates, Project reveals anchored Agent pieces, Agent gives orientation before entering the Session)
- provide top-down orthographic and shallow fixed-angle projections over the same layout and selection state; no free orbit
- preserve automatic deterministic layout now; user-authored placement is reserved for V2.2
- use depth, lighting, and material effects only where they improve hierarchy and interaction feedback
- introduce Attention Scheduling as a product and UI-model concern for routing scarce human cognition to high-leverage blocker, approval, and decision moments

Exit criteria:

- DOM and spatial surfaces render from the same UI-facing model, and Demo Mode and Live Mode use the same UI-model contracts
- the spatial route can select Agents, surface blockers, and enter the Session workspace through a quick visible transition; `/fleet` never loads the Three.js bundle unless spatial is opened
- the first serious demo shows 5–8 Agents across a few Projects as a composed board, with no planets, floating islands, decorative rooms, or excessive empty world space
- zoom changes information resolution rather than glyph scale, and fleets of tens of thousands stay navigable through aggregation, instancing, culling, and label budgets
- top-down and fixed-angle views preserve the same stable spatial addresses and command semantics
- pointer, keyboard, and programmatic camera motion share one damped input model with <80ms acknowledgment and no free orbit
- every Project and Agent selectable in WebGL has a focusable DOM equivalent; reduced-motion and low-power modes preserve the complete command path
- at rest the demand-rendered scene parks, postprocessing is lazy and low-power gated, and performance is measured on the full spatial route
- the milestone narratives in the project doc stay current as work lands

Milestones:

- V0.1 Correct the Metaphor (landed): readable 2.5D Project surfaces, agent tiles, operator attention lane.
- V0.2 Gorgeous 2.5D Command Surface (landed): liquid glass / metal / crystal material system with tasteful state motion.
- V0.3 Zoom-Resolution Model (landed): fleet, Project, and Agent altitude states with increasing information density.
- V0.4 Attention Scheduling (landed): leverage-aware UI-model selectors for hero attention, secondary attention, and ambient work.
- V0.5 Fleet-Scale Readiness (landed): semantic clustering and progressive disclosure for dozens to hundreds of agents.
- V1.0 AgentField Command Surface (landed): live fleet state as an instanced cluster map with hero-sector triage and full keyboard; Console 3D removed.
- V1.1 Feel Pass (landed): camera tuning, held-key glide pan/zoom/orbit, sectors as first-class click/hover targets, drag-guarded clicks, label and tooltip polish.
- V1.2 Meaning Pass (landed): in-world hero-blocker callout with its Attention Schedule reason, sector statLines, and activity blips on agent nodes.
- V1.3 Semantic Regimes & Feel Recovery (landed 2026-07-10): restored Fleet → Project → Agent resolution, stable tactile regimes with screen-aligned DOM controls, corrected camera/tooltip/identity/motion behavior, and a repeatable full-route spatial evaluator.
- V1.3.1 Live Sparse-Fleet Composition Recovery (superseded 2026-07-10): implementation evidence remains useful but operator review rejected the 3D motif; its failed sparse fixture is a permanent regression input for V2.0.
- V2.0 Spatial Operations Board (landed 2026-07-10; B0–B5): deterministic board layout, orthographic tactical renderer, semantic aggregation, anchored Agent pieces, projection switcher, minimap, and a visible board-to-Session transition with exact semantic-address return.
- V2.1 Scale & Truth (parked): density and interaction budgets at 1k/10k+, Initiative-level aggregation, aggregate Project drill, viewport culling, label budgets, frame/memory instrumentation. Parked 2026-07-24 with the demo-asset reframing.
- V2.2 User-authored spatial organization (parked): opt-in drag/reposition, selection groups, drawn Project or team zones, layout persistence and versioning, keyboard equivalents. Parked 2026-07-24 with the demo-asset reframing.
- V2.3 Consolidation (planned, open question): decide from dogfood evidence whether spatial becomes the default fleet view; integrate palette actions; retain `/fleet` as the accessible dense-text sibling and `/hud-gallery` as the workbench. The question stays open and is gated on dogfood evidence, not on a date. Fresh input 2026-08-02: the operator chose board-as-Fleet-only for the V3.3 arc while explicitly reserving the right to revisit board-as-Team later — do not treat either outcome as settled.
- V2.4 Experience pass — graphics, motion, navigation, transitions (landed 2026-07-20): world materiality, entrance/breathing/altitude motion, pointer drag-pan and wheel zoom-to-cursor on the same damped camera, and directional regime transitions. AMENDS the V2.0 parked-at-rest gate: the visible scene may carry ambient status motion, parking under reduced motion, low power, and hidden tabs. Decision `0007` constraints all hold.
- V3.0 Altitude handoff (landed 2026-08-02; decision `0023`): ascending Team → Fleet captures each visible Project card's identity + screen rect, the board arrives at an ENTRY POSE solved from them (least-squares when card order correlates with zone addresses; scale + centroid when it does not, with entry zoom clamped to [1.06×fit, 2.2×fit]), card ghosts crossfade into zones in place, and only then does the camera pull back to fit. Identity and position carry across the DOM→WebGL boundary; content never does. The D11 transition owner remains the only implementation — the board rig is an executor over two events — and hard-cuts to the fast directional transition under reduced motion, low power, a missed 900ms frame budget, stale/missing/duplicate captures, viewport changes, wrong arrival regime, degenerate geometry, or renderer failure; falling back is a normal, event-driven outcome. Transitions never block input (pointer-events-none ghosts, mid-crossfade keystrokes obeyed, operator camera input suppresses the automatic pull-back). Evidence, solver rationale, and the fallback matrix live in the project doc; `eval:spatial` carries four dedicated handoff scenarios over the `/eval/t11-altitude-handoff` Voltaic fixture. SEQUENCE RECONCILIATION (2026-08-03): the same-evening V3.3 plan sequenced this milestone onto S2's continuous-transition foundation, written before this landing was known; the invariant that ordering protects — ONE transition machinery, never a parallel implementation — is held by construction (the D11 owner was extended, not duplicated), so S2 ABSORBS the rig-side entry-pose execution (claim → pose → hold → pull-back) into its surviving-layers machinery rather than rebuilding it; the nav-side handoff contract (capture, store, solver, ghosts, the two events) is stable and stays.
- V3.0 integration refinement (2026-08-03, amended by V3.3 S3): circular Project footprints exposed a latent contradiction in the entry lower bound: `[fit, 2.2×fit]` permits an entry pose exactly at fit, so the promised pull-back can disappear. The runtime lower bound is now `1.06×fit`; the one D11 owner, solver, pose targets, fallback matrix, and 2.2× ceiling are unchanged.
- V3.1 Demo-scale board — RENDERING (landed 2026-08-02): instanced population dot fields expand every aggregate piece into per-agent status dots in ONE InstancedMesh; density-sized zones fix the empty-sliver giant-Project drill; projected-size zone-label tiers (full card ↔ one-line chip) keep the population visible at fleet fit. Measured headed on the real W4 Voltaic fleet (173 Agents) and synthetic 1k/10k headroom: never misses vsync during driven motion at any scale (render-interval p50 = the refresh interval, p95 ≤ 1.8ms over it), render CPU ≤2.6ms p95, ≤13 draw calls, ≤41MB JS heap, parks at rest (0 frames); numbers, caveats, and strategy table in the project doc. Unparked the RENDERING half of V2.1 only; V2.1's truth half (Initiative-level aggregation, aggregate Project drill) stays parked. Ownership boundary held: W4 owns the data, this milestone owns the pixels.
- V3.1 review fixes (landed 2026-08-02, same-day verified review): minority-status pin (a truncated band never drops to 0 dots — 1 blocked among 10k stays visible, test-pinned); scale-eval percentiles cover the driven motion window only (the idle-tail dilution made cadence a vsync tautology) and quote render-interval + render-CPU with the display-refresh caveat; park gate tightened to === 0 and draw-call gates ratcheted onto a max-across-renders probe; label-tier hysteresis bound to the narrowest visible zone and re-evaluated on width changes; reduced-motion entrances snap (matchMedia read synchronously); dot-region insets and the density sizing pitch derive from shared `@exawatt/ui-model` constants.
- V3.2 Fleet-altitude command previews (landed 2026-08-03; pointer/chrome amended by V3.3 production review): multi-select is REAL selection — the landing used shift-drag while plain drag panned; V3.3 now makes plain primary drag the band-selection verb and moves camera pan to middle drag, WASD, and trackpad/wheel. Shift-click still toggles pieces and zone plates, and the complete keyboard path holds (shift+1–9 toggles zones, shift-activate on the focusable zone/agent DOM controls, Esc releases before ascending); band-hit + zone-capture semantics are pure `ui-model` selectors (`selectSpatialBandAgentIds`: piece centers as RTS units, dot-rendered zones captured whole on intersection, piece-owned zones never grabbed wholesale). On any selection the board's existing dashed-teal selection language marks pieces and fully-captured zones (one segmented Line2 draw). The working/blocked/idle scope summary now appears only for an actual multi-selection; the default duplicate fleet summary and its unexplained token-burn figure are retired. The ONLY announced part is the command verb: an `AnnouncedChip` "Direct N Agents" — dashed, inert, honest. No fan-out mechanism ships in this arc.
- V3.3 Feel & Fidelity (shaped 2026-08-02 from the operator UX pass; brief in the project doc's "UX pass — 2026-08-02" section, control model in decision `0024`): F1 continuous transitions — LANDED 2026-08-03 after direct production review: board layers survive altitude changes, Project addresses stay stable across resolution, focusing retains neighboring Projects in one persistent world and a fixed-world minimap, camera snapshots no longer reset navigation to an old origin, rerenders no longer swallow operator pan, and the unit field inverse-morphs from its actual prior frame while the arrival stagger remains entry-only; F2 keyboard unit navigation — LANDED 2026-08-03: arrows walk nearest visible Agents with camera follow, while WASD becomes secondary keyboard pan; F3 RTS pointer grammar — LANDED 2026-08-03 (primary flip) and COMPLETED 2026-08-07 (clamp feedback): click-drag band-selects and never pans, middle drag/WASD/scroll pan, pinch zooms, and the camera now has real bounds that answer instead of eating input — a pushed gesture travels a bounded elastic excursion past the bound and damps back, with a redundant motion-free edge indicator for reduced motion and low power; F4 tiled game board — beveled hex Agent units inside population-sized circular Project boundaries, pushed-WebGL materiality within decision `0007` restraint, prototyped in `/hud-gallery` for operator review first; F5 in-world identity — pieces carry the durable context goal (never the duplicated harness title) plus the current activity sentence at Team/Agent altitude; F6 selection command panel — LANDED 2026-08-07 with S4: one docked selection panel replaces the always-present inspector rail and the fleet-wide activity feed, existing only while something is selected (single Agent: identity, status, goal, cost/tokens/turns, blocker, delegated children, its own recent Events, Open session; multi: D40 bucket counts plus the announced Direct N Agents verb). Board chrome is now the header status strip, one bottom-right tool cluster with the key hints folded in, and the needs-you queue. The production WebGL/Three.js world and circular-Project/hex-Agent hierarchy remain approved. Pick-up-cold contract: project doc §"V3.3 execution contract"
- V3.3 F7 Composition and learnability (landed 2026-08-03 with S3 after operator gallery review; production-review amended the same day; camera/input hardening landed 2026-08-04; doctrine in the project doc's "Composition doctrine" section): decision `0024` settled how the board is CONTROLLED; this settles what it is COMPOSED of, from the same RTS motif — "overlooking my fleet… like StarCraft or Red Alert 2, where I can have all my units working against a given goal and I can select them and re-vector them; a very zoomed-out view of tmux", with the explicit operator caveat that the reference governs the control and legibility model, NOT the aesthetic (decision `0007` restraint holds in full). The production WebGL/Three.js world now applies all four rules: (1) individual hex Agents remain visible through the 173-Agent Voltaic fleet and agglomerate only above the 240-unit very-far boundary, preserving circular Project mass, density, counts, and stable addresses; (2) attention overlays structure through the owning unit/zone mark plus the queue/minimap, with the bell popup retired; (3) only individual Active units move while resting and very-far aggregate scenes park; (4) the existing header metrics strip names and counts all five D40 marks as multi-select filter pills, replacing duplicate legend/filter chrome. S3 also lands circular population-sized Project zones, goal/current-activity identity, one compressed header cluster, and a fixed-world minimap carrying population, active/urgent state, selection, and the actual projected viewport. Camera policy is now pure/tested: bounded composition-preserving altitude zoom, continuous projection interpolation, safe-zone Agent follow with manual suspension/reticle resume, no Agent refit, mouse band selection plus direct-touch pan/pinch/tap and explicit touch selection mode. The accepted gallery study was retired after production wiring per ENG-036.
- V3.4 Delegation composition (landed 2026-08-07; owned jointly with ENG-023 D3c; operator visual review of ratio/tether/overflow still owed): D3b's dot-only treatment is replaced at individual board resolution by same-family beveled hex child units at `0.72x` the parent, deterministic rosette slots on the upper/side perimeter that never enter the parent's label lane, a hairline Project-identity tether carrying lineage, an overflow lobe above five children that preserves the exact census, and finite damped spawn/stop transitions. Slot geometry, the overflow boundary, and lineage endpoints are pure `ui-model` policy (`selectSpatialDelegationUnits`); the R3F layer is a damped executor. Aggregated tiers emit no units by construction, so a very-far fleet never becomes a hairball. Voltaic stays at 13 draw calls and the 1k/10k aggregate tiers at 6

Sequencing (amended 2026-07-10; extended 2026-08-02): V2.0 replaced the V1.3.1 acceptance gate and was executed in independently verified phases. Do not start V2.2 or V2.1's truth half before dogfood asks for them. V3.0 and V3.1 ride the demo arc alongside ENG-026/ENG-027. Do not overinvest in cinematic transition machinery — the fallback cut is the safety valve that makes the handoff worth attempting.

Project doc:

- V3.5 One motion owner (landed 2026-08-16, from an operator usability report): a band-selection rectangle that caught nothing stayed on screen after the mouse released — "it should be more temporary, like I'm in StarCraft or Command and Conquer, selecting units." The cause was not the marquee. The board's pointer listeners re-registered on every fleet tick because the effect depended on the selection callbacks, and gesture state lived as closure locals inside that effect, so live data arriving mid-drag tore down the in-flight gesture: `pointerup` found nothing to end, the rectangle was stranded, and pointer capture was never released. Pans and pinches died the same way, less visibly. Gesture state now lives in a value that outlives re-registration (`operations-board-gestures.ts`, pure and tested), the listeners register once, and release always clears the marquee before deciding whether anything was selected. The same pass answered the operator's standing "the hexes go faster than the circles" and "jerky transitioning between zoom views" reports, which were ONE architectural defect rather than tuning: an altitude change had FOUR independent motion owners — zone plates and edges teleported to their new coordinates, pieces eased at lambda 7.5, the camera at 5.5, and every HTML label ran its own third. Two measured causes. `MathUtils.damp` starts at maximum velocity, so every semantic move began with a step change in velocity, which is what reads as a jerk; and two damped owners at different lambdas diverged 11.4% at peak and finished 0.22s apart. Camera zoom was also mixed in linear space although zoom is multiplicative, so zoom-in read 66% complete at its own midpoint and zoom-out 34% — the two directions were not the same move. Motion is now split by KIND, which is the durable rule (recorded in the R3F authoring guide, points 4b/4c): semantic transitions run off ONE shared clock with a real duration and an ease that leaves and arrives at rest, and every layer samples the same progress; continuous motion (pan, wheel, pinch, follow, hover, clamp rubber-band) keeps damping, because its target is still moving and there is nothing to arrive at. All eight content layers now sit inside one `BoardField` group that owns the transition, so the field moves as one object instead of each layer rediscovering its own coordinates; the least-squares carry transform that keeps what the operator was watching under their eye moved out of the piece layer into pure tested policy. Measured on the bench: motion now starts at 0.1% of peak speed (damping starts at 100%), peaks at the midpoint, and settles in ~435ms. Boyscout: the board study could not reproduce ANY of this — it never wired selection, so the marquee never rendered, and it snapshotted its search params once at mount, so its own altitude control only rewrote the address bar. Both fixed; the bench now exercises transitions and selection. The packing suite gained a containment invariant (no unit ever reaches past its own Project circle) across five fleet shapes at both altitudes, which the overlap tests could not catch.
- V3.6 Contents that agree with their circle (landed 2026-08-16, from the V3.5 review): a Project you are not focused on rendered its population as a short bar hanging under its own label instead of as a mass inside its circle, and the emptiness grew with the circle, so the biggest Projects looked the barest. The cause was in the dot field, not the board model. `computePopulationDotField` generates candidate slots by scanning rows down the circle and then fills them in generation order, so a Project only ever occupies its TOP rows and everything below stays unused. Slots are now chosen NEAREST THE MIDDLE and emitted row-major, which keeps the status banding reading top to bottom while making the population a centred mass whose radius grows with the count: the circle says how much room a Project has, the mass says how much of it is running, and the two finally agree. Measured at 1k scale, a 334-Agent Project fills its circle while its 26-Agent neighbours show small central masses. A second defect sat in the slot lattice itself and affected every altitude: rings were filled as one continuous arc, so a partly filled ring left a Project's contents leaning away from the gap — and because the gap lands wherever the population happens to stop, neighbouring circles leaned in OPPOSITE directions (measured ±0.56 units) and visibly disagreed about where their own middle was. Rings now take opposite sides in turn, and the within-ring order sorts on true cartesian angle rather than the sheared axial pair, which was walking large rings out of order. Seven of ten Voltaic Projects now sit exactly centred and the rest lean by one unpaired unit, which is the floor. **The trade is recorded because it is not free**: centring a Project's contents on the slots actually occupied would balance them exactly, and it was implemented and then reverted, because an Agent's position is its ADDRESS and every arrival would have shifted every Agent already placed. Balance comes from the fill order, never from re-centring, and a test now pins that an Agent never moves because a sibling arrived. One further change was written and reverted as invisible: the board model's aggregate status pieces carry positions and sizes that nothing draws — `population-dots.ts` is their only consumer and reads status and count alone — so sizing that cluster to its Project changed nothing on screen. Recorded so the next reader does not re-derive it.
- V3.5/V3.6 review hardening (landed 2026-08-16, from a review of the same day's work; every finding was reproduced before it was fixed): FIVE defects, three of them introduced by V3.5 itself. (1) **The shared transition clock had two writers and only one of them was safe.** The camera re-snapshots its start pose on every flight, but the field only recomputed its carry on an altitude change, so any camera flight starting within the transition window — toggling projection, recentering — restarted the clock and rewound the field to the pose it began from (measured: one rewind of 0.043 in scale, against a clean monotonic control). The field now re-reads where it actually IS whenever the clock restarts underneath it, so a restart continues the journey rather than replaying it. (2) **Reduced motion switched on mid-flight froze the whole board permanently**, at scale 0.476 and offset (7.3, -7.9) — both the layout effect and the frame loop bailed out on `reduced` and nothing returned the field to rest, so the board stayed at under half size in the wrong place for the rest of the session. The field now takes its resting pose the moment motion is switched off, and `cancelBoardTransition` gives an abandoned transition a name. (3) **The pointer cleanup did not do what its own comment claimed**: the doctrine that a marquee never outlives the hand that drew it was documented as covering unmount, and the effect teardown only removed listeners. It now hides the marquee, resets the cursor, releases every held pointer capture, and ends the gesture — which also makes `pointerId` load-bearing instead of dead state a test was asserting on. (4) **V3.6's dot-field centring cost 2.5x what it should have** (2.6ms to 6.4ms per data tick at 10k Agents) by sorting slot objects twice. Sorting a copy of the distances instead allocates nothing per slot, and since slots are generated row by row, filtering them in place preserves emission order and removes the second sort entirely: back to 3.2ms, with the nearest-set and row-major contracts now pinned by tests. Frame p95 at 10k improved from 16.3ms to 9.5ms. The general lesson, recorded because it will recur: a shared clock is the right shape, but every sampler that captures a start pose owes an answer to "what if this restarts underneath me", and every motion owner owes an answer to "what if motion is switched off while I am mid-flight".
- V3.7 One world, camera altitude (LANDED 2026-08-17; shaped the same day from operator drive: "1 / 2 / 3 do switch between projects but it's jerky, there are split-second scale shifts and translation issues, it feels nothing like a true RTS game UI"): the diagnosis was structural and every symptom followed from it. **Board geometry was a function of `(altitude, focusedProject)`.** Measured on the real `/fleet/spatial` client with a per-frame pixel-diff recorder: on every `1`/`2` press there was ONE frame, 84–142ms after the key, with the camera perfectly still, whose diff was 2–3x the peak of the whole subsequent flight — the world cut, then the camera flew; between two Projects there was no flight after the cut at all. From the model: the focused zone's radius (13.45 → 10.25), pitch (2.75 → 1.3) and unit (1.49 → 0.88) swapped with its neighbour's and 33 pieces flipped kind between hexes and dots in one frame, while zone centres never moved. **Research** (Supreme Commander / Planetary Annihilation strategic zoom, StarCraft II / AoE hotkey cameras, van Wijk & Nuij 2003 as shipped in Mapbox `flyTo` and d3 `interpolateZoom`, d3 semantic-vs-geometric zoom, Misue/Eades mental-map preservation, Unreal/three.js LOD hysteresis, Mapbox label stability) converged on the rule the diagnosis implied: the world's layout is fixed and altitude is a camera state; detail reveals by REPRESENTATION at the same anchor with hysteresis and a fade, never by moving or resizing anything; hotkeys are cuts or short glides; a flight is one coupled path, interruptible. This does not contradict `0007`/`0023` — WHAT is shown still changes with altitude (labels, activity, neighbour dimming, dots → hexes at scale) while WHERE things sit does not; the re-layout was an implementation artefact no decision asked for, and it violated the doctrine's own population-sized circles (the focused circle grew 31% because it was focused). **What landed, and what the investigation kept finding underneath:** (1) `ui-model`: one geometry for every altitude; the `detailed`/`projectFootprint`/`densityZoneRect` enlargement path is deleted and the camera supplies the room (`semanticZoomRatio` 1.45 → 3.6, carrying the 1.69x the unit used to). (2) Piece kind stops depending on focus at individual scale — Voltaic keeps hexes everywhere — and above the individual boundary the focused Project's reveal is a scale-in/scale-out on the board's transition policy (`operations-board-emergence.ts`, pure, tested, snaps under reduced motion), for bodies AND their status marks. (3) Focus reads through information: non-focused zones recede (bodies and plates mix toward the board on the shared clock; status lights never dim; labels compact) — `useBoardFocusRecession`. (4) The client holds the semantic address as STATE and mirrors it to the URL (`board-route.ts`, pure, tested); the URL re-syncs it on deep link/back/forward; the commit is a `startTransition` so a keydown's discrete flush no longer blocks the first frame. (5) The hotkey kicks the camera on its own frame (`focusProject`/`focusFleet` on keydown), and the commit that follows JOINS the flight rather than restarting it; the field joins an already-running clock; the projection effect flies only on a projection CHANGE. Measured: first camera motion 7–33ms after keydown on both fixtures (was 84–142). (6) **Three latent defects the cut had been hiding, all fixed:** the WASD glide fired a zero-nudge on EVERY keyup — any key, camera key or not — which told the camera the operator had taken the wheel (dropped the flight into damp mode, re-clamped the target, suspended follow; measured as a stray 19% zoom step between a hotkey and its flight; now only a key the glide holds can release it, and a still glide never nudges — tested); the bounded semantic zoom (x1.45) and the F3 clamp (min = 0.55 x full fit) never agreed, so the resting pose after a hotkey was outside its own limits (limits now derive from the pose the move lands on); and root-level state that flipped mid-flight re-rendered every layer under the canvas — the zone-label tier (a 58ms task at +376ms, now `operations-board-label-tier.ts`, an external store with hysteresis that only the zone controls subscribe to; the label DOM is one structure with tier and focus as classes, never a subtree swap) and the minute clock for delegation elapsed labels (proven: exactly two renders in 65s, both on the wall-clock minute; now lives with its only consumer). (7) `OperationsBoardSurface` is a `memo` boundary: the React Compiler is NOT enabled in this app (lint-only), so without it every render of the fleet client — the URL catching up, a clock tick, a fleet tick — re-rendered the surface, the canvas, and every layer under it, measured at 56ms and landing mid-flight. (8) **`eval:spatial:motion`** — the oracle for this class: drives real hotkeys on the real client AND the 1k scale fixture (which now answers drills), captures the WebGL canvas in a microtask after every `renderer.render` (a capture on its own rAF races the demand loop and can read an empty buffer), and gates: no cut (a stationary-camera frame may not exceed the flight's moving median), first camera motion within 60ms (any change from a bit-identical rest pose), no mid-flight hitch over 50ms after the commit frame, no rewind, and the commit gap reported and loosely gated. It calibrates the box first (idle rAF cadence and a fixed busy loop) and marks timing gates inconclusive on a starved machine — this box sat at load average 400 during the work and a 20ms task took 80ms — rather than fail or pass by luck. **Two things measured and deliberately not done here:** the ~70–100ms commit at the START of every semantic move in dev is 90% `jsxDEV`/`createElement` for the whole board tree (production `jsx` is far cheaper) and its structural cure — a structurally-shared layout so unchanged subtrees keep referential identity, plus memoised layers — is real work recorded for the next perf pass; and the flight is still the V3.5 shared-clock ease rather than a van Wijk & Nuij coupled path (ρ ≈ 1.42, duration from path length, interruptible mid-flight with velocity continuity) — with today's short hops the ease reads well, and the coupled path is the upgrade for long ones. **Bench inputs for the F4/F5 design pass surfaced by this work:** unit size is board-wide, so a fleet with ANY delegation renders sparse 0.88 units while a fleet with none tiles edge-to-edge at 2.2 (Voltaic vs the 1k fixture look like different products); the ease-in from rest leaves ~30ms of sub-perceptual motion after a keypress (games favour ease-out for camera moves); mobile zone chips overlap each other and the board at 390px and Chrome's touch-target adjustment snaps a pan that starts within ~10–20px of a chip onto the chip (the pointer eval's clear-point scan now keeps a 28px margin from DOM controls; the chip density itself is a design question).
- `docs/engineering/projects/spatial-operations-board.md`

### ENG-021 Objective engine — context at every granularity

Status: active-build — E1 Session-context labels and feedback were verified 2026-07-24; E1.1 readable Session comparison and total title fallback were verified 2026-07-26; E1.2 made the gold corpus executable and E1.3 closed its first live-provider regression on 2026-08-02; later Objective Engine granularities remain planned.

Direction (operator framing): context text should answer "why does this Session exist / what was I working on / why was I doing it?" quickly enough to page the work-world back into memory. It is not a live activity ticker. A necessary subtask or direct continuation keeps the established label; a newer durable purpose that changes the primary object or outcome enough to stand as its own Initiative establishes a new work-world even inside the same app, Project, or Session; returning to an earlier work-world may restore its label. Scratchpad Sessions still receive the best current topic guess rather than a blank or a model control token.

Builds on ENG-015 S4 context paging and ENG-016 D21's durable-Session storage, but explicitly SUPERSEDES the `KEEP` / `NO_GOAL` control-token prompt and the PTY output sweep as the Session-label decision mechanism. Recap generation remains a separate "what changed while away?" concern.

Scope:

- replace W0.4's periodic, output-volume-driven local CLI subtitle guess with an authenticated, server-owned inference path triggered by submitted operator instructions (decision `0019`)
- Electron retains and persists the last good label across offline and provider failures; a new Session may temporarily show a meaningful launch instruction, and an attachment-only launch shows **New agent**, never a temporary file path
- the server uses bounded, redacted operator evidence and structured output, and does not persist inference excerpts
- authenticated product feedback for every signed-in user: fast label votes and exact-label corrections write through the general feedback intake, and **Help → Submit Feedback…** accepts broader bugs, ideas, text, and optional screenshots
- production feedback is evidence; a sanitized, versioned gold corpus in the repository remains the reproducible tuning and regression gate
- derive Session-continuity diagnostics locally from explainable source-neutral
  facts rather than an opaque health score; a future hosted control plane may
  aggregate the projection but does not own its semantics

Milestones:

- E1 Session-context labels and feedback loop (landed 2026-07-24): server-owned label inference, offline-tolerant persistence, and the reusable authenticated feedback intake.
- E1.1 Session comparison projection (landed 2026-07-26): readable shared Sessions cards, semantic state/plan copy instead of terminal transcript tails, and total **New agent** identity fallback.
- E1.2 executable label-quality gate (landed 2026-08-02): the committed corpus now scores semantic anchors, pivots, stability, and output hygiene locally, with an explicit opt-in live-provider evaluation path.
- E1.3 provider-calibrated pivot semantics (landed 2026-08-02): credentialed corpus runs closed the same-product/different-Initiative regression and made distinguishing named-entity retention explicit; the live command now loads local env directly.
- E2 Session-continuity diagnostics (planned, deliberately unshaped; evidence
  2026-08-03 show & tell): define an explainable projection over Events,
  Consumption, intervention patterns, compaction boundaries, repo-owned
  plan/progress state, and completion/review Artifacts. Research a small,
  repeatable, harness-aware continuity probe before assigning diagnostic value
  to it. A probe measures one capability at one point in a Session; it never
  certifies overall work quality.
