### ENG-008 Consumption and controls

Status: active-build — RESHAPED 2026-07-24 (operator): pulled forward from `planned` to be the near-term bridge between the shipped terminal workspace and the fleet/governance story. Nothing the workspace produces today generates the data that story depends on, and both first-party harnesses already write complete per-session usage to local disk — so honest Consumption is a read-only local parse over the existing D31 adapter seam, not a provider-billing integration. The near-term slice is E0–E3; Policy/Budget enforcement stays `planned`.

Scope:

- one source-agnostic `ConsumptionSample` behind the Agent Source boundary, implemented as pure `@exawatt/core` with no React, DOM, or Electron dependency
- the modelled units are tokens, tool calls, time, compute, and energy; raw token and tool usage is preserved before any provider-specific cost conversion
- assurance travels with every rollup: local log reads are `observed`, harness self-reports are `reported`, and nothing is `verified` without provider billing
- deliberately OUT of this slice: dollars as a headline unit, budget enforcement, spend caps, managed Workspace ceilings, and provider billing APIs
- planned, after the spine: Policy/Budget concepts; hierarchical effective policy where future managed Workspace ceilings are absolute while personal Agent/Session preferences and YOLO can only narrow, never bypass them; personal launch preferences stay provider-enforced until managed Workspace policy exists, so there is no conflicting or cosmetic Exawatt policy engine; resource-allocation and spend controls; Consumption connected to Initiative outcomes or KPI velocity where measurable
- direction, deliberately unshaped (operator brief 2026-08-03, full quote in the project doc §4): Usage as the ⌘T-style AGGREGATOR over the four vendor usage tabs the operator juggles daily — pace management is validated as the headline; reserve-aware planning and TOKENMAXXING are captured below as E9/E10; the spend-class and provider-account halves are OWNED BY ENG-038, not this item — the spine's read-only local-parse/no-credential thesis is load-bearing and is not amended by the aggregator direction
- UI: design exploration lands in `/hud-gallery/consumption-lab` (three directions — ambient meter, attribution ledger, watt-per-outcome) before any production surface. RESOLVED 2026-08-02 by E4: production placement is `/consumption`, a manifest-registered VIEW in the `app` tier reachable from ⌘K and the go chord (`g u` since E9 2026-08-11, matching the Usage name; originally `g c` — burned, no alias), composing the three lab directions into one four-act narrative. The lab's atoms and directions were EXTRACTED into `src/components/consumption/`; both surfaces render the same components over one view-model, and neither owns a private copy
- Consumption UI direction (operator, 2026-08-03 — settled, supersedes nothing above but binds everything after): (1) `/consumption` remains a DEDICATED page, rebuilt to dashboard grade, orthogonal to the Agent/Team/Fleet altitudes — never a fourth altitude; (2) an always-on minimal ambient meter (dial/bar form under selection in `/hud-gallery`) lives in app chrome as the page's first-class IA entry — ⌘K is a backstop, never the only discovery path (general IA rule, operator-stated); (3) the headline currency everywhere is plan-window headroom + reset + pace (a Claude Code `/usage`-style pacing marker), with dollars staying drill-in and explicitly modelled; (4) consumption attribution rides entities app-wide — the Team-tile readouts and the Fleet burn lens (E7) are the first two carriers; (5) all of it is demo-corpus-sourced now, live wiring stays E5; (6) page-design directions are being explored in `/hud-gallery/consumption-redesign` (meter forms likewise) with the operator picking. The ENG-036 production-voice rule applies to all consumption copy

Exit criteria:

- per-Session, per-Project, and per-day raw usage is derivable from local logs for both sources, at a Project attribution rate the operator judges truthful
- Codex plan-window burn is visible, and Claude Code's absence of a local equivalent is explicit rather than modelled, inferred, or shown as zero
- consumption reaches the operator within an interactive budget and never blocks the workspace. AMENDED 2026-07-24 from "a full local corpus scan completes within an interactive budget": measurement retired that criterion as unachievable — a cold scan of the real corpus costs 19.3s, ~617MB heap, and 2.66GB read, which no inline budget accommodates. Incremental watermarked scanning with persisted offsets plus an explicitly backgrounded, cancellable first run is therefore required scope, not an optimization
- no surface presents a dollar figure or billing claim the data cannot support

Milestones:

- E0 Source-agnostic consumption contract + local adapters (landed 2026-07-24): Claude Code session JSONL (per-message model/effort/cwd/gitBranch/input/output/cache-read/cache-write/server-tool counts), Claude Code DELEGATED subagent transcripts under `<sessionId>/subagents/`, and Codex rollout JSONL (cumulative and per-turn tokens, reasoning tokens, context window) normalize into one `ConsumptionSample`. Delegated runs are not optional: they are 37.7% of Claude samples and 18.0% of its raw tokens, so a parent-only reader under-reports Claude by nearly a quarter. Codex `total_token_usage` is cumulative per session and Claude `requestId`s span parent and subagent transcripts, so the merge is corpus-global on an idempotency key rather than per-file.
- E1 Capacity truth (landed 2026-08-10, with the E5 scanner): Codex `rate_limits` (used_percent, window_minutes, resets_at, plan_type) is a first-class PlanWindow served live from the scanner — keyed by `planWindowKey` (limitId+scope+windowMinutes; the real machine now shows TWO limitIds, so plan identity is not global and limitId alone collides across a limit's two scopes), degenerate `windowMinutes: 0` records discarded AND counted, `observedAt` surfaced so the renderer's `windowFreshness` rule (expired-not-current, stale-not-live) works unchanged, and pace OBSERVED from the corpus's own rate-limit history (%/h per bucket within one reset cycle; absent — never zero — when unobservable). Claude Code records no local equivalent and is represented as explicitly unreported: no window record exists, none is fabricated. Narrative in the project doc's milestone log.
- E2 Attribution: roll samples up Session → Project → Workspace through the existing durable-Session ↔ provider-conversation identity index and cwd-keyed Project grouping.
- E3 Normalization: model-size-weighted token totals as the compute proxy per `concepts.md`, with a visible, editable weight basis; no public per-token price is treated as truth.
- E5 Live source swap (LANDED 2026-08-10, contract-first in two parallel halves): the typed IPC contract (`@exawatt/core` `consumption/live-snapshot.ts`; `consumption:snapshot` / notification-only `consumption:updated` / `rescan` / `cancel-scan`; `window.electron.consumption`) landed first so both halves could proceed. DATA HALF: the incremental watermarked scanner the amended exit criteria made mandatory runs in Electron main — persisted per-file watermarks + samples in userData (append-ordered crash-safe log, atomic meta, compaction); first scan explicitly backgrounded and cancellable with progress; fs.watch + debounce incremental passes with stale-pull insurance; the durable-Session identity index rides the snapshot. Measured on the real corpus (grown to 3,642 files / 4.31 GB / 127,849 samples): cold 16.7 s backgrounded with 85 MB resident, warm launch 0.43 s, incremental pass 0.08 s reading 168 KB, cancel 75 ms. RENDERER HALF: the Personal tenant swapped onto the snapshot — `/usage`, the ambient meter, Team tiles, and the Fleet burn lens read this machine's corpus through one live store (revision-gated pulls with `sinceMs` bounded to the rendered seven-day window; full-corpus pulls are ~145 MB-equivalent and never issued) and the SAME view-model both demo corpora travel; E2 attribution joins main's identity index with worktree-aware registry grouping (outside-record sessions keep the E8 absent-identity pattern); E3 stays core's stated weight basis (the editable override is owed to a settings design pass); the ENG-026 readiness flipped `preview` → `live` (the one-line manifest change as designed) and the demo banner yields to one scan-state caption + a footer freshness fact. OWED to the contract, recorded in the project doc: live intervention counts (`UserPromptSubmit` via the ENG-023 channel for Claude Code; user turns in Codex rollouts — both already observed, neither yet on the snapshot) and live Codex context pressure.
- E6 Ambient chrome meter — form options (landed 2026-08-03): the minimal always-on consumption presence in the app chrome (operator-settled: headline = tightest plan-window headroom + pace vs even burn; the meter doubles as `/consumption`'s first-class chrome entry — glyph → hover popover → click-through, the iStat three-rung ladder). Four candidate ≤20px forms — arc dial, fraction bar + pace tick, glyph ring, numeral swap — state-switchable (healthy / warm / hot-ahead-of-pace / exhausted-with-reset-countdown / real demo corpus) in `/hud-gallery#ambient-consumption-meter` with zoomed specimens, the shared hover popover (windows, resets, pace, hot-only coach line), and per-form assessments; the operator picks the surviving form. Idiom: monochrome-until-a-window-runs-hot (design-system amendment log 2026-08-03), escalation by state change never motion. Wired placement: title-bar right region behind `AMBIENT_CHROME_METER_ENABLED` (`src/components/consumption/meter/ambient-meter-chrome.tsx`), rendering the real demo corpus windows at the demo instant; the form pick is the one-word `CHROME_METER_FORM` change. E5's live swap feeds this the same `ConsumptionSourceView`s.
- E8 Production composite page (landed 2026-08-03): `/consumption` rebuilt as the operational composite of the two directions the operator picked from `/hud-gallery/consumption-redesign` — Console's top (plan-window pace cards + the headroom-over-time chart) over the Ops board's floor (the all-sessions entity grid) — answering, top to bottom: am I OK? → where is it going? → what should I change? Attribution pivot bars and session-grid rows are doors into one shared drill panel holding the page's only dollars, labelled modelled; ratio diagnostics + the ENG-026 N2 intervention rate compress to one quiet tile row; ENG-014 allocation survives as a Coming-soon chip. Both corpora flow through the same view-model behind the ENG-027 tenant gate, and provider sessions outside the fleet record render with measured figures and honestly absent identity (absent, never zero). The display name lives once in `CONSUMPTION_SURFACE_NAME` (`packages/core/src/surface-names.ts` since D57, so the Electron Go menu reads it too instead of mirroring it). RENAMED same day: the naming research confirmed "Usage" (the category's dominant label) and the operator agreed — display name **Usage**, route **`/usage`**, with NO redirect from `/consumption` (operator: hard cut, the old path 404s; every internal reference — nav manifest, ⌘K labels, Electron Go menu, ambient-meter click-through and popover, tenant-gate route set, proxy prefixes, tests — was swept to `/usage`). Internal ids keep historical spellings (surface id `consumption`, `go-consumption`, `src/components/consumption/`); the canonical CONCEPT stays Consumption (`concepts.md` unchanged) and the wattage brand lives inside the page, never in the nav label. The E6 ambient meter ships enabled in chrome with the operator-picked fraction-bar form (`AMBIENT_CHROME_METER_ENABLED` / `CHROME_METER_FORM: 'bar'`). SUPERSEDES E4's four-act narrative — see the Amendment chain; full narrative in the project doc's milestone log.
- Usage-loop review fixes (landed 2026-08-03): consolidated code+UX review closure — ONE tenant-aware consumption seam (`useTenantConsumption`) so the chrome meter and `/usage` can never disagree; ONE pace derivation/band/vocabulary owned by `meter-model` (±5, "even pace", the verb "spent"); the page adopts the meter's window-freshness discipline; the normalized tooltip prints core's own basis and the stale weight table left production; strip/scope-readout blocked semantics unified (error folds into blocked); `FleetAgentView.rawTokens` optionality restored; meter popover portals out of the backdrop-filter header and closes on click-through; readiness markers render no roadmap IDs; raw mode sorts/scales by raw; live markers leave the FLUX channel. Full narrative in the project doc's milestone log.
- Usage hierarchy pass (landed 2026-08-03): `/usage` restructured from the 2026-08-03 show-and-tell feedback (operator: "looks like an engineer built it"; guest: "with all the different text treatments… no idea what the key takeaway is") — a six-role treatment budget enforced as components (~30 distinct text treatments → 6; color is data-state only, chrome neutral), one glance zone (tightest window: display %, reset, pace verdict in plain words), and the page's bands now literally the operator's five questions in order: Headroom · Burn · Pace · Heat · Spend, with the attribution/session drill floor below. The drill header answers "how many tokens did that use" as a display numeral, and single-session drills add context-window pressure (Codex: peak % of window + compactions, newly authored in both fixture corpora; Claude Code: "not recorded" — absent, never zero; the live read is owed to E5). Full narrative + verbatim feedback citations in the project doc's milestone log.
- Study retirement (landed 2026-08-03, with ENG-036): the workbench rule applied to the shipped consumption loop — `/hud-gallery/consumption-lab` (with its frozen `weights.ts`), `/hud-gallery/consumption-redesign`, and the four-form ambient-meter gallery study deleted; `unit-ladder`, `assurance-legend`, `capacity`, `coverage`, `delegation`, and `model.ts`'s `tightestWindow`/`reportingCoverage` retired as importer-verified dead (the E5 fold-in note pulled forward). The design record is git history, the review screenshots, and the E6/E8 milestone-log entries; the live meter, the shared atoms `/usage` mounts, and the readiness-grammar study are untouched. Full inventory in the project doc's log.
- E9 Pace opportunity — tokenmaxxing readout (LANDED 2026-08-11 on live data; design options landed 2026-08-10, operator pick recorded 2026-08-11: the **C + B combination** — Direction C metric swap + coach everywhere, plus Direction B expiry geometry on the `/usage` pace bars only; B stays off the popover where the study proved a 4px region unreadable, Direction A unshipped): the trigger predicate (healthy|warm ∧ behind ∧ floor ≥ 15 pts ∧ reset ≥ 30m; closing at floor ≥ 30 pts ∨ reset ≤ ¼ window; floor = even-pace% − used%, burn-noise-free; the named standing false positive stays deliberate idle) promoted from the gallery into `meter-model` (`opportunityOf`), joining THE one pace derivation so the chrome meter and `/usage` inherit one verdict and one vocabulary: `paceSentence`/`paceLabel` re-frame a firing window as free-to-spend ("72% free · expires in 9h" / "72% free to spend"), one closing-tier coach line shares the hot line's slot behind the single arbiter `opportunityCoach` (HOT ALWAYS OUTRANKS — any hot/spent window silences it), and the expired state keeps a one-line ledger caption on `/usage` only, derived from the previous cycle's final observation near its reset (three gates: young current cycle ≤ ¼ window, observation within slack of the close, unused ≥ the 15-pt floor — the ledger claims only what was observed, never an extrapolation). The `/usage` pace bars additionally draw the will-expire-unused region (fill → projection hatch → +45° neutral expiry hatch to the ceiling + boundary tick); the third hatch meaning is recorded in the design system with its density widened 5→7px so sparseness separates it from both −45° textures at 6px bar heights. The go chord moved `g c` → `g u` with the Usage name (id `go-consumption` unchanged — ids are addresses; old chord burned, no alias). Verified headful on this machine's real corpus: the real weekly (4% used, 8 pts behind, resets 6d 3h) sits under the 15-pt floor and the voice is correctly SILENT, while the ledger truthfully reports the prior weekly cycle closed ~100% unused; firing states verified via the gallery fixtures, which now render the production predicate (study retained — subject only partly shipped, Direction A remains reviewable). Full narrative in the project doc's milestone log.
- Usage honesty repair (landed 2026-08-14, from the E12 design pass's audit of our own surface): five verified defects found by driving the real Electron app on the operator's real corpus. **The severe one inverted the page's honesty**: with the Claude plan read off, `/usage` headlined `Codex · 5% used · 95% free to spend` while an unreadable Claude account had burned 208M raw tokens in five hours — a source that could not be read made the verdict BETTER, and for a multiplexer over N vendors that scales the wrong way (the more of it is broken, the calmer it looks). Root cause for three of the five was one dropped IPC field: `providerPlanAccounts` crossed the E5 bridge and `live-store.buildState()` never read it. (1) `planReadState` now splits the single absent presentation into `reported`/`none`/`off`/`unreadable`, so a credential failure stops wearing the harness's capability sentence; `MeterReading.fleetUnknown` rides the one shared derivation and `opportunityOf` returns null on it, silencing the whole E9 opportunity voice — metric swap, expiry geometry, and coach line — whenever any sibling ledger is unreadable; Headroom leads with a partial-verdict caption naming what it cannot see, and the meter's aria label says the same. (2) The operator's real `$201.60 of $200` plan credits render in Spend in their own lane with their own `plan credits · vendor-reported` basis, never summed with modelled dollars. (3) The session grid stopped reading the raw harness title and routes through `sessionDisplayCopy` like the Team tiles — 14 of 14 rows that read "Claude Code" now carry 14 distinct real names. The two neighbouring symptoms were genuinely absent data, so they are stated rather than blank: the `INT` column's legend says `Interventions: not recorded`, and `pivotAbsenceNote` explains the Roadmap pivot's single `Not attributed` row (it also gives the empty-corpus Attribution band the empty state it lacked). (4) Account-scoped windows are labelled by their account everywhere, with the ENG-038 plan-wide disclosure moved onto the page. (5) The Fleet burn lens was measured dead on the real board and is a DOC defect, not a code one — see the E7 line and the Amendment chain. Verified on the operator's real corpus in an isolated `EXAWATT_USER_DATA` (his running app untouched), with the failed-read state reproduced through his own privacy switch. Deliberately NOT touched: the page's structure, band order, and visual language — the E12 design pass owns those. Narrative, before/after evidence, and the four pinned failure modes in the project doc's milestone log.
- E10 Reserve line (planned, deliberately unshaped): "enough tokens for the thing I know I need to work on tomorrow" — a user-declared target against the existing projection. The design pass decides whether this is a display-only line on the headroom chart (cheap, honest, no enforcement) or the first personal Budget; it must not become enforcement by accident, since ENG-008 parked enforcement deliberately. Touches ENG-017 (the queue knows tomorrow's work) and ENG-014.
- E11 Multi-provider local spend (open item, opened 2026-08-03; follows ENG-003 S2 and the D46 launch path): a Session run through `opencode` on OpenRouter or a comparable provider is billed in real dollars, which would be the first MEASURED rung on this item's unit ladder rather than a modelled one — and a locally-run model produces a truthful `$0` with watts as the honest unit. Reads the source's OWN local record (`opencode stats`), keeping the spine's read-only/no-credential/no-network thesis intact; a credentialed vendor read is ENG-038's source class, not this one. Operator sequencing: land the launch path first
- E12 Usage as the vendor multiplexer (planned, DESIGN OPTIONS LANDED 2026-08-14 — awaiting the operator's pick): three directions live at `/hud-gallery/usage-directions`, each rendering SIX states over one real read-only capture of this machine (the shipped scanner over `~/.claude` + `~/.codex`, the real ENG-038 Claude account read, and his app's own 516-point plan-window history for real burn rates), deep-linked `?d=roster|ledger|instrument&s=<state>`. The architecture the four research lanes settled is enforced once in the study's shared model, so the directions differ in EXPRESSION only: headline (bound-framed, with coverage) → needs-attention (only when non-empty) → the full source roster → per-source detail; the glance is a projection of the detail, never a second computation; `unreadable` (a malfunction with a repair verb) is a different row state from `unavailable` (a settled fact, product language, no alarm colour); residuals are named drawn rows. The load-bearing rule is stronger than absent-never-zero and is structural rather than a review convention — **losing information must never move the headline in the reassuring direction** — enforced by a FIXED roster (the coverage denominator can never shrink to what succeeded), by keeping a failed read's last good windows at their true `observedAt`, and by applying the lower-bound prefix exactly when the total is knowably incomplete; 22 unit checks pin it. Measured above the fold against the audit baseline (133 text nodes / 34 numerals / 2,148 px): A · Roster 91/23/1,285 px, B · Ledger 77/17/1,555 px, C · Instrument **32/13/1,030 px**. Both audit questions are answered in the first block in all three — "am I fine right now?" as a verdict word (under 1 s vs 8–12 s and ambiguous) and "what runs out first?" in the clause after it (~3 s vs 20–30 s), because the model orders by time-to-exhaustion instead of percent-consumed. **Recommendation: C's first screen over A's roster with B as the drill** — three altitudes of one architecture, not three alternatives; the roster must stay ON the page rather than behind a button, since it is the only place a source that reported nothing can be seen. C's read-history strip (a Grafana state timeline per source over the real observation series, reading Claude Max 86% read vs Codex Pro 14% read on this machine) is the one thing in the study that nothing in the 170-shot corpus does, and it renders the vendor lane's "Codex windows are a passive echo" finding. One of the six states is AUTHORED and says so on screen: three days of recorded plan history never put the Fable weekly below 96%, so the state in which the page may say "you are fine" is not reachable from his own data. Findings the pass could not use as stated, and the full per-direction assessment, are in the project doc's E12 log. Original brief, verbatim — "I want our usage page to essentially be a multiplexer on top of all those things and a single unified place to look. That's super easy with really clear information architecture, and glanceability and deep dive ability." The named surfaces are the ones they otherwise juggle in browser tabs: Claude (claude.ai plan usage), Claude Console, OpenAI platform billing, ChatGPT/Codex analytics, xAI/Grok, Gemini. This is the UX half of the aggregator thesis §4 recorded on 2026-08-03; ENG-038 owns the credentialed source class that feeds it, E11 owns locally-recorded provider spend, and this milestone owns the SURFACE: one honest unified picture across sources measured in different units, with a two-second answer and a twenty-minute drill in the same page. The design pass ran four research lanes (vendor-native surfaces + programmatic feasibility, aggregator/single-pane products, glanceability-and-depth craft, and an audit of our own current surface); its audit half also drove the surgical `/usage` honesty repair landed the same day. Constraints already settled and not reopened by this pass: absent-never-zero for unreadable sources, the assurance ladder (observed/reported/verified), plan-window headroom + reset + pace as the headline currency, the six-role treatment system, and the Voice rule. An `XAI_API_KEY` for the Full Vibe AI org is now in the repo env and on Vercel (all three environments), so xAI is the first vendor whose programmatic usage read can be verified rather than assumed.
- E4 Expository Consumption surface (landed 2026-08-02, presentation superseded 2026-08-03 by E8 — the spine, view-model, demo sources, and readiness reconciliation below all stand): `/consumption` — one scrollable four-act narrative (capacity → attribution → cost per outcome → the unbuilt allocation lever) spined by a four-rung unit ladder that draws an explicit line where measurement stops and modelling begins. Demo Mode emits real `ConsumptionSample`s and rolls them up with core's own `rollupBy*`, so no shape is unique to the demo. The lab's atoms and directions were extracted into `src/components/consumption/` and both surfaces consume them. Full narrative in the project doc's milestone log. RECONCILIATION RESOLVED 2026-08-02 by ENG-026 N0/N1: the `Unbuilt` treatment folded into the shared readiness components (`src/components/readiness/`, tag now the app-wide **Coming soon**), `/consumption` is registered `preview` in the manifest, and the intervention-rate metric renders in the narrative — no N2 obligation remains open ahead of E5. Original contract, for reference: the spine exists only as a package and a fixture workbench, so nothing a user or investor can look at. E4 makes Consumption a real in-app surface whose job is to EXPLAIN — ahead of demos, per the operator brief `research/operator-briefs/2026-08-02-future-of-exawatt-brief.md` ("show surfaces and affordances in the UI, even if they're not yet built"; explanatory work is the highest near-term priority). Demo-sourced through the same UI-model contracts Live would use, so it is not throwaway. It is a VIEW reachable from the navigation manifest and `⌘K`, not a fourth command altitude — Agent → Team → Fleet stays intact. Scope: compose the three `/hud-gallery/consumption-lab` directions into one argument (capacity → attribution → cost per outcome) rather than three peers behind a toggle; carry the unit ladder the brief asks for — tokens, then normalized compute, then dollars and watts — with each rung's assurance and modelling stated in place, since only the first is measured; answer the two user questions the brief records ("how are you tracking cost per agent?", cost per outcome); and render the far-future allocation lever (ENG-014) as a visibly UNBUILT affordance rather than a working control. Deliberately NOT in scope: incremental scanning, live wiring, budget enforcement, and any real dollar figure presented as fact.
- E7 Attribution rides every entity (landed 2026-08-03; **HALF RETIRED the same day** — corrected 2026-08-14 from the `/usage` audit): per-Session consumption readouts on the Team exposé tiles, over ONE shared per-agent burn derivation (`demoAgentBurn` → optional `AgentMetrics.rawTokens`/`normalizedTokens` → ui-model `computeAgentBurn`/`selectFleetBurn`; unreported stays absent, never zero — live local Sessions therefore show no readout until E5). The Team tile carrier is live. **The Fleet burn lens this line used to claim is NOT in production and has not been since 2026-08-03**: the ENG-004 board pass (`bc43842`) removed the `?lens=` URL param, the Status/Burn segmented control, and the `lens` prop pass-through at the operator's own direction — see the Amendment chain. `OperationsBoardSurface` still ACCEPTS a `lens` prop and `/eval/t5-operations-board` still drives it, so the derivation stays reviewable and undeleted; the production board is `data-board-lens="status"` with no control and no `B` key (re-measured on the real board 2026-08-14). Narrative and screenshots in the project doc's milestone log.

### ENG-025 Feedback capture and reinflation loop

Status: active-build — F1, F2, and F2.1 landed 2026-07-27 and closed the operator loop. **Reopened 2026-08-06:** F3's stated precondition ("once non-operator rows exist") is being met by ENG-030 OS0, so the lane split becomes executable rather than unshaped. The no-cron decision from 2026-07-27 is untouched — scheduled automation stays out. The drain protocol remains live operations, not build work: roadmap questions run `/triage-feedback` first (AGENTS.md).

Direction (operator framing): submission must be cheap enough that the operator files everything — votes, bugs, ideas, huge feature kernels — the moment they occur, and the queue must drain somewhere canonical instead of accumulating in a table nobody reads. Reinflation is the deliberate second step where a drained row becomes canonical repo state at the right altitude: evidence on active work, an incident candidate, a gold-corpus candidate, or a new deliberately-unshaped roadmap kernel awaiting its own design pass. Submission never implies verbatim execution — insertion into the backlog is automatic, execution never is.

Builds on ENG-021 E1's authenticated feedback intake, which remains the single write path; the tab label vote stream and Help modal are kept, not forked.

Scope:

- F1 quick capture: a global shortcut and ⌘K verbs open a small overlay capture bar over the existing intake — one text field, kind toggles, Enter submits, Esc dismisses, a pre-captured window screenshot attachable with one key; the Help modal remains the long-form path; no new backend surface
- F2 reinflation: `triaged_at`/`triage_ref` triage state on `product_feedback` (service-role writes only), a drain script (`pnpm feedback:triage`), and a `/triage-feedback` repo command whose protocol classifies every untriaged row into canonical repo state and marks it with the integrating commit
- classification targets: new unshaped ENG kernel, evidence on an owning ENG item's project doc, incident candidate per the incident contract, context-label gold-corpus candidate
- operator-only authority for now: the triage agent auto-writes canonical docs and pushes; big kernels always land deliberately unshaped pending a design pass
- drain triggers (AMENDED 2026-07-27, operator): no cron or scheduled triage infrastructure yet. The drain rides roadmap moments — any "what's next" / sequencing question runs the `/triage-feedback` protocol first (contract in `AGENTS.md`) — plus explicit "drain the inbox" requests. Queue pressure is visible in-product: roadmap surfaces show the operator's untriaged feedback count instead of injecting it into every agent session
- F3 (SHAPED 2026-08-06, operator; scheduled automation remains out of scope and deliberately unshaped): two lanes over one intake. The **operator lane** is unchanged — every ⌘⇧F row the operator files continues to flow into the triage-to-canon queue exactly as F2 built it. A **suggestions lane** holds every non-operator row: read and considered, never auto-promoted to canon. Lane membership is derived server-side from the submitting account, never a client-supplied field, so a user row cannot forge its way into the canon queue; triage columns stay service-role-only as F2 established. The submitter's experience is deliberately unchanged and deliberately minimal — the operator's position (2026-08-06) is that no reply or acknowledgement loop is owed, **provided pressing Enter shows a clear success**, which the existing `sent` confirmation already does once the capture bar can open at all
- F3.1 (DEFECT, found 2026-08-06): the untriaged-count line F2.1 added reads under RLS, so it counts only the _signed-in user's own_ rows — the operator's inbox pressure will silently under-report the moment anyone else files. The same line also renders for every signed-in user, showing a non-operator an operator-only triage concept they cannot act on, which violates the production-voice rule. Scope the count to the operator lane and render it only for operators
- F4 (added 2026-08-03 by ENG-017 S13.2): the triage protocol gains a second write target — a small fix or incident candidate writes a short **backlog entry into the owning repo's roadmap file** (title, owning item, provenance) in addition to its diagnostic narrative in the project doc. The entry is the machine-readable pointer that makes a defect visible to the roadmap lens; the project-doc note stays the reasoning. Today's triaged rows are prose only and therefore invisible to every UI. Depends on the convention/parser gaining a `backlog` status distinct from `later` — see ENG-017 S13.2

Exit criteria:

- from anywhere in the app while signed in, a thought becomes a submitted feedback row in under five seconds without touching the mouse
- `/triage-feedback` drains every untriaged operator row into canonical repo state exactly once, and the mark survives re-runs
- the tab label vote stream accretes as evidence against ENG-021 rather than sitting unread in the table
- the triage step has authority to enqueue work, never to execute it

Milestones:

- F1 Quick capture (landed 2026-07-27): ⌘⇧F and ⌘K-verb one-field capture bar over the E1 intake, with pre-captured screenshots and Project/Session attribution.
- F2 Reinflation loop (landed 2026-07-27): service-role triage state, `pnpm feedback:triage` drain script, and the `/triage-feedback` classification protocol; migration applied to production.
- F2.1 Queue visibility and drain contract (landed 2026-07-27): untriaged-count line on the roadmap rail (Terminal ⌘B and Sessions home), refreshed on submit; `AGENTS.md` gains the roadmap-questions-drain-first rule.
- F1.1 Capture defect fixes (landed 2026-08-03): opaque capture modal, ⌘⇧F summons from terminal focus (the pane now claims only chords it implements), and app-version stamping on every intake payload — feedback row `9ea6098d`, details in the project doc's queued-fixes section.
- F1.2 Screenshot-chip label (landed 2026-08-03): the quick-capture screenshot toggle gains a visible `Screenshot` label beside its thumbnail and ⌘S hint, matching the kind-chip grammar while retaining its accessible name — feedback row `486a87e1`.
- F5 Anonymized diagnostics (2026-08-14, with ENG-030 OS1.6): a bug report can carry machine state instead of a user's recollection of it. Bug in ⌘⇧F gains an `Anonymized diagnostics` chip on ⌘D beside F1.2's screenshot chip, with a review affordance that shows the exact JSON before it is sent. The bundle is build/system facts plus bounded tails of the `updater`, `auth`, and `summarizer` logs; never transcript, prompt, Project, or file content. Because ⌘⇧F is a no-op signed out and a broken install is disproportionately signed out, Settings → Privacy → Diagnostics saves the same report to a file and reveals it in Finder with no account and no network. Redaction moves to write time (see F5.1) so the logs on disk are safe before anything reads them.
- F5.1 Shared log redaction (2026-08-14, architecture): `auth-diagnostics.ts` sanitizes bearer tokens, JWTs, and long opaque values; `diagnostics-log.ts` states in its own header that it is a sibling of that module but only clips to 400 chars, so `summarizer.jsonl` and `updater.jsonl` have always been written unredacted. Extract the sanitizer into `diagnostics-redaction.ts`, have both writers use it, and add home-directory anonymization so a path reads `~/...`. This is a correctness fix independent of F5: it is why F5 can attach a log tail without a bespoke scrubbing pass at send time.

Non-goal, stated on purpose: **no anonymous network intake.** The obvious
completion of F5 is a signed-out send endpoint, and it is deliberately not
built. An unauthenticated write path into production Supabase needs its own
decision, an abuse ceiling, and a rate-limit story; it is not a side effect of
a feedback affordance. The saved file solves the actual support case with no
new attack surface. Revisit only with a decision record.

### ENG-036 Design system of record and design authority

Status: active-build — created 2026-08-02. This item exists because the operator named the problem and said he has no solution for it:

> "It's generally the case that the best coding models and harnesses today (Claude Code and Codex) suck at UX design. In particular, they're bad at translating my vision and goals and feature requirements into a cohesive, highly usable, easily navigable user experience. I don't have a solution for this yet. A senior designer reviewing our app would find tons of examples, big and small, of where the UI and IA fails our users and me."

Diagnosis: the repo has a design _workbench_ (`/hud-gallery`) and a strong record of design _decisions_ (D30 status iconography, D32 one button system, D33 calm attention, D40 status-light protocol, `0022` ribbon, `0007` spatial restraint), but it has no design **system of record**. Accepted taste is scattered across two dozen milestone lines and decision records, so every agent re-derives it from scratch and taste never accumulates. The fix is not better prompting; it is making design decisions durable, findable state the same way architecture decisions already are.

Concrete evidence of the gap, measured 2026-08-02: the app uses **17 distinct hardcoded pixel font sizes** (`text-[8px]` through `text-[22px]`) alongside Tailwind's `text-xs`/`sm`/`base` scale. No document says which is correct where, so an agent building a new surface picks by copying whatever file it happened to open, and drift compounds every time.

A named, reproduced system-level defect from an external user (a partner
conversation): **per-Project identity color and
per-Agent status color are drawn from overlapping hues on the same surface, and
the status reading wins.** A non-engineer partner reported it unprompted at the
Team altitude, and his account names the failure mode precisely: "the problem
with the red, yellows, and blues… is my first thought is stop [and] ready… it
feels like a status thing. So I kept on looking for like thinking, oh,
everything in the yellow is waiting. Even though… even when I knew it wasn't, I
just like instinctively [thought] yellow means I need something." He then
produced two mutually contradictory readings of the same palette inside one
sentence, which is the diagnosis — the mapping is not learnable because it
competes with a traffic-light prior every user already has. The operator's own
coping strategy is the corroboration: "I've instinctively learned to look at the
icons," which is precisely the rote learning a design system exists to make
unnecessary. Two smaller findings ride along: the status marks are
undifferentiated in size ("they're currently the same size. You've got a circle
on one side and a circle on the other… even a size, a color change might help.
Sorry — size hierarchy shift"), and Project tiles identified only by color are
memorization work the operator also wants replaced by the artifact built there.
This is a defect for the system of record to adjudicate against D30/D40, not a
loose UI complaint, because color is exactly the kind of accepted taste this
item exists to make durable.

Operator direction (2026-08-02): ship a **kernel first and build the habit early** of adhering to it and deliberately modifying it. The system must be **agentically malleable** — agents read it before building and amend it when they deliberately improve it, the same way decision records work.

Human design capacity enters the item, 2026-08-13 (a partner
conversation). The founding problem above — "the best
coding models and harnesses today suck at UX design… I don't have a solution
for this yet" — gets its first concrete answer: a senior product designer
engaged part-time (8–15 hrs/week, flexible week to week), scoped to one product
at a time. Three things this changes for agents working here, none of which
alter the item's scope:

- **The system of record is now also a handoff surface.** A human designer's
  output has to land as citable rungs and amendments in
  `docs/engineering/design-system.md`, not as loose comps, or taste stops
  accumulating exactly the way this item exists to prevent.
- **Options before comps.** The agreed working style is early options for
  directional alignment rather than one finished design, at the operator's own
  request: "Early options to get feedback. I'm very opinionated sometimes, and
  I can be kind of stubborn on some things too." `/hud-gallery` is the natural
  place options get rendered against real state for that call.
- **The bar, stated plainly:** "everything's getting, like, a B-, and, you
  know, where possible, I want to upgrade that to an A-, or A+." Read alongside
  the operator's diagnosis from the acquisition side — AI-generated design is
  intrinsically competent but uniform ("everyone's getting the same design
  aesthetic"), and coding agents produce UI that "feels like it's kind of like
  a pedantic, bearded engineer building UI." Differentiation, not just
  correctness, is what this item is buying.

The two surfaces named for that capacity are owned elsewhere and are not scope
here: ENG-016 D54 (the New Agent flow) and ENG-031 (homepage positioning).

The engagement starts, and four things it establishes are canon here
(2026-08-17, a partner conversation).

- **How design work reaches the product, in the operator's own practice.**
  There is no staging environment — "I don't have, like, a staging or
  development environment. I just have production" — and design work is not
  branched: `/v2` is "not even a branch, it's just like literally the
  production code, but it's just kind of hidden a little bit from the world."
  The designer is offered the same seam by name, "slash v3 or slash eve", and
  the ask on an agent is one sentence in the prompt. Her own method — parallel
  worktrees driven by different harnesses so versions can be compared live —
  was accepted without hesitation, with one condition, and the condition is
  the durable half: "as long as you feel like nothing's getting lost in
  translation… you're able to communicate very well through the output of
  those things." For static comps the transfer mechanism is named too:
  screenshots fed to a coding agent "and then build a self-checking mechanism
  so that the coding agent can check the spec against its work."
- **Visual design authority passes to the design partner, subject to operator
  approval.** Asked on the call whether he is "not too married to this whole
  system", the operator answered "Correct." Asked afterwards how far that goes —
  amend rungs, or replace the kernel's visual direction outright — OPERATOR
  ANSWER 2026-08-17: **"Yes fully up to her (with my approval). I kind of hate
  our visual design system right now, especially the (non) organization of the
  canon units."** Three consequences, and they are the item's operating terms
  from here:
  - a design-partner proposal that replaces accepted visual direction is IN
    scope, not a deviation to be argued down. An agent citing a rung against one
    is citing what is accepted today, not a position the operator holds.
  - approval is still his, and it is the only gate. Delegated authority is not
    a bypass of review, and it does not extend to the boundaries other items own
    (decision `0007`'s spatial restraint, ENG-032's theming contract, D40's
    status protocol).
  - **the organization is the named defect, not the taste.** He did not say the
    type scale or the palette is wrong; he said the canon units are not
    organized. That is G2's job — component contracts and extracted IA
    principles — and it now has an operator complaint behind it rather than an
    inferred need. `BUG-061` is the same defect where a reader meets it first.
- **The gallery is failing the job this item gave it**, in the operator's own
  reading, live, top of page: "what is the first thing here? It's the quick
  capture chip row… I don't even know why this is here… pace, opportunity,
  voice. What the hell is this pace? Operator's verbatim shape. I don't even
  know what this means, why this is here. So it started good, but then it got
  really disorganized." His account of the correct shape is the specification:
  "it would start with, like, simple, you know, primitives, and then build up
  to slightly more complex primitives, and it would be, like, really clear how
  to use it, and where to use it, and where to go for things. I'm lacking all
  of that." G1 retired a quick-capture study on 2026-08-02 and a new one
  arrived on 2026-08-15 in the same first position, which says that
  reconciliation was an event and not a rule. `BUG-061` carries the row.
- **An aesthetic direction handed to coding agents degraded their output.**
  The operator's own diagnosis of why he pulled back from the neon game-UI
  look: "I had communicated this to my coding agents, I was like, yeah, I want
  it to look like this, and then they started doing like a lot of like really
  geeky looking things… it seemed like the visual direction was kind of
  confusing my agents a little bit and getting them stuck in like directions
  that only looked cool and weren't necessarily useful." This is direct
  evidence for why this item wants taste written as citable rules rather than
  as a mood: a style reference is an instruction agents over-apply, and a rung
  is one they can check themselves against. The aesthetic conclusion itself
  belongs to ENG-004, which carries it.

`/hud-gallery/design-canon` (landed 2026-08-17, recorded in
`design-system.md`'s gallery table) was built in answer to a request made live
on this call — "can you take a look at all of our canon and build a page on
the gallery that explains it to my designer" — and shipped thirteen minutes
after he said it. It renders canon and owns nothing; when canon moves,
`canon.ts` follows.

Scope:

- a design system of record in the repo: tokens, type scale, spacing and density, color roles, motion vocabulary, status/iconography (D40's protocol is already canonical), component contracts, and the IA principles currently scattered across decisions
- **extract from the shipped product, do not invent** — the app already carries accepted taste that survived operator review; the system's first job is to write down what is already true and correct
- **absorb `/hud-gallery`** (operator, 2026-08-02): the gallery has been serving as the de facto design system. It merges into, and is partly retired by, the canonical system — the written system becomes the source of truth and the gallery survives only as the live workbench that renders it. `AGENTS.md`'s "canonical component workbench" rule is amended when the kernel lands; do not leave two competing sources of design truth
- a design review gate on UI changes: first a checklist an agent must satisfy with screenshot evidence before claiming a surface is done, later ENG-028 T3's Designer Type as the automated reviewer the operator described wanting
- screenshot-and-iterate as a standing verification requirement, not an optional nicety — compiling is not looking right
- a recurring IA audit cadence; the 2026-07 nav/IA audit found real P0s and that practice should not have been a one-off
- boundary — **descriptive first, prescriptive second**: the system records what is accepted, and a change either adheres to it or deliberately improves it and updates it. Deliberate improvement is the point; silent divergence is the failure
- boundary — this item does not redesign the product. ENG-032 owns the new visual identity and theming; this item owns the substrate both depend on

Exit criteria:

- an agent that has never seen the app can build a new surface that matches the product without operator correction on basics
- every accepted design decision is findable in one place instead of archaeologically recoverable from milestone prose
- operator review time goes to taste and judgment, not to spacing, states, and iconography
- a UI change cannot be reported as done without visual evidence
- exactly one source of design truth exists: `AGENTS.md` points at the system, and `/hud-gallery`'s role is restated as the workbench that renders it rather than a parallel canon

Milestones:

- G0 Kernel (landed 2026-08-02): `docs/engineering/design-system.md` — a 12-rung named type scale over the D39 chrome roles (drift re-measured at 23 pixel sizes, off-scale usages registered), spacing steps and density tiers, the four scoped color palettes with the channel-ownership rule, D40 status iconography cited as canon, the amendment rule, and the `/hud-gallery` merge/retire decision list for G1. Docs only; no component changed.
- G1 Gallery reconciliation (landed 2026-08-02): G0's decision list executed — quick-capture, context-label, and keyswitch/tactile studies retired (+301 lines of dead `.tactile-key` CSS out of `globals.css`), `/hud-gallery/agent-field` and `/hud-gallery/agent-sources` deleted, kept sections and labs indexed; `AGENTS.md` workbench rule amended to name the kernel as the source of design truth.
- G2 Full system: component contracts, IA principles extracted from the existing decision records, and the recurring audit cadence.
- G3 Review gate: the checklist-with-visual-evidence gate, later automated as ENG-028 T3's Designer Type.
- G3 partial — demo-path polish (landed 2026-08-02; demo-arc packet P8): the kernel applied, with before/after screenshot evidence, to exactly the surfaces the demo walk shows — app chrome (title bar, account menu, Workspace switcher), the Agent-altitude chrome around the terminal (⌘T opener, launch controls, restore panel, terminal overlays), the Team overlay (tiles, roadmap rail + cards), the Fleet altitude's DOM layer, the ⌘K palette, and Settings' first screen. All bracketed px font sizes on those surfaces migrated to named rungs; 17px→`text-base`, 8/9px words→`text-chrome-micro`, glyph/ordinal 9px→`text-chrome-nano`, the roadmap card's fractional 11.5px snapped to label/meta. Deferred with owners and one flagged kernel contradiction (deliberate Exo 2 tile copy) recorded in the project doc's milestone log.

Sequencing: first in the current front; G0 only, then the demo surfaces proceed against it while G1–G3 continue. Every demo surface is judged against it, ENG-032's theming needs its tokens, and ENG-030's contributor review gate needs it to exist before outside contributors arrive.

System of record:

- `docs/engineering/design-system.md` (the citable kernel; G0)

### ENG-026 Vision-complete information architecture

Status: active-build — created 2026-08-02 from the operator brief and its same-day design pass. This is the explanatory arc the near-term demos depend on; the operator ranked it the highest-priority work. N0 and N1 landed 2026-08-02 together with the N2 remainder (manifest readiness on `/consumption` and the intervention-rate metric); N3–N5 landed the same day with ENG-028 T1 and ENG-029 C1 supplying their content — every milestone of this item is now landed, and the remaining work (source swaps that flip readiness `live`) belongs to the owning capability items.

Direction (operator framing): "Show surfaces and affordances in the UI, even if they're not yet built... I want to see the app improve and expand as I use it." Now canonical as vision principle 8. The marking is subtle but clear — muted affordance plus tooltip for small things, a designed navigable preview page for anything whose picture is worth a thousand words.

Central architectural decision: **readiness is a property of the data source, not of the page.** A preview surface is the real page over a demo source with an honest marker; shipping the capability swaps the source and flips readiness. This collapses Demo Mode, the Demo Workspace (ENG-027), and forthcoming surfaces into one mechanism instead of three, and forbids throwaway mock pages.

Scope:

- a three-state readiness vocabulary carried in the navigation manifest — `live`, `preview` (navigable designed page, representative data, one **Coming soon** marker), `announced` (muted affordance and tooltip, no page) — rendered by one shared component set
- the command-altitude spine stays exactly Agent → Team → Fleet; vision surfaces join the `app` tier with ⌘K, Go-menu, and one contextual on-surface entry point each
- preview surfaces for Organization, Cloud, Coordination, and Agent Types; `announced` affordances for per-Agent _Push to cloud_ and the Agent Type chip
- Consumption's production placement, shaped here and executed as ENG-008 E4/E5
- the four recurring user questions from the brief each get an on-screen answer, as this item's acceptance test: cost per agent (Consumption), handoff between agents (Coordination preview), manual-intervention gaps (a real intervention-rate metric derived from the ENG-023 harness event channel), multiplayer (Team preview plus the ENG-027 Workspace switcher)
- **Coming soon** becomes the app-wide token, superseding ENG-003's provisional `Coming later` wording without changing its underlying requirement
- boundary — truthfulness is not relaxed for demo value: no preview surface presents representative data as the operator's own, claims a live connection, simulates a successful action, writes user data, or mutates harness state
- boundary — copy stays minimal; the design carries the explanation, not pasted prose
- not scope: implementing any of the previewed capabilities; each keeps its own roadmap item and sequence

Exit criteria:

- the complete intended IA is reachable from the real app, and every unbuilt surface is visibly unbuilt without looking broken, disabled-by-error, or unavailable
- readiness is manifest data: shipping a capability is a one-line manifest change plus a source swap, with no page rewrite
- each of the four user questions can be answered on screen in a demo without leaving the app
- no `live` surface anywhere presents a number the data cannot support

Milestones:

- N0 Readiness grammar (landed 2026-08-02): `readiness` in the navigation manifest; the shared **Coming soon** marker, `announced` chip, and `Unbuilt` block in `src/components/readiness/`, prototyped in `/hud-gallery`; ENG-008 E4's local `designed, not built` treatment folded in — one vocabulary survives.
- N1 Surface map (landed 2026-08-02): Organization, Cloud, Coordination, and Agent Types registered as navigable `preview` shells with ⌘K and Go-menu entry points; per-surface contextual anchors deliberately ride N3–N5 with each surface's real content. Nothing in the spine links into a broken state (spine readiness is test-enforced `live`).
- N2 Consumption surface: executed as ENG-008 E4 (demo-sourced preview) then E5 (live source swap). The N2 remainder landed 2026-08-02 with N0/N1: `/consumption` registered `preview` in the manifest (its marker is manifest-driven, so E5's flip is one line) and the intervention-rate metric renders in the Consumption narrative. THE FLIP EXECUTED 2026-08-10 (ENG-008 E5): `consumption` reads `live` in the manifest and the Coming-soon marker is gone — the one-line change landed exactly as designed.
- N3 Organization and Cloud previews (landed 2026-08-02): the multi-human story over Workspace scope (**Organization**, not Team — decision `0023`) with members/roles/spend derived from the Voltaic fixtures, and the one-action push story on `/cloud` told over a real fixture Session; the `announced` _Push to cloud_ row sits inert in the Agent tab context menu with the Cloud entry point beside it, and the Organization anchor rides the Workspace switcher.
- N4 Coordination preview (landed 2026-08-02): blackboard/bus/viewer in broad strokes, a real-lens assignment board over dispatch-engine, the gated coordination ladder, and an ENG-019 handoff-record specimen; vocabulary is **assignment**, never claim (test-enforced). Anchor on the Team altitude header.
- N5 Agent Type previews (landed 2026-08-02): the `announced` Type chip on Sessions cards (the tab-strip chip is deliberately omitted — see the ENG-028 log) and the Agent Types surface over Voltaic's authored roster; the composer source row carries the surface's entry point.

Sequencing: runs alongside ENG-027, which supplies the demo sources this item's previews consume. Neither blocks the active daily-driver arc, but both outrank long-arc items for the current demo posture.

### ENG-027 Workspace tenancy and the Demo Workspace

Status: landed — W1–W9 landed by 2026-08-03. The Demo Workspace is live: Voltaic populates Agent, Team, Fleet, ⌘K, and Consumption through the production surfaces; W5 makes the future shared Organization tenant visible beside Personal and Demo, and W9 makes the same tenant model searchable and actionable in ⌘K. Created 2026-08-02 from the operator brief and its same-day design pass. The operator chose the real tenancy seam over a demo-only toggle.

Direction: the account/organization switcher the brief asks for is `Workspace` — already canonical in `concepts.md` as the boundary for users, agents, initiatives, context, secrets, spend, policies, and governance. **Personal** is local truth; **Demo** is the first non-personal tenant and carries the altitude-scale demo the operator's real dozen-agent machine cannot tell; team Workspaces stay `preview` until ENG-012.

Scope:

- Workspace as a real switchable scope with an account-menu switcher, Workspace-scoped view state, and Workspace-attributed feedback and consumption
- **switching Workspaces never touches live agents** — local PTYs keep running, returning to Personal finds everything as it was; this is the item's most important correctness property, because a demo must never cost the operator real work
- a **pane content source** seam so a Terminal pane renders from either a live PTY or a fixed transcript buffer; demo tabs render honest recorded/authored transcripts and cannot reach the PTY path. No LLM calls, no simulated streaming, no scripted agent replies (operator, explicitly)
- an authored, versioned, resettable demo fleet: 6–12 Projects with real roadmaps that parse under the published convention, Agents spread across the five-signal status protocol including delegation, readable Sessions, and plausible consumption history
- demo content is **one plausible multi-function startup, majority coding** (operator, 2026-08-02): frontend/API/infra/design engineering Projects alongside a minority of marketing, research, and support work, so the fleet sells the Agent Types vision (ENG-028) without implying those non-coding capabilities already ship. Non-coding Agents must read as ENG-026 `preview` content, never as working capability
- a scale tier dense enough that the Fleet "zoom out" moment shows aggregation, clustering, and label budgets doing real work
- boundary — demo data can never be mistaken for real: Workspace identity is always visible, and demo consumption never contributes to Personal totals
- boundary — scale honesty (decided 2026-08-02): the honest authored fleet tops out at ~209 board entities; the synthetic 1k/10k tiers are rendering-headroom proof (ENG-004 V3.1) and are always presented as synthetic, never as "the fleet"; demo scripts say "hundreds"
- future: team Workspaces (ENG-012), shared/read-only Workspace links as a candidate first multiplayer primitive (ENG-034)

Exit criteria:

- the operator can demo Agent → Team → Fleet across several demo Projects, Agents, and roadmaps without leaving the product
- switching to Demo and back leaves every live local Session untouched and exactly where it was
- no demo surface can be mistaken for Personal truth, and no demo tab can spawn a process
- the pane content source is a general seam, not demo-only code
- every demo Agent traces to one authored Initiative, and the Agent/Team projections expose that strategic context without inventing it for sources that do not report it

Milestones:

- W1 Workspace as a real scope — landed 2026-08-02: tenancy module (`src/lib/tenancy/`), account-menu switcher with Demo as `Coming soon`, Workspace-scoped surface memory, and the no-lifecycle-side-effects guarantee proven by `pnpm eval:electron:tenancy` (a live shell keeps executing while another Workspace is on screen and re-adopts exactly on return).
- W2 Demo source and pane content source — landed 2026-08-02: `DemoWorkspaceTransport` feeds the Voltaic fixtures through the SAME FleetManager contract the live transport feeds (timestamps rebased to now); the demo shell renders panes from the content source (hero transcripts or the honest session record — never a PTY, never a blank pane); Demo flips `available`; ⌘K lists demo Sessions with every PTY-reaching verb gated off; `/consumption` joins the tenant-scope-gated set with the Voltaic corpus as its Demo source; `MockFleetTransport`/`DemoControls` demote to eval-only per the recorded disposition. Proven by `pnpm eval:electron:tenancy` (31 checks incl. a relaunch-inside-Demo phase).
- W3 Demo fleet content (landed 2026-08-02): Voltaic Grid Systems — an AI-native virtual-power-plant startup — authored as deterministic `@exawatt/core` fixtures (`packages/core/src/demo/`): 10 Projects (7 coding, 3 `preview` desks), 4 Initiatives, 27 hand-authored Agents spanning all five D40 signals with delegation, 3 hero transcripts, and 14 days of real `ConsumptionSample` history; all 10 roadmaps parse with zero warnings under the published convention, enforced by test. Known rot (recorded 2026-08-03): ~30 hardcoded prose dates and weekday references in the roadmap markdown and agent copy do not rebase with the fixture clock — invisible while the corpus is fresh, but a demo run months out will show "started 5h ago" beside "landed 2026-07-28"; derive those dates from the fixture clock before a later demo cycle.
- W4 Scale tier — DATA (landed 2026-08-02): deterministic scale generator brings the fleet to 173 Agents plus 36 delegated runs, each executing a distinct assignment (singleton workstreams and honestly partitioned fan-out batches) tracing to a real roadmap item; uniqueness, five-signal spread, preview honesty, and determinism are test-enforced. Ownership boundary held: this milestone produced the DATA; ENG-004 V3.1 owns RENDERING it.
- W5 Organization Workspace preview (landed 2026-08-03): the shared Voltaic Grid Systems tenant is a real `TenantWorkspace` row with `preview` availability and a typed destination to `/organization`; it can navigate but can never activate or persist as the current Workspace before a shared source exists.
- W6 Demo shell rides the real workspace chrome (operator, 2026-08-03): the Demo Agent altitude must render through the SAME components the live shell uses — the D45 single-row `TabStrip` ribbon, the live chrome/context-bar/stage frame, and the exposé's inert-underlay pattern — never a demo-only navigation surface (the W2 shell's bespoke left rail was the defect). Demo-local verbs that cannot touch a process (select, reorder, rename, recolor, close/reopen of fixture tabs) work for real against fixture state; process verbs are simply absent. The pane content source remains the only demo-specific rendering.
- W7 Demo legibility pass (operator, 2026-08-03): three directives from the W6 review. (1) Every demo Session opens READABLE — no "No transcript recorded" dead ends; hand-authored Sessions carry a few-bullet work log ("worked on X, then Y"), generated board Sessions derive an honest log from their fixture facts; dense transcripts stay reserved for the heroes. (2) Blocker copy is plain-language first: a technical or non-technical demo audience must parse every blocker title in one read ("Waiting on credentials from the utility's IT team"), with the technical detail below, never in the headline. (3) Counts reconcile across altitudes: Consumption reads the FULL fleet corpus so its totals agree with the Fleet board's burn lens; Team's 27 tiles are the open-Session subset of the board's 173 and every surface's number must map to that story. Landed 2026-08-03: work logs authored for the 24 non-hero Sessions and derived for the generated tier (`demoWorkLog`); every blocker headline rewritten one-read-plain (base and generated, with the fixture roadmap and demo script aligned); the consumption corpus carries the full fleet's current sessions (generated coding tier rebalanced codex-majority so the corpus keeps its measured codex-majority property; the measured delegated-share claim now asserts on the history portion it describes).
- W8 Identity and Initiative coherence (landed 2026-08-03): **Demo** is the tenant's one visible name; **Voltaic Grid Systems** is separately modeled as the organization the fixtures portray. Every one of the 173 Agents carries a valid Initiative assignment consistent with its Project, and the shared source projection shows that Initiative in the Agent context bar and Team cards; `/hud-gallery` carries the DOM specimen. This exposes existing fixture truth only—Initiative aggregation and drill remain parked under ENG-005 / ENG-004's recorded boundary.
- W9 ⌘K Workspace switching (landed 2026-08-03): Personal, Demo, and Organization Workspace rows project from the shared tenancy model into the palette. Available rows call the exact `switchWorkspace` seam, preview rows open their typed destination without activating, and current or coming-soon rows remain truthfully inert.

Sequencing: W1 and W2 precede ENG-026's preview surfaces, which consume the same sources.

## Next

### ENG-019 Cooperative session crystallization

Status: planned — follows ENG-003 because semantic checkpointing belongs behind source-aware Agent Source / Harness adapters.

Scope:

- on graceful quit or update, agents finish their current atomic turn, write a versioned per-Session handoff under `.exawatt/sessions/`, follow repo/local policy and repository git instructions, and report readiness
- Exawatt coordinates and displays that work but never runs git on the agent's behalf
- forced quit always remains available and falls back to ENG-018's latest technical checkpoint

Evidence for the design pass (a partner conversation): an external user keeps per-branch session state ("agent brain": CLI logs, research notes, plan, task list) in `~/_agent/session_state/<branch>`, deliberately outside the repo and unversioned, because he jumps non-linearly through stacked commits during review while his working knowledge only moves forward in human time. A repo-committed handoff under `.exawatt/sessions/` is pinned to a git ref and loses exactly that property — the same repo-visible vs machine-local question ENG-037 records for its frozen artifact. Decide deliberately, don't inherit "in the repo" as a default.

### ENG-003 Unified Agent Source architecture

Status: active-build — OPERATOR LIFT 2026-07-27 is deliberately narrow: design and prototype the Settings Agent Source registry, capability truth, source-owned authentication, and dynamic model-discovery boundary. Broad remote connection implementation and multi-source fleet management remain planned behind the daily-driver arc and gallery acceptance. This amends the 2026-07-12 hold without erasing its sequencing intent. EXTENDED 2026-08-03 (operator design pass) with S2/S3 — **engine plurality**: a third launchable source, `opencode`, is how OpenRouter, Kimi, other open models, and local inference reach a Session, and decision `0027` records why that rather than injecting provider credentials into a single-vendor harness. Authentication stays source-owned and this item's "no local provider token is stored by Exawatt" criterion is unchanged. EXTENDED 2026-08-12 with S4 — the **Grok Build adapter**, operator-pulled during the Grok Bot category wave and shaped for immediate execution; the recon verdict is easy-to-medium because the harness deliberately clones Claude Code's integration surfaces.

This item owns the source-facing portion of Exawatt's agency-control spine. That spine is a contract threaded through the existing source, Decision, Approval, Policy/Budget, Secrets, hosted-control-plane, and orchestrator work — not a new roadmap item or a mandate to build downstream integrations now. Decision `0016` records the provider-first boundary.

The operator lifted the hold after reviewing the two-source evidence and a
planning pass. The executable mile is the registry/capability foundation, not
the ten-mile fleet program. Production Terminal wiring followed review of the
`/hud-gallery/agent-sources` prototype (S1; the prototype itself was retired
in ENG-036 G1 after graduating to `/settings`); Settings architecture and
canon may advance now without pretending hosted/custom sources already
connect.

Scope:

- model Agent Sources/Harnesses generically
- model multiple configured instances of one source type without making
  `Connection` a second product boundary
- keep installation, reachability, authentication, identity, version
  compatibility, capability, freshness, and provenance as independent facts;
  derive compact UI state without destroying those facts
- Claude Code, Codex, and OpenCode adapters pulled forward (they are ENG-002's interactive terminal harnesses); local OpenClaw and Demo Scenario Source normalize through the same boundary
- keep source-specific protocol details behind adapters
- keep Claude Code, Codex, and OpenCode login credentials with their harnesses; launch the
  source-owned sign-in flow and recheck rather than ingesting provider tokens
- treat eligible subscription-backed harness sign-in as a first-class current
  entitlement path: Exawatt does not require an API key or separate token
  balance when the source is already launchable, and Consumption never infers
  billing mode or unreported plan headroom
- permit only narrowly scoped OS-keychain material for remote Gateway/custom
  source connections; ENG-009 still owns general Agent Secrets/Credentials
- discover model catalogs at runtime through supported source contracts and
  retain provenance/freshness. Claude Code's catalog comes from its SDK
  `initialize` control response (the rows `/model` renders); offline fallback
  uses only observed configuration or the account default, never provider
  aliases. OpenCode's bounded verbose catalog supplies source-reported
  `provider/model` rows and exact variant keys; S2 applies model, interactive
  variant, and ordered permissions through a guarded unique launch agent
- separate global source/account facts in Settings from the
  Project-effective model and effort shown at the Agent altitude
- describe each source's supported launch policies, reported activity, available evidence, and actual enforcement owner without converting unknowns into safety claims
- distinguish installed or advertised skills/tools from capabilities actually connected to the active Session; represent unavailable runtime backends explicitly. The [verified Codex CLI browser case](projects/agent-terminal-workspace.md#dogfood-investigation-codex-browser-capability-boundary-2026-07-22) is the first concrete evidence for this requirement
- normalize declared intent, proposed/attempted actions, resulting effects, and the independent reported/observed/authorized/enforced/verified assurance facets when sources expose them
- keep harness manufacturers and user-chosen systems responsible for near-term security enforcement; preserve an adapter seam for later Exawatt-owned or third-party mediation
- decide the local evidence-retention default: redacted structured metadata by default with detailed content opt-in, or durable evidence entirely opt-in; secret values are never evidence
- prepare for hosted OpenClaw and custom harnesses

Exit criteria:

- UI does not directly depend on one provider's internal vocabulary
- Demo Mode and local OpenClaw normalize into the same UI-facing concepts
- Settings can distinguish ready, connecting, action required, degraded,
  unavailable, not installed, incompatible, and unknown from normalized facts
- a supported-but-uninstalled source and a future **Coming soon** source are
  visibly different; neither looks like a broken connection
- no local provider token is stored by Exawatt, and every displayed catalog or
  effective model has a named source or explicit unknown/default state
- UI can state who reported, enforced, or verified an activity and can represent unavailable assurance explicitly

Milestones:

- S0 Agent Source registry and capability-truth lab (landed 2026-08-02): canon and the operator-reviewed `/hud-gallery/agent-sources` prototype cover Connected, Sign-in required, Gateway degraded, Add source, the official OpenClaw mark, connection value, and Project-effective model provenance at desktop and compact-responsive geometry. Production wiring remains a separate accepted slice (the lab itself was retired in ENG-036 G1 once `/settings` shipped)
- S1 production registry and composer wiring (landed 2026-08-02): Electron-main discovery now normalizes local Claude Code, Codex, OpenClaw, and Demo Mode into production Settings; the composer consumes launch capability truth, Claude mirrors its live source catalog with an honest configured/account-default fallback, and dedicated Electron evaluation covers secrets and responsive geometry
- S1.1 source-truth and recovery hardening (landed 2026-08-02): generated shared declarations eliminate main/renderer drift; observed, declared, and simulated evidence remain distinct; OpenClaw readiness requires a protocol probe; registry failure is fail-closed; Electron revalidates before spawn; and Settings provides bounded sign-in reconciliation plus actionable install recovery
- S2 The `opencode` adapter (landed 2026-08-03): Exawatt's third launchable source spans the generated declaration, registry, Settings/composer launch paths, live model and exact-variant catalog, guarded unique launch-agent policy, native recent history, and exact post-first-turn identity/resume; verification evidence and amendments live in the project doc
- S3 Provider and model plurality as observed truth (shaped 2026-08-03, follows S2): a provider is a fact a source reports, not an entity Exawatt connects to; the model axis becomes searchable and provider-grouped for catalogs of hundreds; local inference earns a distinct readout (nothing leaves this machine, no billed dollars, watts as the honest unit) and never a distinct code path. Per the operator the near-term job is the UI that shows the app supports this, under the ENG-026 readiness grammar — running local inference is not itself a near-term requirement
- S4 The Grok Build adapter (landed 2026-08-13; operator-pulled during the Grok Bot wave): xAI's open-source terminal harness is the fourth launchable source — generated declaration, registry facts, D46/D49 launch path, live `grok models` catalog, Exawatt-allocated `--session-id` identity with exact `--resume`, native recent history from its own `summary.json`, and `updates.jsonl` `turn_completed` usage as the ENG-008 local consumption read. Auth stays source-owned (its own OAuth in `~/.grok/auth.json`, never read by Exawatt); decision `0027` is untouched because this is a native harness, not credential injection. TWO shaped assumptions were corrected against the real install: `signals.json` is live context occupancy, not a billed total, so the ledger reads `updates.jsonl` instead; and the interactive TUI exposes no per-launch hook seam, so delegation is declared unobservable and status rides inference like Codex's rather than the ENG-023 channel. Grok-the-model remains launchable via the S2 `opencode` source. Verified facts, both corrections, and the follow-up seam: project doc §S4
