### ENG-023 Delegation visibility — subagents as fleet truth

Status: active-build — **D1/D3a/D3b/D3c/D4/D5 LANDED; D2 remains planned.** D3c was shaped from operator dogfood on 2026-08-06 and landed 2026-08-07 with ENG-004 V3.4: D3b's Spatial dots were truthful but too recessive, so individual-resolution Fleet now carries parent-like child units, visible lineage, and lifecycle motion. D5 reopened the Codex source capability after 2026-08-16 operator dogfood proved that Codex runs real parallel subagents while Exawatt rendered none. The harness mechanism, state model, altitude sequence, and content boundary remain as verified against real Claude Code delegation; full evidence and contracts live in the project doc.

Direction: a Session that has delegated work is not one Agent. Today Exawatt counts tabs, so a tab reading "1 agent" can be four Opus children editing four files in three worktrees, and the strip, exposé, and board all report one. Every concept this touches is already canon and unimplemented (`concepts.md` on subagents; `reference/agent-state.md`'s `Delegated` Event verb and Spatial's delegation-topology mandate). The gap is a reader, not a vocabulary.

Mechanism (SUPERSEDES the capture-time framing): delegation is observed from **harness-reported lifecycle events**, not from a directory layout. Claude Code accepts hooks injected per launch via `claude --settings <file>`; the pass verified that `SubagentStart` / `SubagentStop` / `Stop` / `UserPromptSubmit` all fire, that `type: "http"` hooks POST to a loopback listener, that injected hooks MERGE with the user's own project hooks rather than clobbering them, that `SubagentStop` hands over `agent_transcript_path` and the child's result, and that a dead listener fails open without breaking the Session. Path derivation therefore leaves the critical path — which is what makes this safe for users who are not the operator — and the on-disk reader survives only as a fallback for history and for Sessions Exawatt did not launch. The channel is a general source-agnostic observation seam; delegation is its first consumer, not its purpose.

Partner evidence for why observation must be ARTIFACT-GROUNDED, not
self-reported (a partner conversation). Watching a live
five-sub-agent demo, a non-engineer partner kept asking where the output
actually went — the children each reported "printed hello" and then died,
leaving the parent's summary as the only trace. He then designed his own test
rather than requesting a feature: "just for illustration purposes, can you have
them generate a file and add a file to the folder or something? Like, yes, I
made this. I was here." His stated reason is prior-model distrust that will not
be argued away by better copy: "I've seen so many times with Gemini, with the
hallucinations, it'll be like, I did that. And they're like, no, you didn't."
Once files appeared: "so they basically can prove they've done the job." Read
this against the content boundary above — that boundary governs where a child's
RESULT may be read, and nothing here reverses it. What the evidence adds is that
a user with no ability to read code treats a harness-reported completion as an
unverified claim, so the surfaces owe evidence of effect and not just lifecycle
state. Do not shape a mechanism from this; it is demand, and D2 is the milestone
that would carry it. The same session confirmed the Codex asymmetry already
recorded in ENG-003 S4 against a real external user: he could not reach a Codex
child at all ("I don't think Codex supports that yet"), and Codex chose to run
three children, kill them, and spawn two rather than run five and kill three —
behavior neither participant could explain, which Exawatt reported without
interpretation, correctly.

Scope:

- a source-agnostic **harness event channel**: loopback listener (127.0.0.1, ephemeral port, per-Session bearer token, bounded payloads), a per-Session settings file written into Exawatt's own state directory, and inspectable disclosure of exactly what Exawatt injects
- a **delegation capability contract** with three mechanisms — push (Claude Code hooks, verified), pull (the existing local-log reader), protocol (OpenClaw gateway events and the Codex app-server adapter) — so a source declares whether delegation is observable and how
- the Codex PTY byte stream remains delegation-blind; D5's separate read-side app-server adapter declares protocol observation, correlates exact thread IDs, and fails to absent without filesystem, process, worktree, or terminal inference
- a **two-fact state model** — own turn (`generating` between `UserPromptSubmit` and `Stop`, else `available`) and delegated work (outstanding `SubagentStart` minus `SubagentStop`) — kept independent, broadcast to the renderer, and consumed by one shared derivation in `session-status.ts`
- Agent answers "can I talk to it?"; Team and Fleet answer "is this work moving?" — a Session with running children never reads as finished at fleet-comparison altitude
- compact DOM surfaces retain child **dots, not counts**, in a constant footprint; D3c supersedes the Fleet board's dot-only treatment at individual resolution with parent-like child units while preserving exact census and stable layout
- Agent-altitude ribbon boundary: delegated children aggregate into the parent Initiative-shaped tab and compact Project signal; they do not multiply top-level ribbon tabs or auto-expand a Project
- `attention-monitor` stops raising `turn-end` while children are outstanding — the concrete correctness fix, since byte quiescence currently reports a delegating parent as a finished turn
- boundary — **no new light**: the reviewed five-light vocabulary is unchanged; existing states get more correct inputs and the composer behaves correctly, rather than growing a sixth state
- boundary — **content**: labels (agent type, the child's own description, elapsed, state) may appear on every surface; a child's result is readable at the Agent altitude only, stays in the main process, and is never sent off-machine or routed to the ENG-021 summarizer
- boundary — **no activity exhaust**: `PostToolUse` inside children is available and deliberately NOT subscribed; a child-by-child tool ticker at the Team altitude is a regression, not a feature
- boundary — **absent reads as absent**: never an empty state, a zero count, or a broken surface in a parallel first-class regime
- boundary — **hook latency is operator latency**: one loopback POST, short timeout, fail-open, as a permanent regression gate
- not scope: a results-collection surface; the operator explicitly deprioritized it and results stay reachable at the Agent altitude

Exit criteria:

- a delegating Claude Code Session never reads as "result ready" while any child is running, at any altitude
- a Claude Code Session waiting on the operator — a question, a permission, an MCP elicitation — reads as needs-you, and reads the SAME before and after the operator focuses its tab
- ~~typing into a parent whose own turn has ended sends immediately~~ — CORRECTED 2026-07-27 during D1: Exawatt never gated input. `terminal-pane` passes `term.onData` straight to `pty.write`, so queueing is entirely the harness TUI's own behavior and there was nothing to build. The availability fact is real and observable; it simply needed no code, which is why D1 adds no new light and no composer change
- a Codex Session with a compatible app-server shows the exact source-reported child census and lifecycle; an unavailable or incompatible protocol shows no delegation affordance at all — not an empty one — and Demo Mode drives the identical view model
- Exawatt closing, crashing, or never starting its listener leaves harness Sessions completely unaffected
- the user can see exactly what Exawatt injects into their harness configuration, and Exawatt never mutates `~/.claude` or `~/.codex`
- at individual Fleet resolution, a four-child Session reads as one parent plus four recognizable child units with visible lineage; spawn/stop motion is finite, input-safe, reduced-motion equivalent, and preserves the board's existing performance budgets

Milestones:

- D1 "Is that quiet tab done, or waiting on children?" (landed 2026-07-27): `electron/main/harness-events/` — loopback channel, per-launch token, Claude adapter, delegation monitor, hook-settings store — plus the two-fact state model, `sessionGlyphState` delegation awareness, the withheld turn-end, and capped child dots. Terminal, Sessions, and the ⌘K switcher inherit correctness from the one shared derivation. `pnpm eval:electron:delegation` drives the whole pipeline through a fixture harness that consumes the ACTUAL injected settings and posts the ACTUAL hook payload shapes; a real Claude session spawning a real Explore subagent was observed reporting `{ownTurn: "generating", children: [{agentType: "Explore"}]}` and holding `working` with no turn-end for the full life of the child
- D4 "Is that tab done, or waiting on ME?" (landed 2026-08-02): the third reported fact — `blockedOn` — beside own turn and children, from the hooks that actually report an operator gate (`PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse` MATCHED to `AskUserQuestion`, `Notification` matched to `permission_prompt`/`agent_needs_input`/`elicitation_dialog`, `ElicitationResult`, and `PostToolBatch` as the granted-permission release). Captured 2026-08-02 from an operator report with screenshots: a `⌘4` tab showed a green Result while Claude sat on an `AskUserQuestion`, then flipped to the blue Active rotor the instant the tab was focused. Three defects, one event — (1) `attention-monitor` was handed a delegated-children boolean rather than the reported record, so quiescence went on concluding "turn finished" for a Session whose harness had reported the turn still `generating`; (2) no subscribed hook reported an operator gate at all, so the truthful `needs-you` light was unreachable and the tab read "working — output streaming" forever after focus cleared the bogus result; (3) `sessionStatusLightState` short-circuited to `result` on a turn-end signal instead of going through the priority encoder, so the contradiction stayed invisible until focus cleared the signal and revealed the state underneath. The durable rules: reported outranks inferred AT THE SOURCE (one `setReportedTurnSource`, not one injection per fact); gate releases are reason-scoped so an unrelated hook can never answer an open question, with turn boundaries as the never-latch backstop; and attention is what the operator has not SEEN while the light is what is TRUE, so focusing a tab can never change the light. `idle_prompt` is deliberately not a gate. Still no sixth light: a gate lands on the existing `needs-you`. `turn-truth-pipeline.test.ts` wires the real monitors to the real render derivation and asserts the light before and after focus on every path, and `eval:electron:turn-truth` replays the whole scenario in the running app. VERIFIED against Claude Code 2.1.220 itself, not only against fixtures: matchers genuinely scope HTTP hooks (a `Bash` call posts no `PreToolUse`/`PostToolUse`, so the no-activity-ticker boundary holds), and a live `AskUserQuestion` reports `UserPromptSubmit -> PreToolUse[AskUserQuestion] -> Notification[permission_prompt]` with NO `Stop` — the root cause, observed. That measurement also corrected the design mid-flight: one question is announced TWICE under two different names, so the first report of a gate wins until released, otherwise the second would overwrite the reason and strand the scoped release. REVIEWED and corrected 2026-08-02: D4's first cut suppressed inference for ANY reported-open turn, which was wrong — an aborted turn emits no boundary at all (measured against every documented hook), so a bare `generating` is not proof of life and every interrupted tab spun forever. A reported turn now outranks inference only while something EXPLAINS its silence (a running child, an open gate — both end with a guaranteed event); a bare `generating` is reclaimed by inference as an ordinary `turn-end`, on the same single condition that gates the queue so `⌘J` and the light can never disagree. The same review found the Project ribbon dot re-deriving Session truth from raw activity/attention instead of the shared derivation — the loudest surviving instance of the original bug, since a collapsed Project shows only that dot — and routed it through `sessionStatusLightState`
- D5 Codex spawned-thread observation (landed 2026-08-16; product-feedback `72c79f40` / BUG-020): a version- and shape-probed read-side Codex 0.147 app-server adapter correlates exact parent/descendant thread IDs and source lifecycle into the existing delegation model; reconnect resnapshots authoritatively and protocol failure withdraws to absent without synthetic completion. The 23-check Electron eval drives two live children through Agent, Team, Fleet, exact completion, loss, and reconnect; no filesystem, worktree, process-tree, or terminal-text evidence enters the adapter
- D2 Terminal delegation detail: the per-child rail — type, description, elapsed, state — with the child's result readable there. Inherits D3a's description channel; open
- D3 Fleet topology and child zoom (design pass 2026-08-02, operator; grammar in the project doc's "D3 design pass" section): D3a Sessions child rail (landed 2026-08-02) — per-child type/description/elapsed rows on the parent tile, fed by a new `PreToolUse[Agent|Task]` subscription whose spawn-time POST is the `Delegated` Event itself, so the no-activity-exhaust boundary holds; labels only, minute-granularity elapsed, constant tile footprint (248→272px, the one deliberate geometry change), capped rows with the census in the D1 tooltip and the tile's accessible name; `eval:electron:delegation` grew the label path and asserts the child prompt never crosses IPC. D3b Spatial delegation satellites (landed 2026-08-02) — instanced accent dots above the delegating piece at every altitude, count+kinds in the DOM control copy, delegated children force `working` in the fleet transport with tab-strip precedence, `pty:delegation` push refresh; repaired `eval:spatial`'s agent-unit selector, which still matched pre-status-light lowercase labels and failed on every scenario at baseline. Child zoom stays open behind D2 so the zoom has a destination. REVIEWED 2026-08-02 (three-perspective adversarial pass, fixes landed same day): correlation now fails to absent — never to wrong — under redelivery, reordering, and cap overflow (adopted-label and ended-child tombstones; the cap drops the incoming label); the monitor ignores post-drop stragglers; the tile pins Next by construction under one clipping band and carries the census in its accessible name; the satellite buffer matches the 600-instance layout budget. Full record in the project doc's post-landing review
- D3c Fleet child units and lifecycle motion (landed 2026-08-07 with ENG-004 V3.4; brief in the project doc; operator visual review of the tuning calls still owed): D3b's dot-only treatment at individual resolution is replaced by same-family beveled hex child units at `0.72×` the parent (the brief's `0.70×` floor holds), deterministic rosette slots across the upper/side perimeter that never enter the parent's label lane, a hairline Project-identity tether for lineage, an overflow lobe above five children carrying the exact remaining count, and finite damped spawn/stop transitions with reduced-motion parity. Slot geometry, the overflow boundary, and lineage endpoints are pure tested `ui-model` policy; the R3F layer is a damped executor with no lifecycle logic inside `<Canvas>`. Children are focusable DOM controls exposing type, description, elapsed, and parent; activating opens the PARENT Session and children never join **Direct N Agents**. No org-chart reframe, new status light, child activity exhaust, or parallel camera. Two visual calls the brief reserved for operator review are made and flagged, not settled: the orbit places each child just clear of the parent body (close enough that a dense Project altitude stays legible, far enough that no outline or halo is needed to tell the two apart), and the overflow lobe was chosen over a second ring

Why it earns a pass beyond the visualization: it would be the first STRUCTURED truth Exawatt holds about a Claude Session (attention, turn state, and context labels are all derived from raw PTY bytes today), and ENG-008 Consumption cannot be honest while delegated spend stays invisible. CORRECTED 2026-07-24 by measurement (ENG-008 E0, see `projects/consumption-spine.md` §3): this item previously claimed delegated spend "dwarfs parents' own usage locally." It does not. On the real corpus it is 37.7% of Claude samples, 18.0% of raw tokens, and 23.7% of normalized tokens — far too large for Consumption to ignore, but not dominant. The visualization case stands on its own; do not restate the dwarfs claim.

Sequencing: after the design pass, and behind the active daily-driver arc (ENG-015 / ENG-016 / ENG-021). Feeds ENG-004's delegation-topology mandate and ENG-008 Consumption; the source-capability boundary belongs to ENG-003.

### ENG-028 Agent Types

Status: planned — created 2026-08-02 from the operator brief and design pass C. T1 (the preview affordance) landed 2026-08-02 under ENG-026 N5; the mechanism (T2 onward) follows.

Direction (operator framing): "Perhaps our UI should convey which _type_ of
agent is running a given project, not just which harness happens to be
orchestrating it." Amended by decision `0037`: the Agent is the individual
coworker, the Type describes what kind of coworker, and the harness is the
engine. Today Exawatt says "Claude Code" where it should eventually say "your
Designer, running on Claude Code."

Scope:

- an Agent Type is a named portable bundle — identity (`SOUL.md`-style: who this worker is, not what task to do), instructions, required tools and permissions, default model and effort — applied by Exawatt at launch
- a Type is a reusable blueprint, not a roster identity: several Agents may
  share it, and a named source Agent is never promoted to a Type automatically
- mechanism already proven: ENG-023 D1 verified per-launch settings injection that MERGES with the user's own config and never mutates `~/.claude`; Types extend that seam and ENG-016 D35's typed launch boundary rather than inventing a meta-harness
- Types are repo-storable AND portable across Projects; design toward a documented format so a Type can later be shared or vended
- portability across harness classes (a coding harness vs a general-purpose one) resolves as **Types declare what they require; sources declare what they can do** — ENG-003's capability truth used at a second altitude, so an under-capable launch shows what is missing instead of silently degrading. The operator's instruction on this is explicitly "architect for change"
- self-improvement inherits one hard constraint: a Type may evolve its own identity file, but changes are disclosed and reviewable; silent self-modification contradicts vision principle 4
- boundary — Types are not a second permission system: launch policy stays ENG-016 D14's per-Project/harness Ask first / Auto-review / YOLO, provider-enforced per decision `0016`
- deliberately unshaped: Type-scoped conversation that outlives a Session ("I want to be able to talk to my Designer over time"), and how a Type scales out into clones or delegates

Milestones:

- T1 Agent Type preview (landed 2026-08-02, executing ENG-026 N5): the `announced` Type chip on Sessions cards (named by the source when a Type is declared, the empty Type slot otherwise; the 46px condensed tab-strip chip is deliberately omitted — the project doc records why) plus the `/agent-types` preview surface over Voltaic's authored roster with its composer-row entry point.
- T2 Type format and launch application through the existing injection seam, with capability requirements declared against ENG-003 source truth. AMENDED 2026-08-03 by ENG-016 D46 and its operator-confirmed lightweight-launcher review: a named Launch Configuration is only a friendly reusable preset; naming does not turn it into a worker. T2 later supplies the explicit portable Type identity, instructions, required tools, and capability requirements that a configuration may select through quiet Customize. T1's standalone composer icon retires into that `announced` Customize entry when D46 ships. ENG-003 S2 supplies a real mechanism to declare against: `opencode --agent`.
- T3 Reviewer/collaborator Types: the operator's concrete first case — a Designer that gates others' front-end work, either enforcing the style guide or deliberately improving it.
- T4 Type-scoped conversation and disclosed self-improvement. Unshaped.

### ENG-029 Project blackboard and agent bus

Status: planned — created 2026-08-02 from the operator brief and design pass C. C1 (the viewer) landed 2026-08-02 as a preview under ENG-026 N4; the substrate (C2 onward, gated) follows.

Direction (operator framing): "There needs to be some inter-agent substrate between the agents — some sort of shared, mutable, discoverable state... like a 'bus' between CPU components... But also state: learnings, activity, decisions, debates, plans, and history, but not too unmanageable and infinite." Researched 2026-08-02: this is the **blackboard architecture**, where agents coordinate indirectly through a shared structured workspace instead of conversing. 2026 orchestration surveys find context INCONSISTENCY, not orchestration pattern, is the leading production failure — so shared single-copy state is the primary mechanism and messaging is secondary. The blackboard shape also structurally prevents the debate/indecision/sycophancy loops the operator named.

Scope:

- **the repo is the blackboard, Exawatt is the bus** (operator decision): durable shared state lives in the project repo under the `.exawatt/` path ENG-019 already claims, as plain git-versioned files readable by agents that have never heard of Exawatt. Same posture as ENG-017 and ENG-019 — with this third instance it becomes product philosophy, and a marketing claim: your agents' shared memory is yours, in your repo, offline, no lock-in
- **no second event channel**: ENG-023 D1's source-agnostic harness event channel (loopback listener, per-Session token, merging injection, bounded payloads, fail-open) is the bus. Coordination is its second consumer
- coordination climbs the least-chatty ladder on evidence only: **claims** now → directed one-way notes later → a queryable room later and gated
- **all inter-agent traffic is auditable by the operator**, at every rung — a coordination layer the human cannot read is what becomes untrustworthy at scale
- **agency** is a runtime capability, not a kind of agent: an Agent can be started by a trigger — time first (the form OpenClaw solves with cron), then bus events. Triggered launches stay as visible, attributable, killable, and policy-bound as operator launches
- a findings/learnings board ships LAST and only with an earn-your-place contract plus a consolidation pass, because the operator's skepticism that it "would just grow crufty and bloated" is well-founded; this repo's `incidents/` and `decisions/` directories work precisely because entries must earn their place and `AGENTS.md` makes agents read them
- consolidation ("dreaming") inherits the sleep-time-compute constraint: async, off the critical path, emitting CANDIDATES with the original intact and rollback available
- future, explicitly allowed and not foreclosed: a hosted per-Project brain as an upgrade path (likely repo for durable state, hosted for live queryable state)
- boundary — the substrate degrades to nothing: an agent with no Exawatt running, or a repo with no `.exawatt/`, behaves exactly as it does today

Milestones:

- C1 Coordination viewer (landed 2026-08-02 as the `/coordination` preview, executing ENG-026 N4): blackboard/bus/viewer, the ladder with assignments gated, and a handoff-record specimen; the derived board shows what the ENG-017 lens already reads. Speaks **assignment**, never claim, test-enforced. The live viewer over real traffic remains this milestone's remainder and follows the substrate.
- C2 Assignments: `.exawatt/` records of which Agent is working which roadmap item, read by other Agents before starting. RESCOPED 2026-08-02 (operator); POSITIVE DEMAND EVIDENCE 2026-08-03 (Ben Kim) now warrants a joint design pass with ENG-017 S15, but does not authorize building the rest of the bus. Vocabulary: **assignment**, never "claim"; `claim` is already the assurance word in this product (source-reported claims, billing claims) and must not be overloaded.
- C3 Triggers: time-based, then bus events, under the same visibility and launch policy as operator launches.
- C4 Directed notes: bounded one-way messages, fully auditable.
- C5 Findings board and consolidation: gated on evidence from C2–C4.

What this does NOT solve (recorded 2026-08-02 after the operator corrected an overreach): assignment records would not have prevented the one real collision this repo has seen. On 2026-08-02 two agents working DIFFERENT items (ENG-026 design, ENG-008 E4 build) both needed "how do we show capability that is not built yet" and independently invented `preview` and `Unbuilt`. That is a shared-VOCABULARY failure, and its fix is ENG-036 owning one answer in one place — not assignment. Meanwhile same-file collisions are already handled by mandatory worktrees, `pnpm agent:land`'s fast-forward guard, and ordinary git conflicts. Collision prevention alone therefore does not justify C2. The 2026-08-03 Ben Kim request supplies a different positive use case: batch dispatch needs durable, inspectable item-to-Agent assignment bookkeeping. Design that narrow path with ENG-017 S15 before building it; a growing directory of per-agent files without the batch workflow would still be a mechanism without a product.

Sequencing: C1 rides the demo arc; C2 onward follows the daily-driver work and is gated on evidence, not assumed. Feeds ENG-013 (a lead agent needs a board to read) and ENG-019 (handoffs are blackboard writes).

### ENG-005 Initiative primitive

Status: planned

Scope:

- introduce Workspace -> Initiative as the main high-level product frame
- define Initiative UI patterns
- connect Agents and Sessions back to Initiatives

Note: ENG-002 ships a thin slice early (Initiative-LABELED terminal windows — grouping and naming only). The durable primitive, its data model, and cross-surface patterns remain here.

### ENG-006 Decision model

Status: planned

Scope:

- make Decision a first-class scoped record
- allow Decisions to attach to Workspace, Initiative, Agent, Session, Context Signal, Artifact, Gateway/Harness, and other durable scopes
- show Decisions as trust/audit context
- support recommended defaults and option sets for high-leverage human touchpoints
- explore orthogonal decision prompts that constrain broad agent fan-out with minimal user input
- cluster decision prompts by context so users can re-enter work without paying the full context-switching cost
- represent Approval scope and lifetime explicitly, and test whether the normal unit should be one proposed action, an action class, or a bounded Session mandate; do not assume the Session mandate before this design work

### ENG-007 Context Signals

Status: planned

Scope:

- model Context Signals as many-to-many inputs across Workspaces, Initiatives, and Agents
- start with source definitions before deep integrations
- examples: PostHog, Slack, email, GitHub, calendar

### ENG-009 Connections, secrets, and configuration

Status: planned — RENAMED and INFLATED 2026-08-03 from the operator's answer during the ENG-003 S2 design pass, deliberately unshaped pending a design pass of its own.

Direction (operator framing, 2026-08-03): source-owned credentials are the right answer for now, _"then I want a more robust connections / integrations system for authing this app with other vendors in the future."_

Direction amended (operator, 2026-08-10, `product-feedback 71d9b784` — the first concrete pull for this item, from daily use rather than planning): _"I find myself repeatedly giving the same instructions and the same credentials to agents across different projects. For example post-hoc admin API key to add projects or add a dashboard or add telemetry support. And also for example Resend and Improv MX API keys to add email aliases pretty much use the same script or whatever between different projects and having to give every agent access to credentials is repetitive and rote. Therefore I proposed a security vault to unlock the safe-use store and provide access and observability of access to such credentials. This would be managed invisibly with Exawatt, the app itself, and then accessible, safe for other agents… This could be extensible for access to other resources as a time capping managed by Exawatt."_ Three things this adds to the recorded scope, none of them shaped here: the vault is **cross-Project by default** (the repetition is the pain, so per-Project custody is the anti-goal); access is **observable** (who read which credential, when); and grants are **time-capped**, which makes Exawatt the broker rather than a copy-paste source. Open question the design pass must answer: the row also asks for repeated **instructions** to be reusable across Projects, which is not credential custody at all — decide whether that belongs here, to ENG-028 Agent Types, or to a third thing, before shaping either.

Scope:

- research buy-vs-build options for secrets management
- document decision in `docs/engineering/decisions/`
- define how agents request and use credentials under policy
- preserve a future credential-broker seam for Exawatt-mediated actions without treating email, payments, messaging, network access, or other integrations as current scope
- a first-class **Connection**: Exawatt authenticating with a third-party vendor on the user's behalf, listed, revocable, and legible — the shape decision `0016` already anticipates when it permits narrowly scoped OS-keychain material for gateway and custom-source connections

Boundaries recorded now, not shaped:

- this is NOT how model freedom arrives. Decision `0027` routes open, hosted, and local models through a multi-provider harness whose auth stays source-owned, precisely so that ENG-003 does not wait on this item and this item does not get built in a hurry to unblock it
- an Agent Source's own sign-in stays owned by that source. A Connection is for vendors Exawatt itself talks to, not a replacement for `claude auth login`
- relation to ENG-007 (Context Signals): a Signal is an input Exawatt reads; a Connection is the authenticated relationship that makes reading it possible. The design pass decides whether they are one mechanism or two
- relation to ENG-038 (Provider consumption accounts): that item is the first concrete consumer of a Connection — credentialed vendor usage reads need exactly this custody, and its own design pass already names ENG-009 as the owner. Its 2026-08-11 slice 1 deliberately did NOT create a Connection: it reads Claude Code's own Keychain credential in place (no copy, no persistence, no Exawatt-owned auth), which keeps the interim surface small and leaves the first-class Connection design entirely to this item; when ENG-009 lands, ENG-038's custody migrates onto it

### ENG-030 Open-source readiness

Status: active-build — the pre-seed safety, legal, provenance, and packaging
foundation is landed; OS4's distribution seam is the active critical path,
followed by call-site migration, the private hosted overlay, fork-safe CI, and
the exact public seed. Gates set the publication dates, not the calendar.
AMENDED 2026-08-12 (operator, Grok-wave decision): OS2–OS5 are fast-tracked and
the formerly optional broad announcement becomes a planned coordinated launch
moment once those gates pass. Nothing publishes with a red gate. See the
Amendment chain and the project doc's dated execution log.

Scope:

- make fresh public `Full-Vibe/exawatt` the canonical daily product-engineering repo and keep current history in living private `Full-Vibe/exawatt-company`
- keep the dependency one-way: private company/cloud/distribution consumes an immutable public revision; public product never needs private code or access
- license application/implementation as AGPL-3.0-or-later and the agent-readable compatibility specification as Apache-2.0, as unmodified standard texts; counsel review is trigger-deferred (`0036` amendment, 2026-08-11), never a publication gate
- use a license-grant CLA, operator-led roadmap governance, module ownership, agent-assisted review, design locks, and Exawatt trademark controls
- make community builds fully useful for Demo Mode and local Agent Sources with no Exawatt service configuration or network calls by default; automatic Exawatt-owned own-account reads require a distribution-declared stable signed identity rather than packaged status alone
- open day-one lanes for harness adapters, roadmap compatibility, Demo/evals, docs, and roadmap-approved defects or performance work

Exit criteria:

- a clean unauthenticated public checkout builds, tests, and runs without a private package, company environment, Exawatt account, or Exawatt service
- the license/CLA/trademark boundary uses standard instruments (off-the-shelf CLA live before the first external PR) and every dependency and asset has a documented distribution basis
- the exact public tree clears secret, provenance, fork-CI, and supply-chain gates; a packaged community build is OBSERVED making zero Exawatt calls (`eval:community:network`, 2026-08-17: resolver interception plus socket sampling, because macOS reserves `/dev/bpf*` to root and an unattended gate may not take sudo)
- three to five Mac developer friends complete public setup and at least two roadmap-gated contributions before contribution lanes are advertised beyond the cohort

Milestones:

- [x] OS0 External-user identity and onboarding.
- [x] OS1 Official analytics, hosted controls, and update-failure evidence.
- [x] OS2.1 Fail-closed path classification, public-content scrub, deterministic seed projection, and secretless worktree bootstrap.
- [x] OS3.1 AGPL/Apache boundary, CLA text, community policies, notices, dependency licensing, asset provenance, and self-hosted fonts.
- [x] OS4.0 Public release-provenance contract and private immutable-release publisher core.
- [x] OS5.1 Content, dependency, asset, license, provenance, and verifier-integrity gates.
- [x] OS5.2 Fork-safe public CI and a fully secretless Next/Electron build (least-privilege permissions, no `secrets.*`, no `pull_request_target`, pinned actions, whole-tree publication gates, `electron:compile`, ubuntu-only; the committed CodeQL/gitleaks/dependency-review scanners stay dormant until OS2.3 flips visibility and OS5.3 runs them on the exact seed).
- [x] OS4.1 Versioned community/official distribution seam and packaged/network-identity neutrality proof (the seam, contract-derived package identity, fail-closed release/dogfood/operator custody, and runtime identity are landed and proved against a real community package; `pnpm eval:community:network` closes it by OBSERVING one, and the row says what the instrument is because it is not tcpdump: `/dev/bpf*` is root-only on macOS and no unattended gate may take sudo, so the gate maps every hostname Chromium resolves to a recording loopback sink — complete for the renderer, Electron main's `net.fetch`, and every Chromium background service — and samples `lsof` at 200 ms over every process running a binary from inside the bundle. Over a 60 s run exercising sign-in, the workspace, settings, and a launch, a packaged community build resolved no hostname beyond the gate's own control probes and held no socket off loopback. Both instruments prove themselves live in-run: an unobserved control FAILS rather than reading as silence).
- [x] OS4.2 Cloud call-site migration, including browser admin capability and honest unconfigured states (all seven WP2b call-site groups are integrated, the neutrality gate proves a community build registers no `exawatt://` handler, the network observation ran (OS4.1), and `community-closure.test.ts` holds WP2's closure assertion — the census of every shipped module that can construct a remote client, each one answering under the community contract with the constructors mocked to throw. Its last item was BUG-060, the automatic Anthropic own-account read, and the code half of that is integrated: the grant is distribution schema V2's `ownAccount.claudePlanUsage`, community declares none, and `eval:community:network` asserts the boundary CLOSED in both directions instead of printing it OPEN. The Privacy surface shows "Not configured in this build" for the Claude plan row rather than a dead switch. What is left is not engineering: the stored official contracts — Vercel Production, Vercel Preview, the GitHub Actions repository secret, and the operator's `~/.exawatt/distribution.official.json` — still carry schema V1, so official and dogfood builds also make no automatic read until their custodian rewrites them. V1 stays accepted precisely so nothing breaks in that window. Tracked on BUG-060.)
- [x] OS4.3 Private hosted-service overlay, public compatibility contracts, and official/community composition proof (28 hosted files live in `company/overlay/web/`; the public tree keeps the typed contracts and 404s; `pnpm company:proof` shows both profiles deterministic with `official-web` restoring all 28 and `official-desktop` refusing them; a real official-web `pnpm build` puts every hosted route back in the deploy, and the community build no longer has them; the runtime census now spans both trees and forbids any `src/` import across the boundary; `proxy.ts` deliberately keeps a static prefix list and the public `/download` page is owed by OS2.2 — see the project doc).
- [ ] OS2.2 Private-name cutover and fresh reviewed public seed, initially kept non-public.
- [ ] OS3.2 Live repository governance: CLA assistant, CODEOWNERS, Discussions, and branch/ruleset protection.
- [ ] OS5.3 Secret and supply-chain gates executed against the exact seed and private history (2026-08-19, EXECUTED but not closable until a seed exists). Green on the exact public tree this commit projects and on all 1189 commits of private history: gitleaks 8.30.1 in the version and configuration public CI installs, `publication:check`'s seven gates over 1459 tracked paths, `content:scan` over all 1322 rendered public files, `pnpm audit --prod` at zero advisories across the 265 shipped packages, and `LICENSES.chromium.html` observed at 19.96 MB inside a packaged `Exawatt.app` with both packagers now asserting it. Six historical findings, none a credential, all classified; the four on PUBLIC-classified paths would have turned the public repository's CI red on its first push, and they are allowlisted **by exact secret value** in `scripts/gitleaks.toml` because projection rewrites the commit SHAs that gitleaks' own ignore mechanism keys on. `pnpm security:secrets` re-runs the whole thing. The 88 whole-graph audit advisories are all dev/build-only and left to Dependabot. **OUTSTANDING**: gitleaks has not run on the projected history ITSELF — `projectPublicHistory` refuses at a mid-history `AGENTS.md` revision its recipe cannot render — so that history is covered only by the projector's subtractive property (public bytes are always a subset of private bytes at the same path), which is an argument rather than a run; and six declared public outputs still have no renderer, so the projected tree has no `README.md`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, or path manifest for OS2.2 to seed from. Re-run every gate on the seed once OS2.2 produces one.
- [ ] OS2.3 Public visibility, immutable private source lock, and public-first daily-development cutover.
- [ ] OS6.1 Three-to-five Mac friends complete clean public setup with no private knowledge.
- [ ] OS6.2 Two real contribution loops and the governance/security edge-case rehearsals pass.

Sequencing: OS2 establishes the irreversible repository/history boundary; OS3
makes the project legally and socially legible; OS4 makes the technical claim
true; OS5 verifies the exact tree and release path; OS6 rehearses the
contributor loop and opens availability incrementally. OS2 and OS4
configuration work may overlap, but publication waits for OS3–OS5.

`0034` (proxied analytics ingest), and `0036` (publication model).

### ENG-031 Website — vision communication and guides

Status: active-build — SHAPED 2026-08-14 by a six-track research pass and three operator taste sessions. The overhaul **is** the ENG-030 launch surface, not a follow-on (operator): the agent fleet implements in this repo, and no agency is engaged. Spine: the altitude ladder is the narrative, so the vision costs zero words. The page is a band system, not fixed sections, so open source, security, privacy, observability, and cost can each be promoted to a first-class band by a data edit. **The proposed homepage is assembled and reviewable at `/v2`** (noindexed): the whole arc, the real chrome, the same composer `/` uses, over ONE board instance that spans the fold and the pinned run. W6b, 2026-08-17, cut it to 392 words over 8.6 screens after two independent reviews of the rendered page, split the fold so nothing is printed over the board, stacked the phone rather than shrinking it, and put the public surface on a real loaded face inside the ENG-032 T5.3 boundary. **The operator graded that result a C plus the same day** and named two distinct failures in it — the copy still does not tell a story, and the page does not say where to look (a partner conversation); the board motion is the half he rates. Read the promotion line below as available, not as earned. Promoting it is one line, `HOMEPAGE_ARRANGEMENT = 'proposed'` in `src/components/site/bands/manifest.ts`. Created 2026-08-02 from the operator brief.

Boundary: minimalism is a constraint on this item, not a phase of it. More copy is not the goal; more _conveyed vision_ is.

Milestones (only landed ones are listed here):

- W2 Hero board idle options — LANDED 2026-08-16: three idle behaviours in `/hud-gallery/hero-board` over one frozen Demo Workspace capture (173 Agents, 10 Projects), each measuring its own idle motion live against the measured budget. Planted 0.37/3.1%, scroll at rest 0.21/0.4%, orbit 1.87/11.7% (over budget, and the layout risk reproduced). Three draw calls, DPR 1.5, `antialias: false` with in-shader coverage, demand frameloop that renders zero frames while parked, reduced-motion and mobile poster paths. Ships with `pnpm eval:hero-board`, the `t12-hero-board` R3F task, and the frozen capture rig W4 depends on. Awaiting the operator's pick; nothing is wired into the homepage.
- W2b Hero board names itself — LANDED 2026-08-17: the operator chose the scroll-driven pull and rejected the board itself ("just a pile of rotating icons"), so the three-way switch is gone and the board explains itself without copy. One behaviour: planted at rest, page scroll drives Fleet to Team to Agent, distance in log space. Every Project circle carries its real fixture name, agent count, and needs-you count; a five-signal legend and `173 agents · 10 projects · 25 need you` sit in the frame; past a third of the pull every Agent becomes a hoverable, focusable, clickable DOM target that reveals its real name, Project, six-word contract, and live status. All annotation is DOM projected from 3D anchors, zero raycasts, still three draw calls. At rest 0.159 mean delta and 0.32% of pixels per second, so `pnpm eval:hero-board` promotes the idle budget from advisory to a hard gate. The study is now the board plus two controls; the orbit option, its constants, and its measurement path are deleted.
- W4 Pinned altitude sequence — LANDED 2026-08-17: the three altitude bands are now ONE persistent board and three explanations that scroll past it, at `/hud-gallery/altitude-scroll`. Each panel drives the board to an altitude AND emphasizes what it is describing: every agent waiting on a human, then one Project, then one Agent with its card open, while the rest recede to a floor rather than disappearing. The subject's NAME is read off the frozen capture by the same selector the camera flies to, so copy and camera cannot drift. The bands own the panels, their order, their headings, their budgets, the altitude, and the emphasis; `medium: 'pinned-board'` collects a consecutive run into one sequence, so adding a fourth panel is a manifest row plus a copy entry. Native scroll only: sticky positioning, one passive rAF-coalesced listener, no wheel or key binding, and every visual is a pure function of scroll POSITION, so reading up is identical to reading down. React state carries one thing, the active panel. Reduced motion and phones unpin the SAME DOM through `motion-reduce:` and `max-md:` rather than swapping a second tree, so the layout shift is zero and the board falls to its poster on its own existing rules. Measured at 1440x810: at rest 0.159 mean delta and 0.32% of pixels per second, and 0.033 and 0.25% in the highlighted resting state the sequence actually holds, both now gated. Three draw calls. The altitude bands stay reserved; nothing is on the homepage.
- W3 Fold and close copy — LANDED 2026-08-17, SUPERSEDED by W3b the same day: four complete arrangements of the fold and the closing band at `/hud-gallery/fold-close`, switchable on production, rendered through the real band components at real type scale under the real sticky header. Both 2026-08-14 directions were developed and the thesis line got a two-way door. One primary CTA, flat DOM, `Download for Mac` with `macOS 12 or later. Apple silicon.` pinned from `electron-builder.yml` (arm64 only) and Electron 43's `LSMinimumSystemVersion`. First render of the `site-closing` rung, measured at 72px. The mechanism survived; the copy did not.
- W2c Board legibility fixes — LANDED 2026-08-17: three operator notes on the production board, plus a zoom cap. (1) Project names now read as WORK, not repositories: the Voltaic demo fixture's `name` carries a plain-English display name (`Battery Dispatch`, `Device Telemetry`, `Customer App`) while `key` and `dir` keep the slug, so grouping, roadmaps, cwd resolution and every cross-reference are untouched. (2) The Project label is a primary typographic element, not chrome: 18px semibold sans on the `section` rung, up from 10px mono, with the counts a rung below it and the label tucked into its own zone's crown so ten circles no longer collide. (3) The fold's product sentence is singular and drops the possessive: "Exawatt is the command interface for your agent fleet." All four variants are re-counted at 19 to 24 words against the 24-word ceiling. (4) Scroll is bound to a maximum zoom depth, stated as a frame share rather than a distance: one Agent mark may never exceed a tenth of the frame height, derived from the live fov, applied to the closest keyframe and to `minDistance`, with the log-space interpolation unchanged. At rest still 0.159 mean delta and 0.32% of pixels per second; under scroll 4.4/29.9%, down from 6.4/33.1% because the camera travels less. Poster regenerated with the new names.
- W3b Fold and close copy, rebuilt on the operator's frame — LANDED 2026-08-17: the operator rejected all four W3 variants because they made the READER the bottleneck ("'Two hundred you cannot' - are you trying to pitch me that I can't watch 200 agents?"). The enemy is the old way of working and the tools that have not kept up, never the reader's capacity, and that is now a standing copy rule. The same switching mechanism now carries four developments of the operator's own draft: today into tomorrow, tools as the bottleneck, one reader and one fleet (amended by W2c from "your team's agent fleets"), direct CTA. A holds the whole climb as a two-line h1 and spends thesis plus enemy at 72px; B gives the future number the entire h1; C keeps his thesis sentence whole in the subhead; D names the old tool as the foil. 20 to 24 words above the fold against the 24-word ceiling, 4 to 10 at the close. Trajectory also fixes honest scale: a future-tense 10,000 is true where a present-tense one is not. `READER_AS_BOTTLENECK` is asserted against every variant, and `bandCopyWords()` stopped undercounting multi-line bands. Agent recommendation: A. Awaiting the operator's pick; the homepage still renders the W1 fold.
- W5 The homepage narrative — LANDED 2026-08-17: the arc rebuilt as an ARGUMENT rather than an architecture tour, end to end (at `/hud-gallery/homepage-narrative`, an address W8 retired in favour of `/v2`). The operator's verdict on W4 was that the mechanism was right and the story was not ("needs us to think through the narrative at a very high-level and build the story as the user scrolls down"); the diagnosis is that the panels described the view instead of making a claim the board proves, and that Fleet/Team/Agent is our architecture, not a story. Four structural changes: `thesis` moves ahead of the board and names the FOIL (the tools, never the reader), so the board arrives as evidence; `altitude-attention` is a new panel sharing the fleet altitude with `altitude-fleet`, so the camera holds still while 148 of 173 marks recede and 25 stay lit, which is the one beat no competitor can screenshot; `altitude-delegation` is a new panel and the only anchor that reverses, opening all the way back out to the fleet while 23 real delegated runs bloom out of 16 parents (ENG-023 D3c data, not a motion graphic); `observability` moves directly behind the run because truthful status is a claim about colours just watched. Copy is written band by band to 1,213 words against a 1,516-word declared ceiling, both inside the measured 1,200 to 1,700 band, closing the gap W1 recorded at 474 with content rather than budget. Reserve bands decided: `observability`, `cost`, `trust`, `open-source` promoted and written; `security` waits for a copy reason (its honest half is a control, so it lives in `trust`); `voice` waits for a real named operator and its job moved to `proof`. The camera ladder is now derived from the band list and `applyProgress` walks N keyframes, which is what lets two panels share an altitude and the last one reverse. Mobile stopped being a fallback (operator: demonstrating on a phone at conferences): the phone pins exactly like the desktop and legibility is answered by a portrait CROP rather than a fit; reduced motion keeps its own poster path. The fold is the live board with the type in a radial well, and the actor's publicity still (`public/images/hero-bg.png`, a real likeness on the production homepage) comes off the narrative fold and is flagged for removal. Nothing is on the homepage; shipping any band is a `status` edit.
- W6/W8 The board is the page — LANDED 2026-08-17 at **`/v2`**, the proposed homepage at an address a person can be handed: noindexed, real nav and footer, and the same `HomeBands` walking the same manifest as `/`, so the reviewed page and the shipped page cannot fork. The operator's verdict on the assembled arc was height and placement: "way too much height, too many sections... move the interactive thing to the second section, right after the fold... I don't see why we shouldn't keep it onscreen to help communicate some of the other points too, like security, spend". The board now enters at section two and holds the WHOLE argument, and each panel drives a **lens** as well as a camera. W5's conclusion that provenance and ownership "are not spatial" was wrong on the facts: a harness, a burn figure and an approval choice are properties of every mark. `boardLens` sits beside `boardHighlight` and is independent of it (a highlight says which marks lead, a lens says what they mean); `hero-board-lens.ts` returns a per-unit palette ordinal, a six-colour palette and a legend, written through the instanced attribute and uniform status colour already used, so three draw calls, no recompile, no per-frame JavaScript, and a lens change crossfades on the existing status clock. `source` (harness colours from `contracts/agent-sources.json`, the same colours ⌘T shows) and `burn` (`burnIntensity`, the Operations Board's own figure, banded at its own 0.62/0.85 break points) are REAL; `permission` is declared on `trust` and resolves as status because the capture carries no approval mode, and prints no legend rather than a false one. Height fell from 16.6 screens over fourteen bands to 11.0 over ten, seven of which are one graphic: `thesis` became the lede of the first panel (foil still named before the evidence, one screen cheaper), `observability` merged into the attention panel beside the colours it is about, `altitude-team` merged into a single dive, `open-source` moved to the footer column. Every merged row keeps its slot and its `reservedUntil`. W6 chrome shipped with it: Architecture out of the nav and into the footer, `Download for Mac` the only button, the open-source column stating the AGPL/Apache-2.0 split once; **Docs is deliberately absent** because no public docs route exists and a nav item that 404s is a broken page, not aspiration. Mobile is a demo surface and was measured as one at 390x844: real pinned sequence, reading column capped at 463px of 844, three cards per panel maximum, secondary nav links stepping aside below `sm`. `pnpm eval:hero-board` and `pnpm eval:r3f` (100/100) both green, zero console errors at three viewports. `/hud-gallery/homepage-narrative` retired outright (operator: "we're now using /v2 to prototype it"). **Promotion is one line**: `HOMEPAGE_ARRANGEMENT = 'proposed'` in `manifest.ts` moves the arc onto `/`, swaps the fold's interior, and switches the chrome, because all three derive from it.
- W6c Quality and marketing pass — LANDED 2026-08-17: **301 written words to 257**, same eight bands and same 8.6 screens, and six rendering defects fixed on the three review viewports. Five copy cuts, each named: `altitude-attention` loses the mechanism coda ("Reported by the agent, never guessed from its output" is how the colour is true, which a stranger wants after they trust us, not before); `altitude-agent` loses 24 words that narrated the frame the reader was looking at; `altitude-delegation` loses a sentence that restated its own heading; `any-lab` loses the caption on a legend printed directly beneath it and PROMOTES its coda, because "Bring the plan you already pay for" is the commercial answer a cold developer wants and it was set at 13px; `trust` keeps its coda, since a disclosure with a control attached is a feature. Three changelog rows and the footer's license sentence are rewritten out of our own vocabulary with dates, facts and sting unchanged. Quality: Project labels stopped being sliced mid-word at the frame edge (the fade measures from the label's OWN cached width now, not from a guessed 140px chip, so `owth Marketing` and `Customer Suppor` are gone and the probe reads zero off-frame elements); the phone's board names at most TWO Projects instead of stacking five chips through each other; the phone's fold centres `cluster` on the Project it is actually pointing at, so it opens on a named Project instead of an unnamed corner; the portrait crop is keyed on frame WIDTH instead of on the card clearing `height > width` by thirteen pixels; the identity card can no longer render off the left edge; and the proof band is bottom weighted, so 810px of continuous dead ground before the close becomes two pauses of 420 and 580. `PROOF_LEDE`/`PROOF_CODA` are deleted rather than left empty. `PAGE_COPY_BUDGET.min` drops 280 to 230 with the reason recorded in the manifest; per-band budgets tightened instead of the page ceiling. `eval:hero-board`, `eval:r3f` (100/100) and `eval:typography-stability` all green; no decoration, no motion, idle budget unchanged. **This does not answer the C plus:** it removes what was in the way of a story and what was competing for attention, and the narrative plus the visual hierarchy remain the design partner's directions work.
- W9 Monotonic zoom, native snap, and a board that answers a mouse — LANDED 2026-08-17: four operator notes on the assembled `/v2`. (1) **The zoom only goes one way.** W8's run reversed three times in six panels (out to the fleet, in to one agent, out again for delegation and both lens panels), and no easing hides a reversal. The same eight bands are reordered so every step holds or closes in: fold and `altitude-attention` HOLD the fold's crop while the board recedes to what needs you, `any-lab` and `trust` step in 16% for the lens panels, `altitude-delegation` steps in to the Project framing where a child mark blooming out of its parent is actually legible, and `altitude-agent` is the dive AND the finale, handing straight to the dated list and the button. No copy changed; the claims were already independent. Fitted distances 25.5, 25.5, 22.4, 22.4, 15.3, 4.2 at 1440x900. It is ENFORCED, not intended: `BAND_ALTITUDE_DEPTH` declares each altitude's depth and `manifest.test.ts` fails any reorder that decreases it, while the framing maths lifted out of the R3F scene into a pure `hero-board-framings.ts` lets a test assert the world-unit distances are non-increasing in LOG SPACE on both a wide frame and a phone, and that the geometry agrees with the manifest rung for rung. (2) **The page settles on its steps, natively.** `scroll-snap-type: y proximity` on the document scroller, scoped by `:has()` on the arrangement, with one zero-height sentinel per panel placed on the CAMERA KEYFRAME rather than the panel box (they differ by up to 114px at 1.2 screens, so snapping the box would settle the camera between two steps). Measured: a gesture under ~390px settles back, 400px or four quick notches moves on, a deliberate stop past ~400px is held, PageDown lands exactly on the next step, Home and End reach both ends. Desktop only and off under reduced motion; the phone's unpinned reading column has no steps to settle on and is gated twice. No scroll library, no `scrollTo`, no wheel handler. (3) **The board stopped shouting and started making sense to strangers.** Project labels drop from 18px semibold near-white in a bordered chip to 13/14px medium in the muted token with the count line receding further, and the Project a panel points at is the only one at full strength; the ten Voltaic Projects are renamed to work any founder or developer recognises (Web Platform, Data Pipeline, Design System, Social Marketing Team and so on) with `key` and `dir` untouched; the per-Project caption changes from "N need you" to **"N active"**, live off the scheduler, where active means not idle through the product's own `statusLightStateForAgentStatus`; and status turns go from 3.5 a second to 9. (4) **Constrained mouse interaction.** The camera leans up to 3 degrees toward the pointer, damped, unwinding to exactly zero when it leaves, angles only so it can touch neither the monotonic zoom nor the step; hover works at every altitude, with a target ring plus the Project's name below a 17px projected mark radius and the full identity card at or above it; mouse only, nothing under reduced motion, no drag-orbit. Two latent defects surfaced and were fixed: the reading column's panel BOXES had been swallowing every pointer event over the board on every desktop viewport since the page existed (only the column carried `pointer-events-none`, and only the column was asserted), and the annotation bridge's live status array was born as zeros where zero means `blocked`, which made the frozen poster claim 100% of every Project active. At rest 0.443 mean delta and 0.74% of pixels per second, up from 0.159/0.32% and all of it the new turn rate, well inside the 2/5% budget; under pointer lean 2.379/17.6%, input-driven and recorded; `eval:r3f` 100/100 at three draw calls; zero console errors at 1440x900, 390x844 and reduced motion. Poster regenerated.
- W10 Six operator notes on the assembled `/v2` — LANDED 2026-08-18: (1) **`What shipped` is deleted**, component and copy both, per burn-bridges; the manifest row drops to `reserved` with what would earn it back, the nav's `Changelog` item leaves the page for the releases list because its `#proof` anchor would point at nothing, and `PAGE_COPY_BUDGET.min` drops 230 to 170. Seven bands, about 7.6 screens, about 192 written words, and the dive now hands straight to the close. (2) **The first scroll is a camera move.** W9's still-camera hold through `altitude-attention` is superseded: a new `cluster-in` rung sits ten percent in from the fold's crop, so fitted distances run 25.5, 22.9, 20.4, 20.4, 15.3, 4.2 and the first step is real. The fold's own framing is untouched. The monotonic guard stands; the "widest framing holds longest" guard is REPLACED by one asserting the first rung changes, with the 8-to-15-percent magnitude asserted in `hero-board-framings.test.ts` on a wide frame and a phone. (3) **Working to done is a moment**: a 0.44s ease-out-back pop to 1.45x and a 0.78s ring-plus-six-particle burst in the result colour, entirely in the unit shader, keyed per instance off `aChangeAt` plus one `aPop` float. It stays in the UNIT mesh rather than becoming a second one — every quad is padded 2.6x and the fragment shader scales its coordinate space back, so the mark is drawn identically and the resting board is still THREE draw calls against a gate of four. Reduced motion gets the plain crossfade. At rest 0.450 mean delta and 0.77% of pixels per second, against 0.443/0.74% before and a 2/255 and 5%/s budget, so the turn rate did not have to come down. (4) **The bottom-right legend is deleted**; the attention panel's copy and the identity card teach the colours. (5) **Hover coverage goes 36/173 to 173/173 at every altitude** via one delegated pointer handler over the board, which made the per-frame write pass SMALLER (36 rings to one) rather than larger; the card opens on every mark, `AGENT_TRACK_MIN_RADIUS_PX` and `AGENT_AFFORDANCE_PROGRESS` are deleted, click pins and click-away or Escape releases. Keyboard stops stay bounded at 24 nearest the frame centre, and the reason is recorded: 173 tab stops in a marketing hero is a worse accessibility outcome than a sample. (6) **The identity card and the `any-lab` legend carry the harness's own brand mark**, reusing the marks Exawatt has drawn in the launcher since 2026-07-03 rather than sourcing new ones; three of the five (OpenAI, OpenCode, Grok) were approximations and are now the vendors' own path data. Provenance moves to `LICENSES/brand/harness-marks.md`. Anthropic, OpenAI and xAI all forbid recolouring, so every mark draws in one neutral ink and never in the source's identity colour, with the app's own tinting recorded there as a pre-existing accepted risk. Gemini CLI and Antigravity are excluded on purpose: neither is a declared Agent Source, and a mark for a harness the product does not launch is the overclaim `marketing.md` records twice. `eval:hero-board` and `eval:r3f` (100/100, three draw calls) green; zero console errors.
- W12 Eight operator notes on the assembled `/v2` — LANDED 2026-08-19: (1) **The product sentence sets on one line.** It needs 445px at 18px and the subhead's own `max-w-[26rem]` cap allowed 416px inside a column offering 472px at `lg`, so the cap comes off and the 18px rung starts at `lg` where the column widens, with `md` at 17px against 432px. Tracking and the sentence are untouched. One line at 1280, 1440 and 1536. (2) **A second control beside `Download for Mac`**, and it is an ordinary in-page link rather than a click handler: `panelStepId()` gives the next panel's existing snap sentinel an id, so the affordance lands exactly where a scroll settle parks and nothing calls `scrollTo`. Outline weight, no fill, named "See the fleet", focusable, smooth with a reduced-motion override. Measured delta from the sentinel: 0px in both modes. (3) **The attention panel stops being aphoristic**: "A hundred agents, one screen" over what every agent is doing, with the needs-you idea in one plain sentence. (4) **`any-lab` NAMES the harnesses** in the product's own labels, four that launch plus OpenClaw arriving, running side by side on the plan you already pay for. (5) **Every stat line and the last coda are deleted**, and the `coda` FIELD with them: all four stat lines were a second printing of the board chip or of the identity card. The lens legend stays, because it maps a colour the board IS using. (6) **A new `cloud` band** carries ENG-033's promise, after `altitude-delegation` (scale across a fleet and scale through time are one claim on two axes) and HOLDING the Project framing, so the monotonic ladder and the dive's finale are both untouched; written as real with no badge, future tense in the sentence, and the board does not pretend to prove a placement the capture does not carry. (7) **The GitHub link keeps its real destination** with its interim 404 recorded as a dated accepted operator decision. (8) **Leaderboard returns to the nav** (Changelog, Leaderboard, GitHub, Download), with `siteMenuLinks()` de-duplicating the phone menu; no header overflow at 1280, 1440 or 1536. Eight bands, 8.2 screens, 202 written words against a 170 floor. `eval:hero-board` at rest 0.451 mean delta / 0.77% of pixels per second and `eval:r3f` 100/100 at three draw calls, both green; zero console errors at four viewports. One latent defect fixed: W11's `hasFold` was read by the scroll pass and missing from its dependency array, which had bailed the React Compiler out of the page's one scroll-frequency component.

### ENG-032 Theming and visual identity

Status: done — T0–T5.4 landed through 2026-08-04. Created 2026-08-02 from the operator brief.

Direction (operator framing): "Right now I kind of hate our ultra-geeky fonts and colors, not so readable. Users will want to customize. Cleaner UI." Reference feeling: light, airy, liquid glass, high-tech — offered as inspiration, explicitly not a constraint.

Sequence decided by the operator: **the theming system comes first**, then the operator's preferred look becomes one theme among several. This prevents a second hardcoded aesthetic replacing the first.

First-release boundary (operator, 2026-08-03): themes may own color and application typography plus a bounded material/transparency treatment; they do not own motion or meaningful layout. Selection is an app-global personal preference and does not change with Workspace or Project. The first release proves the contract with two or three first-party presets; community distribution is a future marketplace seam, not initial scope.

Preset and preference model (operator interview plus delegated design resolution, amended by operator after first rollout, 2026-08-03): prove three roles—the current dark compatibility preset, a new light/airy direction, and its calmer modern dark sibling. Manual mode pins one preset; Auto retains a light/dark pair and follows the OS. Presets recommend validated typography/material defaults; global interface font/scale and optional system accent survive theme changes. Manual Enhanced contrast and Reduce transparency controls are retired; platform accessibility signals remain automatic. Terminal typography remains independent. Appearance is device-local for the first mile.

Architecture (decision `0026`): versioned declarative presets resolve once into complete semantic snapshots, then thin adapters project them into DOM/CSS, xterm, R3F/Three, and Electron boot/native chrome. Future marketplace payloads must normalize through the same data-only trust boundary; arbitrary CSS, JavaScript, remote assets, and fonts are not accepted.

Boundaries: themes do not own motion, meaningful layout, density, geometry, product state, or Demo/Live data. Status, action, Project identity, Consumption, and readiness stay distinct channels. D40 semantics and redundancy survive every preset. Native glass is optional output with an opaque fallback, never a cross-platform promise.

Exit criteria:

- Manual and Auto appearances persist app-globally, switch live without disrupting Agent/PTY/Fleet state, remain stable across Workspace/Project and Demo/Live changes, and match the Electron launch frame before hydration.
- Classic, Air, and Night pass the complete Agent/Team/Fleet/Settings/Consumption/xterm matrix, WCAG contrast, enhanced-contrast/reduced-transparency/forced-colors, 90–120% interface scale, narrow viewports, and R3F performance gates.
- No migrated theme-owned scope retains an unexplained presentation literal; invalid settings and unsupported material fall safely to Classic/opaque; packaged offline Electron and hosted-local adapters share one schema.
- The design-system kernel and architecture manifest describe the landed system; the temporary gallery theme study retires after production ships.

Milestones:

- T0 Contract, generator, and Classic parity oracle — LANDED 2026-08-03: strict data-only schema, three authored built-ins, deterministic CSS/renderer/Electron generation, pure resolver, contrast/security gates, Classic parity assertions, and `/hud-gallery#theme-system` scaffold. Production remains Classic-only.
- T1 device-local preference sources, pure runtime resolver, and flash-free Electron/web bootstrap — LANDED 2026-08-03; Classic remains the only production/persistable preset.
- T2 `/hud-gallery` Air/Night authorship and DOM/R3F/xterm/accessibility acceptance — LANDED 2026-08-03: scoped workbench, fixed first-party type/material profiles, expanded contrast and exact channel-collision gates, ANSI specimen, and bloom-free DOM/R3F siblings; non-Classic remains gallery-only.
- T3 DOM and xterm percolation — LANDED 2026-08-03: T3A app chrome/Settings/overlays/feedback/readiness; T3B Workspace/Roadmap/status and live/retained xterm; T3C Usage/Consumption visualizations, drill surfaces, ambient meter, and material popovers.
- T4 Fleet/R3F, launch/native chrome, and bounded material-adapter spike — LANDED 2026-08-03: production Operations Board and DOM overlays consume the concrete-sRGB spatial adapter without scene-state reconstruction; V1 ships renderer material only after the native spike proved an opaque renderer makes system backdrop APIs invisible.
- T5 production picker/Settings exposure, default Auto Air/Night rollout, verification matrix, literal-completeness gate, study retirement, and canonical closure — LANDED 2026-08-03.
- T5.1 post-rollout stability and contrast hardening — LANDED 2026-08-03: one first-paint owner, fixed public-home typography/root ground, retired manual contrast/transparency overrides with V1 migration, avatar theme submenu, contrast-safe Agent Source identity projection, and explicit readable-role gates for HUD micro-copy.
- T5.2 client-navigation paint continuity — LANDED 2026-08-03: root-caused the live Home → Architecture white flashes, retired the two theme-colored full-screen curtains/navigation delay, stabilized opaque fixed-dark public chrome plus the Architecture pending floor, and added a forced-cold browser-pixel gate; incident `0003` carries the five-whys.
- T5.3 public typography continuity — LANDED 2026-08-03: completed the fixed public-exhibition boundary across Home, Architecture, shared chrome, and pending UI; removed the route-local font owner; and added a browser metric/raster gate that perturbs every production typography profile and 90–120% interface scale. Incident `0004` carries the five-whys.
- T5.4 external appearance consistency — LANDED 2026-08-04: external renderer/tab preference and OS/native streams settle to one final distinct snapshot while local intent remains immediate; the Goal Visuals gate reproduces 60 valid cross-tab writes and covers decoded raster arrival, Air contrast, and narrow viewports. Incident `0005` carries the five-whys.

Completed sequence: T0 → T1; T2 froze the visual contract before the disjoint
T3/T4 adapter packets; T5 exposed production selection only after those adapters
and gates were assembled; T5.1 hardened first paint, accessibility ownership,
selection access, and Agent Source contrast after production rollout; T5.2
extended paint continuity across the actual client-navigation lifecycle; T5.3
made that public boundary complete for typography and scale; T5.4 moved the
remaining app-surface protection to the provider by settling and deduplicating
external snapshots before resolution.

Discovery and execution detail:
