### ENG-010 Connected OpenClaw on customer infrastructure

Status: active-build — shaped 2026-08-16 from the operator's two live OpenClaw
VPS installations. C0–C4 landed; H2 write authority landed under ENG-033. The
read path is proved against both dogfood Gateways and hardened.

Scope: connect existing OpenClaw before provisioning anything. Preserve each
Gateway's native configured-Agent/context graph, project one stable Exawatt
Agent per configured OpenClaw Agent, map each explicitly into a renameable
Project, and keep placement, connection freshness, and work state separate.
The first slice is read-only; closing or quitting Exawatt leaves remote work
untouched, and relaunch reconnects to the same Agent without duplication.

Milestones:

- C0 Projection kernel and public-safe authored two-Gateway fixtures — LANDED
  2026-08-16:
  source-qualified Agent/context records, primary-conversation role, versioned
  projection plan/output, pure mappings; no network/UI writes.
- C1 Saved remote source and read-only transport — LANDED 2026-08-17, proved
  end to end against both operator Gateways: customer-hosted
  placement, OS-owned connection material, Gateway discovery,
  capability/freshness truth, authoritative reconnect snapshots/subscriptions,
  and optional source-declared replay positions. Transport decided 2026-08-17:
  an SSH-forwarded tunnel to the source's own loopback Gateway port, chosen
  because every VPS operator already has SSH and none should have to expose a
  port or type an IP. Server access is SSH-alias-first with manual entry as the
  only keychain-writing fallback; the Gateway's shared secret is resolved
  through the authorized tunnel, held in memory only, and used once to pair a
  read-scoped device token that is what persists. A same-day read-only probe of
  both dogfood Gateways confirmed the shape: `agents.list` is first-class
  discovery, the source's own scope map makes read-only enforceable server-side
  (`operator.read` covers all of H1, `operator.write` is H2), pairing through
  the tunnel is silent, and one dogfood Agent has no `main` conversation at
  all, so the null-primary path ships exercised. C1 also retires the pre-registry
  single-Gateway environment-flag path, whose either/or posture showed one
  Gateway instead of the local fleet, and folds the `hosted-openclaw`
  coming-soon adapter into `openclaw` at a different placement.
  The live run corrected four things no fixture could: the client was pinned to
  protocol 3 while current Gateways require 4, device identity used hex and a
  truncated key where the Gateway expects base64url and a SHA-256 digest,
  `config get` on the token returns a masked value rather than the credential,
  and `ssh -N -L` under `ControlMaster auto` hands the forward to an existing
  master so a detach would have left a port open. Pairing as a UI client (not a
  backend one, which the Gateway treats as a local self-connection and skips)
  yields a device record carrying `operator.read` and nothing else. The Settings
  surface moves to C2, where the Connect flow gives it something to show.
- C2 Connect flow and coworker projection — LANDED 2026-08-18: `/hud-gallery` review, then **⌘N →
  Connect existing Agent…**, explicit Project mapping, and read-only Agent/Team
  views with bounded primary-conversation history and meaningful current work.
- C3 Relaunch and dogfood proof — LANDED 2026-08-19: outage, source restart, rename, detach,
  reattach, and retired-Agent cases against both operator Gateways.
- C4 Hardening — LANDED 2026-08-19: the surface's first visual review, the
  manual transport proved against a real machine, and a type-check for the
  Electron tests, which nothing had ever compiled. It found three defects the
  suite was green about, each because a double could do something the real
  boundary cannot: the connection checklist never received a stage, since its
  callback crossed `ipcRenderer.invoke`, which carries only structured-clonable
  values; the credential bootstrap authenticated over a multiplexed socket it
  had not opened, so a key file that does not exist read a credential
  successfully; and a work state nobody reported rendered as Idle. The last
  adds an `unreported` status-light reading with its own mark and word, and
  makes `ExawattAgent.status` nullable so an absence cannot be coerced back
  into a claim of rest.

Exit criteria: active discovery offers Marcus, Scout, and Tyler exactly once;
Priya remains retired unless explicitly selected; source Sessions/cron/helpers
stay subordinate; each Agent opens its exact source-declared primary
conversation; Remote placement and stale connection are legible without
borrowing D40; quit/relaunch resnapshots without duplication or replay; detach
mutates nothing on either VPS; Demo and Live pass the same
projection/lifecycle contract.

Project doc:

- `docs/engineering/projects/connected-openclaw-and-hosted-agents.md`

Decision:

- `docs/engineering/decisions/0037-project-coworker-agents-over-source-topology.md`

### ENG-033 Hosted Agents

Status: planned — design pass complete 2026-08-16. Created 2026-08-02 from the
operator brief and reshaped around the existing OpenClaw dogfood topology.

Direction (operator framing): "One-click to push this agent up to the cloud, in
an Exawatt-hosted plan. Agents should generally be whatever the user chooses."
Decision `0037` makes this one progression rather than a separate cloud roster:
placement is a fact on the configured source, and local, customer-hosted, and
Exawatt-hosted Agents use the same Agent, Project, state, and command surfaces.

Captured direction (operator, 2026-08-17): ENG-010's first mile is deliberately
the near-term step — reach and administer already-hosted OpenClaw coworkers
beside local Agents — but it sits on the path toward shipping any local Agent up
to a VPS or to Exawatt-managed hosting. H3/H4 design must not assume
OpenClaw is the only runtime that gets pushed.
The generality test for every H1 decision is whether it holds for another
operator on another provider, not only for the two dogfood Gateways. This is
captured direction, not a shaped hosting business; H3 and H4 still require their
own design pass before any of it is scoped.

Milestones:

- H0 Hosted topology contract — SHAPED 2026-08-16: one coworker-shaped Agent
  projection over preserved source topology; placement orthogonal; detach
  non-destructive.
- H1 Observe existing infrastructure — ENG-010 C0–C3, read-only first.
- H2 Command connected Agents — LANDED 2026-08-18: main-context conversation first; steer, abort,
  schedule, and context controls only through exact observed capabilities.
  Generic remote Pause is deferred until OpenClaw can verify a named halted
  scope and resume the same preserved work; prompts, cron edits, Gateway stops,
  and VPS shutdown are not substitutes.
- H3 Exawatt-managed placement — provision OpenClaw behind the same source and
  Agent contract; ENG-012 supplies control-plane ownership.
- H4 Clone or move — enumerate what transfers and what remains source-local
  before offering a gesture. No false provider-session continuity.

The existing _Push to cloud_ announced affordance remains a promise, not the
label for attaching an existing remote Agent. It becomes actionable only at H4,
when Exawatt can state what moves.

**The homepage carries this promise publicly from 2026-08-19** (ENG-031 W12,
operator: "add a section about the ... feature to push an agent up to the cloud
to manage long-horizon agents / long-horizon work"). The `cloud` band at `/v2`
says "Soon you will push a running agent to the cloud and close your laptop.
It works all night and hands you the result in the morning." That is FUTURE
TENSE ON PURPOSE and carries no readiness badge, which is the operator's own
call and the `marketing.md` → "Aspiration is half the message" register. The
constraint it puts on this item is worth stating here rather than only on the
website: **a present-tense rewrite of that sentence is owed to H4**, not to H3,
because the sentence a reader will hold us to is that a RUNNING agent moves and
keeps working, and H4 is where Exawatt can enumerate what actually transfers. The daily connection route starts at **⌘N →
Connect existing Agent…**; `/cloud` evolves into the later placement-management
surface, never a duplicate roster.

Ownership: ENG-010 owns H1, ENG-011 later proves mixed-source fleet scale,
ENG-012 owns hosted metadata/governance/billing, and ENG-033 owns the
user-facing progression. No fourth execution plan.

Project doc and decision: same as ENG-010 above.

### ENG-034 Multiplayer and sharing

Status: planned — deliberately unshaped pending a design pass. Created 2026-08-02 from the operator brief.

Direction (operator framing): "Google Docs-like permissions, sharing, potentially something like avatar cursors (like Figma cursors, metaphorically)." Answering "what does multiplayer look like in Exawatt?" is one of the four recurring user questions ENG-026 must answer on screen.

Captured direction: ENG-027's Workspace tenancy is the prerequisite — sharing needs a scope to share. A shared or read-only Workspace link is the candidate first primitive, and ENG-027 W2's pane content source is what would make a shared Session readable by someone who is not running it.

Boundary already implied by canon: an observer must never be able to drive an Agent they lack authority over, and attribution — who spawned, who is interfacing, who approved — is an open industry design problem the brief names explicitly. Do not paper over it.

### ENG-035 Leaderboard and shareable stats

Status: active-build — design pass complete 2026-08-03; A0 plan and telemetry decision landed before application code.

Direction (operator framing): "Cool hook to get people using and talking about Exawatt... I want to be able to brag about and post how I'm tokenmaxxing, and my record-longest running task (right now 1h36m of continuous Fable execution)... Like how people boast about their GitHub contributions map." A viral-loop distribution play, not a product feature.

Shaped thesis: **Agentmaxxing is competitive self-expression for the emerging operator class.** Exawatt turns otherwise invisible machine labor into a public identity, a high score, and evidence that one person can command extraordinary productive capacity. `Agentmaxxing` is cultural slang; durable product objects are the Operator profile, Leaderboard, and shareable Run projection over a Session turn and delegation tree.

Scope: one opt-in global arena with weekly/all-time windows and transparent Command (autonomous agent-hours), Endurance, Fleet, and Tokens axes; a GitHub-seeded but provider-neutral public identity; an autonomous-agent-hours activity graph; and individual Run receipts. No opaque composite score, social feed, friends/clubs, historical backfill, public task text, or anti-cheat system in V1.

Privacy boundary: decision `0029`. No upload before explicit consent; aggregate profile/day/Run fields only; no prompts, responses, code, repo/Project/task/path/file/transcript identifiers or content. Supabase is the first hosted aggregate dependency. Public evidence says Recorded by Exawatt, never verified value.

**Outward framing amendment (operator, 2026-08-04, from a partner conversation): the public face leads with OUTPUT, not input.** Command hours, Endurance, Fleet, and Tokens are input axes — internally they read as status, but a first-time visitor reads hours-of-compute as "look how much money and energy I burned," into a public that already believes each prompt boils off bottles of water. The axes are also gameable by idle Agents, which the design pass never addressed: an Agent twiddling its thumbs still accrues Command hours. Direction: the headline claim becomes what the operator SHIPPED — launches, projects, published work — with the input axes surviving as the how, not the lede. Cheap proxies exist today (commits, publishes, releases); richer output (users made happy, props) is harder and stays unshaped. A portfolio reading ("here are the 20 projects") is part of the pivot, because unverifiable volume invites the obvious rebuttal. The measurement contract, privacy boundary, and assurance grammar are unchanged; this changes what the arena RANKS and how it speaks, so it needs a deliberate amendment to the A1 public-metrics table rather than a copy edit. FIX-003's copy half shipped 2026-08-04 (self-explanatory axis labels: **Agent hours**, **Longest hands-off**, **Peak fleet size**, **Tokens used**, with matching ids `agent-hours`/`hands-off`/`peak-fleet`/`tokens` through the URL contract and the RPC, old vocabulary removed rather than aliased); its measurement half — counting subagents in fleet size — rides this pass with the output pivot. Externally validated 2026-08-12: "agentmaxxing" is now live in the wild independent of Exawatt, corporate raw-burn leaderboards (Amazon, Meta) were abolished mid-2026 after being gamed, and "autonomous agent-hours" remains publicly unclaimed as a metric.

Exit criteria: the operator is visibly #1 on a real public leaderboard of one after GitHub link and opt-in; profile graph and records come from real post-opt-in local source data; a public Run reveals no forbidden content; disable removes every anonymous read without touching local history; hosted migration/config, deployment, origin/master integration, and dogfood installation are verified.

Milestones:

- A0 Plan, privacy decision, and canon — LANDED 2026-08-03: approved design brief, measurement/upload contract, decision `0029`, and execution slices committed before feature code.
- A1 Pure stats kernel and local corpus proof — IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-03: source-neutral Run/day/rank contracts, conservative Consumption adaptation, strict payload bounds, deterministic fixtures, and installed post-opt-in corpus proof.
- A2 Hosted aggregate spine and GitHub identity — active-build: production migration/RLS/public projections and hardened sync/disable APIs are live; one-time GitHub OAuth App registration and provider configuration remain. **Sharpened 2026-08-06 by the external-user audit:** the link demonstrably worked once — `operator_profiles` carries a genuine GitHub subject and handle from a real sync on 2026-08-04 — but `auth.identities` now holds four rows, all `google`, and no `github` row at all. The public leaderboard is therefore serving a profile that can no longer be refreshed: `githubIdentity()` returns null, so `/api/operator-stats` answers 409 "Link GitHub before publishing", and `sync_operator_stats` would raise `linked GitHub identity required` independently. The auth audit log retains nothing from that window, so what removed it is unrecoverable. Acceptance consequently changes from "publish once" to **the identity persists across sessions and a later resync succeeds**, with a guard that surfaces a missing link instead of leaving a stale public profile silently frozen.

  **RESOLVED 2026-08-07 — and the sharpening above was reading the wrong subsystem.** The provider was configured that day and a GitHub identity now exists. The reason there had never been a durable one is incident `0007`: **desktop linking never worked in any build.** `ElectronAuthCoordinator.startFlow` builds a fresh main-process Supabase client per flow — correct for `signInWithOAuth`, wrong for `linkIdentity`, which is an authenticated call and threw `AuthSessionMissingError` before a browser opened. Every GitHub identity this project has ever held was created from the web renderer. Three defects stacked on it: link verdicts were routed to `/sign-in` (a signed-in operator sent to a sign-in form), the panel read linked-ness from a stale `session.user.identities` snapshot so a completed link stayed invisible, and Supabase's `identity_already_exists` — which covers both "already yours" and "someone else's" — was passed through as prose and painted red, rendering a success as a failure. All four fixed; the renderer's session is now installed on the main client before linking, and outcomes ride a closed channel back to the publish panel. Also fixed the same day: Supabase **Site URL** was `http://localhost:7000`, so GoTrue fell back to it for unallowlisted redirects **and for every email link** — password-reset mail pointed users at a dev server on their own machine, proven by reading `redirect_to` out of a delivered message.

- A3 Local live source, consent, and sync — IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-03; AMENDED 2026-08-10: the off-by-default `operatorProfile.autoPublish` preference is the consent act, with coalesced launch +2min, six-hourly, flip-on, and manual sync; pause and remove remain distinct. REPAIRED 2026-08-16: Operator stats now projects the shared incremental Consumption service instead of cold-rescanning the corpus, consent/sync/profile state persists in Electron settings across random renderer ports, legacy profiles recover their hosted consent boundary, and failure status names the actionable layer. Decision `0029` and the project milestone log carry the full contract and evidence; the upload allowlist and exclusions are unchanged.
- A4 Public arena, profile, and Run receipt — IMPLEMENTED 2026-08-03: accepted/retired gallery study plus responsive leaderboard, profile, activity graph, permanent receipts, metadata, share action, and public-header/`⌘K` discovery.
- A5 Dogfood proof and closure — active-build: real hosted #1-of-1 and signed-out local-production proof are complete; integration, production deploy, clean-master dogfood install, and GitHub provider configuration remain. PULLED INTO THE LAUNCH WINDOW 2026-08-13: the operator's public profile and a Run receipt are launch-thread material (operator delegation 2026-08-12, taken), so A5 closure rides the ENG-030 launch packets (`projects/launch-execution.md` L7).

Project doc: `docs/engineering/projects/leaderboard-and-shareable-stats.md`.

Decision: `docs/engineering/decisions/0029-opt-in-aggregate-telemetry-for-public-agentmaxxing.md`.

### ENG-037 Cross-harness Session transfer

Status: planned — deliberately unshaped pending a design pass. Created 2026-08-03 from operator feedback (row `035ca003-d361-4e13-90a7-9dc604c878c7`).

Direction (operator framing): "I want to build a feature which lets you easily, with one click or one keyboard shortcut, transfer the agent to a given harness type. For example if I'm out of Claude credits but I've done all my planning in a given Claude session or it's midway through some work, I want to be able to freeze and reinflate it into a codex harness."

Relations, recorded not shaped:

- ENG-028 and decision `0037` make this coherent: the Agent is the coworker,
  its Type is portable worker-kind configuration, and the harness is the engine
  — this item is an engine swap mid-flight and a concrete test of portability
- ENG-019's crystallization artifact (a versioned per-Session handoff) is a natural freeze format; a transfer is a crystallize-then-relaunch, not a live migration
- ENG-016 D31's source-neutral conversation catalog already reads both harnesses' histories; ENG-018 owns exact provider identity for what gets resumed vs re-primed
- ENG-008 E1's plan-window capacity truth ("out of Claude credits") is the trigger signal that could surface the verb exactly when it is needed

Open questions for the design pass: what honestly carries across (instructions, plan, context summary, file state) versus what cannot (provider-side conversation state — the deep transfer is not a provider resume, and the UI must not imply otherwise); and whether the frozen artifact is repo-visible (ENG-019) or machine-local.

PARTIALLY ANSWERED AND CHEAP HALF LANDED 2026-08-03: D46 ships **Clone to…** on every started Agent Session's keyboard-complete context menu and in `⌘K`. It creates a new Agent on one exact available configuration using bounded `sessionClonePrompt` handoff text, leaves the original Session intact, passes no provider resume identity, and excludes Shell and unavailable targets. Supporting copy says it starts a new Agent with a handoff; “transfer” remains acceptable vocabulary for the broader user problem but cannot claim live provider-state migration. Deliberately NOT pulled forward: automatic suggestion on plan-window exhaustion (it would mean pattern-matching harness output, the inference class this repo has been burned by in D4/D38/D40), the frozen-artifact format, and everything that carries more than a prompt. This item stays `planned` and unshaped for the freeze-and-reinflate half.

### ENG-038 Provider consumption accounts

Status: active-build — slice 1 (Claude plan-window visibility) shaped and landed 2026-08-11, operator-pulled ahead of the planned sequencing ("it's missing Claude even though I have visibility here", real Max-account values on claude.ai beside an empty Exawatt row). Network identity repaired 2026-08-16: the installed app uses its signed Chromium transport, and routine unsigned development cannot initiate the credentialed read. Created 2026-08-03 from the operator brief captured in `projects/consumption-spine.md` §4 and the same-day vision review. Project doc: `projects/provider-consumption-accounts.md` (endpoint reconnaissance, custody design, refresh policy, milestone log).

Direction (operator framing, abridged — full quote in the spine doc): the operator checks four vendor pages daily (claude.ai usage, Anthropic Console workspace cost, OpenAI billing, ChatGPT/Codex analytics) "to check all my consumption and usage as my agents burn down my plan… it would be good to see all this in one place — much in the same way analogously that cmd+t in our app is sort of an aggregator for starting agents of different harnesses or vendors, sort of making that distinction less meaningful."

Why a separate item and not an ENG-008 milestone: the spine's thesis — read-only local parse, no credential, no network call — is load-bearing (its privacy posture and open-source argument rest on it) and must not be amended quietly. This item is the OTHER source class: credentialed, remote, per-vendor reads that land as `reported`/`verified` rungs of the existing assurance ladder. The contract comes free from the spine: `PlanWindow` keyed by `limitId` with `observedAt`/freshness, source-agnostic rollups, absent-never-zero, and the per-source capability pattern (`SOURCE_CAPABILITIES`). ENG-008 is the consumer; ENG-011 is explicitly NOT the owner (a billing API has no sessions to command — forcing it through the Agent Source contract would be shape-abuse); ENG-012 is the hosted successor.

Design-pass answers (2026-08-11, slice 1 — full evidence in the project doc):

- **Endpoint**: `GET https://api.anthropic.com/api/oauth/usage` with `anthropic-beta: oauth-2025-04-20` — verified as what the installed Claude Code binary's own `/usage` calls (`fetchUtilization`), and verified live against the operator's Max account. Its self-describing `limits[]` array (session / weekly-all / weekly-scoped-per-model with `percent`, `resets_at`, model scope) is the primary parse; legacy `five_hour`/`seven_day` is the fallback; the churning experiment codename buckets are never parsed. Undocumented and unstable by construction, so it lives behind a clearly-marked adapter whose every failure mode (network, 401, expired token, schema drift) degrades to the pre-ENG-038 absence — never an error state, never a stale figure presented as fresh.
- **Credential custody**: reuse, in place, the OAuth token Claude Code itself holds in the macOS Keychain (service `Claude Code-credentials`). Read at request time, never copied/persisted/logged, sent only to `api.anthropic.com` with redirects refused, never sent expired, and NEVER refreshed by Exawatt (rotating it would race Claude Code's own credential lifecycle). Unit-pinned. This is interim custody, not a Connection: ENG-009 still owns the first-class Connection this item eventually consumes.
- **Polling**: no dedicated timer. Refresh rides snapshot pulls and rescans (the meter/`/usage` already re-pull on a 5-minute visible cadence), throttled main-side to one fetch per ~5 minutes with jitter. The vendor page is sub-minute fresh; this is deliberately conservative.
- **Chat-usage tension, decided**: plan windows ARE plan truth — they include claude.ai chat burn, and that is the point (the same ceilings gate agent launches). They enter as per-source `reported` windows (`origin: 'provider-account'`, vendor-reported, nothing locally observed), presented under the source with claude.ai's own window names and one disclosure line ("From your Claude account — plan-wide, including claude.ai."); they are never attributed to Sessions, Projects, or rollup totals, so Consumption's "what agents burn" story is not widened by stealth.
- **Spend class**: the endpoint's usage-credit figures (used/limit/currency) are captured on the snapshot (`ProviderPlanSpend`) so the model carries the dimension, with deliberately NO dollars UI — the spend-class surface is a later slice of this item.
- **Off switch**: `Settings → Privacy → Your own accounts → Claude plan usage` (`claudePlanWindows.enabled`), default ON — the operator pulled the feature, and it rides the decision-`0031` outbound-controls contract with its own disclosure row (this is Exawatt's first direct desktop read of a vendor API). Off constructs no request and serves absence.

Remaining scope, still unshaped pending its own passes:

- other vendors (OpenAI/ChatGPT plan analytics, Anthropic Console workspace cost, OpenAI billing; xAI added 2026-08-12 — Grok Build's local `signals.json` belongs to ENG-008's local class via ENG-003 S4, while SuperGrok plan windows would be this item's credentialed class) — per-vendor reconnaissance of what exists and under which credential class
- the spend-class UI (plan / overage / metered-API as a modeled dimension; local-vs-provider reconciliation wants the assurance vocabulary)
- migration of custody to a first-class ENG-009 Connection when that item lands

### ENG-040 Semantic agent search

Status: planned — deliberately unshaped pending a design pass. Created 2026-08-04 from operator feedback (row `4caa9b8c-6e0c-4369-ac56-e02d6b7747e1`), corroborated the same day by a partner conversation.

Direction (operator framing): "Semantic search across agents in Command-K and Fleet (or anywhere there's a lightweight search bar)."

Corroborating framing (a creative director, 2026-08-04): the operator finds a defect in the product before he knows which Agent produced it — "say the tabs are too big, but you're not sure which one of your five tabs made that tab bigger. Is there a way to say to the whole project, hey, whoever made these tabs wider, raise your hand?" The retrieval key is the WORK, not the tab title, and the target may be an Agent the operator already closed.

Why this is not covered by an existing item: ENG-015 S2's `⌘K` switcher is lexical over titles and Project names, and ENG-021 owns what a Session's label SAYS, not retrieval across Sessions. This item is the retrieval layer over what ENG-021 already infers.

Boundaries and open questions for the design pass:

- what the corpus is — context labels only (cheap, already server-owned, already durable) versus turn history, plans, or Artifacts (expensive, privacy-loaded, and the spine's local-first posture is load-bearing)
- whether closed and recoverable Sessions are searchable, and for how long; the 14-day Recently-closed ledger is the obvious boundary
- where results land: one ranked list inside the existing `⌘K` (which decision D48 just re-ranked and should not be destabilized), a distinct mode, or a Fleet-altitude filter that selects units on the board
- whether semantic ranking is local or hosted, and what happens offline — ENG-021's rule that a good last-known answer beats a blank applies here too
- the routing question Dan's framing implies (address the finding to the owning Agent, or spawn one) belongs to ENG-013, not here; this item ends at retrieval

  Sources

### ENG-012 Hosted Exawatt control plane

Status: planned

Scope:

- team/workspace governance

  diagnostics; ENG-021 owns the semantics

INFLATED 2026-08-02 from the operator brief, as recorded direction rather than shaped scope:

- **IT/admin management**: "As an IT leader within an organization, I should be able to see, provision, manage usage and permissions of the tool (SCIM, etc. in the future). Spend management and observability."
- **executive visibility**: "As an enterprise leader, I want to [see] agents company-wide and who is interfacing with them (or spawning them)." The brief names the hard part itself — "'agent' isn't really defined in the industry yet, so ownership / interfacing / attribution is still a bit of an open design problem." Do not paper over attribution; model it explicitly or state it as unknown.
- the **Organization** surface ships as an ENG-026 `preview` long before any of this is real (ENG-026 N3), and ENG-027's Workspace tenancy is its prerequisite

### ENG-013 Lead agent — the non-worker role

Status: planned — RESHAPED 2026-08-02 (operator). The brief asked for a "task-master agent, or project CEO or something... whose sole job it is to completely grok your desires and intent, and ensure that's codified into the roadmap state, and that all constituent project agents are working within that vision productively." That is not a new item: this item already carried "maintain an operator profile (role, goals, values, priorities)". Rather than layer a second orchestrator alongside the first — the exact feature-layering failure mode the brief warns about — the item is rearchitected to name ONE role.

There is one kind of agent that is not a worker. It has three faculties and two scopes.

Faculties:

- **intent custody (looks up)** — hold the operator's goals, values, and priorities; keep roadmap state true to them; judge whether work in flight actually serves the vision. This is the brief's "project CEO".
- **attention filtering (looks down)** — monitor all Sessions, collapse their state to the minimum the operator needs, and decide when to interrupt versus letting work continue. This is the original middle-manager framing and the engine behind Attention Scheduling at scale: the operator's working-memory ceiling (~5-6 live contexts) is fixed, so fleet growth requires a layer that absorbs context instead of surfacing it.
- **dispatch (looks across)** — propose and enqueue work, and send bounded follow-up prompts to worker agents under Policy.

Scopes: the same role instantiated per Project (a **lead**) and eventually per Workspace (the **fleet** scope). One concept at two altitudes, not two agents.

Scope:

- reason over activity provenance and assurance instead of treating source-reported actions as independently verified effects
- read the shared blackboard rather than inventing private state — a lead needs ENG-029's board to know what the fleet is doing
- never become a second permission or policy system; it operates under the same launch and approval policy as any other agent
- the operator's ability to see and override every lead judgment is required, not optional

Sequencing: depends on ENG-003 (unified sources) and ENG-029 (the board it reads); benefits from ENG-006 (Decision model). Design before build.

### ENG-014 Wattage allocation surface

Status: planned

Far-future direction — explicitly not near-term. Accepted as the end-state resource-allocation model: the operator allocates wattage to goals instead of assigning tasks to agents.

Scope:

- goal-level levers: set what share of token wattage flows to each Initiative (e.g. 70/20/10)
- agent spin-up/spin-down becomes an ancillary effect of allocation changes, not a manual operator action
- priority cutline: Initiatives below the allocation line simply do not receive wattage
- build directly on ENG-008 Consumption normalization and Policy/Budget; do not start before Consumption is real

Sequencing: after ENG-008 and ENG-011.

### ENG-022 Agent development-loop hardening

Status: active-build — H1–H6 landed through 2026-08-03; H12 and H13 landed 2026-08-19. REOPENED 2026-08-03 after the delivery-log audit measured 90 stale-base stops against 182 successful landings, non-FIFO lock acquisition in all 20 stress trials, dogfood builds inside the integration critical section, and an 89% red post-merge CI signal. Decision `0030` (as amended same day) adopts a contention-first delivery queue: dogfood leaves the lock first, a FIFO ticket with head-of-queue self-integration replaces the elected coordinator, the exact-tree gate narrows to a cheap changed-path floor rerun only when the base moved, and the full sequencer is held as a measured contingency triggered by the H11 verdict or by remote writers.

Scope:

- one idempotent command bootstraps a fresh worktree (install, optional linked Vercel Development env refresh with a linked-only last-good fallback, macOS signed-browser identity, node-pty Electron rebuild, Electron main compile); unlinked/community checkouts never inherit another checkout's private env
- the eval harness refuses to run against the wrong checkout, an unhealthy dev server, or a missing native binding, with actionable remedies instead of bare native errors
- a dev server nobody is attached to expires on its own, so the worktree rule that mandates one per agent cannot accumulate abandoned renderers
- one owner answers which checkout a dev server serves, so no path can adopt another worktree's renderer
- bounded launch resilience for the one observed Playwright transient; a second failure surfaces
- fold future agent-loop friction here rather than into product items
- keep `pnpm agent:land` as the only public delivery entrypoint while internal queue, verification-policy, and post-integration modules replace polling races
- separate candidate, integration, and post-integration artifact work; only the head of the FIFO queue mutates `master`, holding the delivery lock for seconds
- preserve immutable snapshots, atomic dogfood replacement, signature checks, and the stale-base recovery path throughout the queue trial

Exit criteria:

- a fresh worktree reaches a passing Electron eval with exactly `pnpm worktree:setup` + `pnpm dev -p <port>` + `EXA_BASE=... pnpm eval:...` — validated 2026-07-21
- pointing an eval at the wrong tree's dev server fails loudly instead of testing the wrong code — validated 2026-07-21
- no dev server outlives the agent that started it by more than an hour, measured against the 2026-08-19 baseline of a 2d7h orphan
- 30 representative queued landings produce zero stale-base re-verification loops, zero exact-floor escapes, and all-green completed queue-drain Linux batches, with p95 queue wait under three minutes at comparable load and lower Actions minutes per integrated commit than the 2026-08-03 audit baseline
- a burst of at least ten Electron-facing candidates advances `master` without waiting on dogfood and installs a verified integrated snapshot no more than ten minutes after queue-drain eligibility

Milestones:

- H1 `pnpm worktree:setup` (landed 2026-07-21): idempotent one-command bootstrap, referenced from AGENTS.md's worktree rule.
- H2 Eval-harness preflights (landed 2026-07-21): node-pty binding assertion before launch and a `/api/dev-identity` guard that refuses a dev server serving a different tree; diagnosed live against a stale orphaned `next-server`.
- H3 Bounded launch resilience and regression pins (landed 2026-07-21): one sweep-orphans-and-retry on the observed Playwright transient, plus `scripts/electron-eval.test.mjs` pinning the preflight, wrong-tree refusal, and tolerated identity-less server.
- H4 Environment truth (landed 2026-08-02; narrowed by ENG-030 D6 2026-08-16): linked worktrees pull Development values directly and fall back visibly to their content-idempotent `0600` snapshot; unlinked/community worktrees visibly skip the optional env stage and never copy a main-checkout snapshot; provider evals load `.env.local` explicitly.
- H5 Generated route-type freshness (landed 2026-08-02): `type-check` runs Next's supported type generator first, so route removals cannot leave stale `.next/types` failures behind after a rebase.
- H6 Stable browser QA identity (landed 2026-08-03): macOS evals share one signed-browser resolver that validates the main and network-helper Team identity, worktree setup runs the doctor, and a live-product smoke replaces recurring Chrome-for-Testing/Little Snitch prompt handling (incident `0002`).
- H7 CI truth, measurement, and the retry trap (active-build, implementation landed 2026-08-03; batching repaired 2026-08-16): immutable per-attempt refs remove rewritten-branch retries; hosted Linux now advances one dedicated CI ref after queue drain, a 60-second quiet window, and a two-hour budget interval instead of running on every `master` push; same-ref cancellation remains the race guard, the v1 metric schema records dispatch/floor/queue/cost evidence, and ten consecutive green completed batches remain to exit.
- H8 Supersedent dogfood (active-build, implementation landed 2026-08-03): dogfood is outside the delivery lock; a detached consumer coalesces to the newest immutable integrated SHA on queue drain with a ten-minute ceiling and refuses a superseded artifact before atomic installation; the ten-candidate production burst remains to exit.
- H9 FIFO ticket queue (active-build, implementation landed 2026-08-03): monotonic common-Git-dir tickets, ownership epochs, dead-PID-only recovery, head self-integration, seconds-only final locking, an operator-only direct recovery path, and non-blocking shared-`master` sync are implemented; production killed-head and sustained-load evidence remain to exit.
- H10 Exact-tree floor and changed-path policy (active-build, implementation landed 2026-08-03): the repository now owns the always-on type/delivery floor and conditional related-test, Electron, browser, R3F, CI, and documentation checks; a moved base rebases in the author worktree and reruns that floor on the exact attempt SHA; the 30-landing observation window remains to prove zero escapes.
- H11 Measured verdict (planned): 30 representative landings against the audit baseline — zero floor escapes and all-green completed drain batches, since batched CI cannot see every intermediate commit — decide whether the decision `0030` elected-coordinator sequencer contingency activates or stays shelved; remote writers force the evaluation early.
- H12 Dev servers that expire (landed 2026-08-19): AGENTS.md tells every agent touching a gated surface to start its own `pnpm dev -p <free-port>`, and nothing had ever stopped one — an audit found a server in a landed `exawatt-glide` worktree that had served zero requests for 2d7h, plus an Electron lifecycle eval's server whose temp userData directory was already deleted, together holding two ports and pushing load average past 11. Parent-death detection cannot fix it: these are launched detached, so the root `pnpm` is already reparented to PID 1 at startup. `scripts/lib/dev-idle-watch.mjs` polls ESTABLISHED connections to the dev port instead — a renderer with the page open holds an HMR socket, so "no client for 45 minutes" means abandoned rather than merely quiet, and the whole mechanism stays in the supervisor without touching `src/proxy.ts` on the auth path. A probe that cannot measure (absent lsof) reports UNKNOWN and keeps the server alive rather than reading as abandoned. `EXAWATT_DEV_IDLE_MINUTES=0` opts out. H2's `/api/dev-identity` guard was the earlier half of this same problem: it refused to TEST against a stale orphan but never stopped one existing, and `electron-dev.mjs` still adopts any server already on 7000/7090/3000.
- H13 One owner for dev-server identity (landed 2026-08-19): "which checkout does this dev server serve?" had two owners with different rules. The eval harness asked properly (H2) and refused a mismatch; `electron-dev.mjs` did not ask at all, adopting whatever answered a bare `GET /` on 7000/7090/3000 — so with parallel worktrees `pnpm electron:dev` could silently dogfood ANOTHER checkout's renderer, the exact defect H2 exists to stop, on the path H2 never covered. `scripts/lib/dev-server-identity.mjs` now owns the read and returns a discriminated outcome (unreachable / unverifiable / unhealthy / identified); `assertDevServerServesTree` becomes the assertion layer over it with its refusal messages and all 16 harness pins unchanged, and the launcher becomes a query caller that steps over a foreign server and starts its own beside it. Only a POSITIVE identification counts as ours: an unverifiable server is no longer adopted. Live testing against a real foreign server on 7000 caught a second defect in the fix itself — a `127.0.0.1` free-port probe reports a port FREE while `next dev` holds `*:<port>` on macOS dual-stack, so the launcher would have collided with the very server it stepped over; a port now counts as free only when both the wildcard and loopback bind, since either probe alone lies in one direction.
- H14 Worktrees that expire (visibility half landed 2026-08-19; sweep half planned): H12 stopped abandoned dev servers; nothing stops abandoned worktrees, because creation is a command (`pnpm worktree:setup`) while teardown is prose (AGENTS.md and `agent-delivery.md` step 5, addressed to an agent precisely when its task is done and the session ends). The audit found seven stale sibling worktrees holding ~9.5G logical (physically less — pnpm on APFS clones `node_modules` copy-on-write from the store), oldest sixteen days: three landed-then-abandoned, and four whose sessions died before `agent:land` — whose work later sessions could not see and so RE-IMPLEMENTED from scratch (WP3/WP5a/WP6a, ~3,500 lines, plus a 15k-line bulk capture of another dead session's uncommitted edits). The duplicated work, not the disk, is the cost. Mechanism: `pnpm worktree:sweep`, run first by every `worktree:setup`, so it fires in proportion to fleet activity with no scheduler and no cron. It removes a sibling worktree (and its branch) only when fully merged into `origin/master` AND clean AND quiet ≥72h AND unlocked; a failed read of any of those counts as unsafe, never as clean. Unmerged or dirty trees are never removed — they are printed as an in-flight roster (branch, age, last subject) at every setup, which is the guard against the re-implementation mode: the next agent sees the orphaned branch before redoing its work. **The roster half landed 2026-08-19**: `scripts/lib/worktree-roster.mjs` collects one row per agent branch (worktree, clean/dirty, ahead/behind, age, subject; a failed read renders UNKNOWN, never clean), `pnpm worktree:roster` prints it on demand, and `worktree:setup` prints it before doing anything else — advisory by contract, it can never fail a setup. The sweep (removal) half stays planned as shaped here. Deliberately NOT in `agent:land`: the command runs inside the tree it would remove, and deleting a live session’s cwd breaks every command that session runs afterward; sweep-on-next-setup covers the same tree within hours at current cadence.
- H15 Machine slots for heavy work (landed 2026-08-19): the 25% vitest worker cap is per PROCESS, sized when four concurrent worktrees was the composability target — at a dozen agents twelve polite processes oversubscribe the machine threefold, and the delivery metrics show what that costs: the same `vitest-related` check has p50 8.8s and max 46 MINUTES, `type-check` p50 12.4s and max 20 minutes, across 19.1 hours of recorded floor compute. The scarce unit is the machine, so the bound moves there: `scripts/lib/machine-slots.mjs` is a jobserver-style pool (default `max(1, min(3, cores/4))` slots, `EXAWATT_MACHINE_SLOTS` overrides, `0` disables) with the delivery lock's own semantics — mkdir-as-lock with an owner file, dead-PID-only reclamation, and reentrancy through an inherited token so a slotted parent's child commands never queue behind their own parent. `runDeliveryChecks` takes one slot around the whole floor run, and `test:run`, `test:changed`, and `build` take one through `scripts/with-machine-slot.mjs`; the Electron eval harness keeps its own pre-existing single machine lock and is not double-gated. The pool is QoS, never a gate: an unresolvable pool or an expired deadline (20 minutes) proceeds UNSLOTTED with a loud warning naming the holders, because a leaked slot must never be able to stop the fleet — and a leaked slot is also reclaimed the moment its holder's PID is dead. An unreadable owner file inside the write grace counts as HELD: a failed read must never look like a free slot.

Sequencing: independent; extend as new agent-loop friction is diagnosed.

Project doc:

- `docs/engineering/projects/agent-development-loop.md`
- Operational reference: `docs/engineering/agent-delivery.md`

### ENG-039 Module-owned code and verification topology

Status: planned — accepted 2026-08-04 from the unit-test throughput pass. The immediate `test:changed` / `test:related` dependency selection is a fast feedback layer, not the desired end-state architecture.

Scope:

- discover module seams from runtime responsibility, dependency direction, change/test co-occurrence, and existing package boundaries; do not impose a big-bang folder taxonomy
- give each accepted module one public entrypoint, declared inward dependencies, an explicit runtime environment, and ownership of its unit and contract suites
- distinguish fast module units, boundary contracts, and intentionally cross-module integration suites so a change has a conservative verification closure rather than a filename heuristic
- evolve ENG-022's changed-path policy to select the changed modules, downstream contract consumers, and shared-infrastructure gates; shared test/configuration changes still force the full suite
- preserve one full-suite backstop for integration and CI while shrinking normal feedback through architecture rather than weaker assertions or longer timeouts

Exit criteria:

- every production source file in the adopted scope belongs to one declared module or an explicitly shared kernel, with cycles and undeclared deep imports rejected mechanically
- every module declares its Node, DOM, Electron, or mixed execution boundary and owns unit plus public-contract coverage at that boundary
- selective verification derives a conservative module dependency closure and records no escaped full-suite failure across a 30-landing observation window
- for representative single-module edits, the selective suite's median wall time is at least 60% below the full unit suite without reducing the full landing and CI backstop

Milestones:

- M0 Seam inventory and baseline (planned): dependency graph, cycle map, test co-change evidence, runtime environments, and per-module/full-suite timing baseline.
- M1 Module contracts (planned): public entrypoints, allowed dependency direction, environment declarations, and mechanical deep-import/cycle enforcement for the first high-change slice.
- M2 Layered suite ownership (planned): colocated module units, boundary contracts, named integration suites, and shared-fixture ownership replace cross-tree test accretion in that slice.
- M3 Module-aware verification closure (planned): ENG-022 policy selects changed modules plus downstream contracts, with full-suite escalation for shared infrastructure and ambiguous ownership.
- M4 Measured rollout (planned): extend slice by slice only while the 30-landing no-escape and feedback-time gates stay green.

Sequencing: future developer-platform work after ENG-022 H11 establishes the delivery-loop baseline; it may begin earlier only as bounded refactors demanded by active product work.
### ENG-041 Public defect communication

Status: planned — deliberately unshaped pending a design pass. Created
2026-08-18 alongside the operator's decision to keep the `BUG-*`/`FIX-*`
backlog and the incident post-mortems private.

That decision is right for the records themselves: they are post-mortem detail
on already-fixed defects, they map the product's weak points, and several
describe security boundaries. But it leaves the public project with no way to
show what it knows about its own defects, which a serious open-source project
owes its readers.

Direction (operator, 2026-08-18): "figure out some community-ish industry
standard way of collecting, representing, and communicating bugs", and
likewise "establish some sort of public thing" for incidents.

Scope is deliberately not fixed here. Shape it in a design pass. The obvious
candidates to weigh, not a chosen answer: GitHub Issues as the collection
surface with labels and templates; a published security policy and advisory
path for the subset that are vulnerabilities; a public changelog or release
notes carrying user-visible fixes; and a periodic public write-up for the rare
incident worth teaching from, authored for outsiders rather than projected
from the private record.

Exit criteria: a public reader can see what defects exist, report one, and
learn what was fixed, without the private backlog being published, and the
public roadmap's Backlog note points at that surface instead of only saying
the records are private.

## Backlog

Defect and small-fix records are kept in the private engineering repository
rather than published here. They are post-mortem detail about defects that
are already fixed, and publishing them would map the product's weak points
without helping anyone contribute.

Two consequences worth stating plainly, because a reader will notice both:

- **Milestone entries above cite internal ids** such as `BUG-041` or
  `FIX-002`. Those reference the private records, and they are kept because
  removing them would damage the history they explain. Nothing above depends
  on reading them.
- **Defects in the public project are tracked in the open.** ENG-041
  shapes that surface; until it lands, GitHub Issues is the place to
  report one. This section is history, not process.

## Amendment chain

Later milestones amend earlier ones. These supersessions are load-bearing: an agent reading only the roadmap must not act on a superseded decision. Full narratives for both sides of each pair live in the linked project doc's Roadmap milestone log.

| Amended                                                                                                                                                                                  | Amended by                                                                           | What changed                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| ENG-031 W6b's "one stat line per panel" rule, W6c's decision that `trust` keeps its coda, and the `coda` field itself | ENG-031 W12, 2026-08-19 (operator) | "The last two lines of copy are horrendous here - you're just putting random numbers and labels '172 agents' and 'Every outbound feature' - kill that and other such overpedantic hyperpolite copy lines." Rendered, ALL FOUR stat lines were a second printing of something already in the same frame: `trust` printed the board chip's three numbers verbatim on the same screen, `altitude-attention` printed that chip's needs-you figure again, `altitude-delegation` printed a count the bloom is the picture of, and `altitude-agent` printed the identity card's own three lines. So no panel prints one now, and `showSubject` is deleted rather than narrowed. The coda goes with them and so does the `coda` FIELD, per burn-bridges: W6c's "a disclosure with a control is a feature" is not violated, because with the disclosure gone there is nothing for the switch to attach to. The lens legend STAYS and the distinction is the record: a legend maps a colour the board IS using to a name, and nothing else on screen carries it. |
| ENG-031 W6c's `altitude-attention` and `any-lab` copy, including "Every colour here is a claim, and a wrong one costs you a trip" and "None of them will sell you the seat you command them from" | ENG-031 W12, 2026-08-19 (operator) | "this copy really sucks - make it more about see 100s of agents in one screen" and "way too abstract". Both panels were making aphoristic claims where a cold reader wanted a concrete one. `altitude-attention` becomes "A hundred agents, one screen" over "See what every one of them is doing without opening a single one" plus the needs-you idea in one plain sentence. `any-lab` NAMES the harnesses in the product's own labels from `contracts/agent-sources.json`: Claude Code, Codex, OpenCode and Grok Build launch, OpenClaw lands next, and they run side by side on one board on the plan you already pay for. Known gap recorded rather than papered over: the `source` legend names two, because the frozen capture's Demo Workspace assigns only two harnesses; widening it is a Demo Mode fixture change, which W10 also declined. |
| ENG-031 W6b's `max-w-[26rem]` cap on the fold subhead, and its `sm:text-lg` rung | ENG-031 W12, 2026-08-19 (operator) | "Don't wrap 'Exawatt is the command interface...'." The sentence needs 445px at 18px and the cap allowed 416px inside a column offering 472px at `lg`, so the cap comes off and the subhead takes the column's measure; the 18px rung then starts at `lg`, where the column widens, and `md` reads at 17px, which needs 421px against 432px. Tracking and the sentence are both untouched, which were the operator's third and fourth preferences. One line at 1280, 1440 and 1536; still two on a phone, which is allowed. |
| ENG-031 W4's "no `scrollTo`" rule read as "the page offers no way down" | ENG-031 W12, 2026-08-19 (operator) | "Put a second CTA next to download like an arrow button to indicate scrollability which scrolls down to the next frame." The no-scroll-jacking rule is unchanged and is why the control is an ordinary `<a href>` to the next panel's snap sentinel, which W9 already places on the camera keyframe: `panelStepId()` gives that sentinel an id, so the affordance lands where a settle parks by construction rather than by tuning, and nothing calls `scrollTo`. Smoothness is `scroll-behavior` with a `reduce` override, stated as base-plus-override because Lightning CSS drops the `no-preference`-nested form outright in this pipeline. Measured delta from the sentinel: 0px, smooth and reduced alike. |
| ENG-031's seven-band page, and `manifest.test.ts`'s `<= 7` bands / `< 8` screens ceiling | ENG-031 W12, 2026-08-19 (operator) | "add a section about the (forthcoming ... it'll likely be live tomorrow) feature to push an agent up to the cloud to manage long-horizon agents / long-horizon work." A new `cloud` band carries ENG-033's one user-facing promise. It follows `altitude-delegation` because scale ACROSS a fleet and scale THROUGH TIME are the same claim on two axes, and it HOLDS the Project framing at `team`/`status`/`whole-fleet`, so the monotonic ladder is untouched and the dive keeps the run's one remaining arrival. Written as real with NO badge (operator): the future tense is in the sentence, per `marketing.md` -> "Aspiration is half the message", and the board does not pretend to prove it, because placement is not a property the frozen capture carries. Eight bands over 8.2 screens, 202 written words; `PAGE_COPY_BUDGET.min` stays at 170. |

That'll launch imminently and I get zero traffic today." The nav is Changelog, Leaderboard, GitHub, `Download for Mac`: `/leaderboard` is a live public surface already in `proxy.ts`'s signed-out allowlist and was in the shipped homepage's nav, so the promotion would otherwise have taken it dark. Architecture stays out of the nav and in the footer, per W6. `siteMenuLinks()` de-duplicates the phone menu, which merges the nav with the footer's Product column. The GitHub 404 until ENG-030 flips the repository public is now a DATED ACCEPTED OPERATOR DECISION rather than an open risk: no badge, no tooltip, no disabled state, no fallback, and it is not raised again. |

The drop was 41px and took the pane's height with it, so it resized every terminal rather than only reading as a jolt. And removal was never the only repair: the row was RESERVED instead. It states the directory the workspace is standing in, which the empty-Project stage knows before any tab exists, so it holds its height across the hand-off with nothing removed and the gate untouched. The rule is now: every apply is expected until it lands or expires, a landed apply retires the ones it superseded, and a location that is a stage of ANY apply in flight is still a stage. D50's diagnosis of the hybrid state was right; what it did not cover was concurrency. Same file, same family, one round trip later.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |

| ENG-030 OS0/OS1 "after the demo arc" sequencing                                                                                                                                          | external-user audit, operator 2026-08-06                                             | OS0 was scoped as a gate on OSS publication and OS1's analytics as publication-time work. The audit found the multi-user path had never run once — an invited user on `0.1.8` with no account, so hosted enrichment and the feedback box were silently dead for them, and no instrumentation existed that would have shown it. OS0 is rescoped from "real-user auth" to **external-user identity and onboarding** and becomes active-build; its exit proof tightens from "a newly created non-operator account" to "an invited non-operator completing the path **unaided on a released build**". OS1.1–OS1.4 (proxied ingest, minimal event slice, global ceilings, disclosure alignment) are pulled forward with it. ENG-030's publication half — history curation, licensing, contribution mechanics — remains a release gate and is unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ENG-030 OS1 direct-to-PostHog ingest                                                                                                                                                     | decision `0034`, operator 2026-08-06                                                 | Decision `0031` assumed the client initializes PostHog against a configurable PostHog host. The desktop client now reaches PostHog **only** through an Exawatt-owned reverse proxy, so its sole analytics destination is `exawatt.ai` and the outbound connection is indistinguishable from a heartbeat. `0031`'s substance survives intact — public event allowlist, no autocapture or session replay, real off switch, configurable/self-hostable sink, published outbound-data manifest — only the default sink changes. Motivated by desktop firewall-identity constraints (ENG-016 D17, incident `0002`) that the web-only projects do not have.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ENG-025 F3 "deliberately unshaped"                                                                                                                                                       | operator, 2026-08-06                                                                 | F3's precondition ("once non-operator rows exist") is being met by ENG-030 OS0, so the public-user lane is shaped: two lanes over one intake, membership derived server-side, operator lane unchanged, suggestions lane never auto-promoted to canon. Scheduled triage automation stays out of scope under the 2026-07-27 no-cron decision. The operator explicitly declines a reply/acknowledgement loop; a clear success on Enter is the whole contract owed to a submitter.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ENG-035 A2 "publish once" acceptance                                                                                                                                                     | external-user audit, 2026-08-06                                                      | A2/A5 tracked GitHub identity as one-time provider configuration. The link demonstrably worked (real subject, sync 2026-08-04)       and the identity is now absent from `auth.identities`, leaving a public profile that can no longer be refreshed and a publish path that 409s. Acceptance becomes identity persistence plus a **later** successful resync, with a guard that surfaces a missing link rather than freezing the public profile silently.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ENG-035 A3 preview-then-publish consent flow                                                                                                                                             | operator, 2026-08-10                                                                 | The A0/A3 consent vehicle — mandatory Preview local stats, then publish — is superseded by an off-by-default `operatorProfile.autoPublish` preference whose enabling is the explicit consent act; syncs are automatic and coalesced while on, pause and remove-profile are distinct verbs, and the preview survives only as optional passive numbers. Decision `0029`'s allowlist, exclusions, and prior-to-first-upload consent boundary are unchanged; only the vehicle changed.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ENG-035 A3 duplicate cold scanner and renderer-origin publication state                                                                                                                  | operator freshness report, 2026-08-16                                                | The first automatic-sync implementation bypassed the incremental Consumption spine and stored consent/sync/profile metadata in port-scoped `localStorage`; a real corpus overflowed while merging irrelevant plan-window history, and packaged relaunches could move the local boundary. Operator stats now projects the shared settled sample view, stores lifecycle state in validated Electron settings, and recovers legacy consent time from an authenticated owner-only hosted read. Decision `0029`'s upload allowlist and consent semantics are unchanged.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ENG-008 E5's "compaction rewrites the log from live state, so the log's bloat is bounded"                                                                                                | BUG-032, 2026-08-16                                                                  | Compaction bounds the LOG against the STATE, so it was only ever a bound while the state was bounded — and samples had no retention horizon, unlike the plan-window observations beside them. Samples now carry the same 14-day horizon, anchored at the newest sample and enforced where they are written; the horizon is a policy input that main widens to cover an active Operator-profile publication anchor and clamps at 400 days. The compaction floor is now what a compaction would write today, not the historical `compactedBytes` in meta, which could only rise.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ENG-015 S4.1's persisted `goalVisual`, carrying its `dataUrl` inline in the layout                                                                                                       | BUG-031, 2026-08-16                                                                  | The layout persists a REFERENCE (`identityKey`, `revision`, `state`); the pixels live in a content-addressed side store under `userData/goal-visuals/` and are resolved by main through `pty:restore-goal-visual`. `identityKey` was always the content address — it was simply never used as one. Layouts written before this still carry `dataUrl` and are migrated on load, once, in main. A reference whose pixels are gone degrades to `fallback`; it never claims `ready` with no image.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       |
| ENG-016 D49's model-catalog cache policy, "stale-while-revalidate", which governed only what is SERVED                                                                                   | BUG-033, 2026-08-16                                                                  | Staleness is a storage question too. A row past `CATALOG_MAX_AGE_MS` is deleted rather than refused, a row whose `cwd` no longer exists is deleted (the retired agent worktree), and the file is trimmed to the newest `CATALOG_MAX_ENTRIES`. Eviction runs where the file is written and once on load. Entries carry an explicit `cwd` (schema v2, migrated from v1); `clear()` is deleted.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
